 PLANS FOR INSTALLATION OPERATIONS

  1.0   PURPOSE
       The purpose of this policy is to identify critical operations in order for each mobile offshore
          drilling unit (MODU) to provide a systematic and consistent means of performing these
         operations.
  2.0   SCOPE
        This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.


  3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible to ensure that this policy is implemented.


  4.0  PROCEDURE

         4.1    Each MODU shall create and maintain job plans for critical operations.

         4.2  A job plan may be defined as a rig-specific procedure/work instruction or Job
                Safety Analysis (JSA).

         4.3    At a minimum, MODUs shall develop task-specific job plans for the following
                   critical operations:

                 4.3.1  Cementing operations

                 4.3.2  Changing liner, piston, seat, valve and screens on mud pump

                 4.3.3  Changing shaker screens

                 4.3.4  Changing crane wires

                 4.3.5  Changing out loading station hoses

                 4.3.6  Cleaning mud pits

                 4.3.7  Cleaning shaker tanks

                 4.3.8  Cutting / slipping / changing out drill line

                 4.3.9  Driving surface pipe

                4.3.10 Handling drive pipe hammer with crane

                 4.3.11 Handling and rig up of coil tubing

                4.3.12 Handling tubulars from and to the drill floor

                4.3.13 Handling of hazardous material

                4.3.25 Offloading supplies from vessels or barges, including casing

                4.3.26 POOH & lay down production string

                4.3.28 Raising the BOP work deck

        Job plans for critical operations, on board, current

 FIELD OPERATIONAL REPORTING

  1.0 PURPOSE

    The purpose of this policy  is to establish complete, precise, and on-time reporting of
     operational incidents.

  2.0 SCOPE

     This procedure shall apply to  all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
     operations.

  3.0 RESPONSIBILITY

    The Rig Manager is responsible to ensure that this policy is implemented.

  4.0 PROCEDURE (General)

         4.1     Internal operational reports shall be in English and submitted in a timely manner as
                described below.

         4.2  A daily report is required by ADES mobile offshore drilling units (MODUs).

  5.0 PROCEDURE (Operational Abnormality Reporting)

         5.1    Each Rig Manager shall ensure where any of the following incidents have occurred,
               a report is submitted from the Rig on dashboard/Oracle/maintenance system as
                 applicable:

                 5.1.1  An equipment failure that has lasted more than six (6) hours

                 5.1.2  An equipment failure that has the potential to last more than six (6) hours

         5.2   The following incidents shall be reported into HSE Dashboard.

                 5.2.1   Near-hits

                 5.2.2  Property damage


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 9 از 259 ]

PO

                 5.2.3  An environmental incident

                 5.2.4   Injuries and illnesses

         5.3    Reporting requirements to Liberia, United States, and Panama flag States are
              found in HSE 100.00 and HSE 100.02.

  6.0 RECORDS

         6.1    Equipment failure reports as defined above, Oracle or Maintenance system as
                applicable

         6.2    Property damage near-hit, environmental incident, injury, and illness reports, HSE
               Dashboard, indefinitely.

  7.0 REFERENCES

         7.1  ADM 100, Corrective and Preventive Action

         7.2   HSE 100.00, Incident Reporting

         7.3   HSE 100.02, Injury / Illness Reporting

         7.4   HSE 200.02, Spill Contingency, Drills, and Reporting

         7.5    Shipboard Oil Pollution Emergency Plan (SOPEP), as applicable

         7.6    Oil Pollution Emergency Plan (OPEP)

  DRILLER’S STANDING INSTRUCTIONS

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to provide consistent and standard instructions to the Driller.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible to ensure that this
                 policy is implemented.

          3.2   The Driller shall carry out the instructions and guidance contained in this policy.

   4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

          4.1   The following items represent the basic day-to-day responsibilities of the Driller
                with regards to monitoring the operation to reduce the chances of unplanned events
                while ensuring appropriate information is available to handle the events safely and
                    efficiently.
          4.2    This list is not all encompassing and should be viewed as a minimum requirement
                  to be supplemented with other checks or controls as required according to the
                 operation on the installation at the time.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (The Driller shall perform the following duties at a minimum)

          5.1    Verify all well control equipment and proper alignment of choke manifold and
                  adjustable

               choke is lined up according to the installation operating procedures at the beginning
                 of each tour.

          5.2    Immediately close the well in when a kick is detected or suspected.

          5.3    Flow-check all drilling breaks after surface pipe is set unless you are in a shallow
                gas area.

          5.4    Perform flow checks while tripping as follows:


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 11 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                          Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                        Policy No. OPS 110
                      Policy: DRILLER’S STANDING INSTRUCTIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed
                                                          Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                   Page2 of 4

                 5.4.1   Just off bottom
                 5.4.2  At last casing shoe
                 5.4.3  Prior to pulling heavyweight drill pipe or drill collars through the blowout
                       preventer (BOP) stack
         5.5   Keep the well bore full at all times.

         5.6   The trip tank shall be used while tripping.

         5.7    Accurate trip fill-ups shall be recorded on a Trip Sheet.

         5.8   Know at all times the distance from the rotary to the top of all rams and annular in
                the BOP stack.

         5.9   Know true vertical depth (TVD) and measured depth (MD).

        5.10  Keep a complete record of all tools and tubulars run in the hole showing made-up
                 lengths,  tool  joints, outside diameters (ODs),  inside diameters  (IDs),  serial
               numbers, and fishing necks.

        5.11  Keep the hole covered at any time there is no drill string, casing, or wireline in it.
                In case of known or missing object that might have dropped in hole, must be
                reported immediately to the OIM.

        5.12  Keep rotary bushing locked while drilling / tripping.
        5.13   Obtain verbal confirmation from the Derrickman that he is in the safety harness and
                the harness is properly secured before tripping operations begin.
        5.14   Record slow circulating rates:

                5.14.1 At the beginning of each tour

                5.14.2 Anytime the mud weight changes 0.3 pounds per gallon (ppg)

                5.14.3 Anytime the bit or bottom hole assembly (BHA) has been changed

                5.14.4 As soon as possible after bottoms up following any trip

                5.14.5 At least every 500 feet (152.5 meters) drilled

                5.14.6 Following major mud pump or surface equipment changes or repairs


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 12 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                          Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                        Policy No. OPS 110
                      Policy: DRILLER’S STANDING INSTRUCTIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed
                                                          Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                   Page3 of 4

        5.15   Trip and reset Crown-O-Matic (if required) as follows: Set and test, high and low
             work limits

                5.15.1 At the beginning of each tour

                5.15.2 After slipping or cutting drill line

                5.15.3 After handling long BHA components

                5.15.4 After any other events when the settings have been changed

                5.15.5 Testing of the Crown-O-Matic must be recorded on the International
                       Association for Drilling Contractors (IADC) logbook

        5.16  Have drilling mud properties checked on a regular basis and verified every 30
               minutes or immediately upon any noted change.

        5.17   Constantly  monitor  speed, pump  strokes,  pressure,  pit  volume,  trip  tank,
                 penetration, mud weight, and rotary torque to detect anything unusual or out of the
                 ordinary.

                5.17.1 Trend sheets should be kept by the Driller and a record should be kept on
                        the electronic trend monitoring system and calibrated regularly to ensure
                          that recorded data is accurate compared to physical conditions.
        5.18   Avoid operating at or near critical rotary speeds as indicated by string vibrations.
        5.19   Secure the Driller’s mechanical brake in such a way to prevent the drawworks drum
              from turning whenever the Driller’s position is left unattended, e.g., chain for
              manual brakes.

        5.20   Conduct an inspection of the derrick or mast for potential falling objects after
                  jarring, sudden shock loads, dropped blocks, extreme heavy weather, or any other
                 similar conditions where the derrick or mast may have been subjected to stress or
                   strain.
        5.21  The Driller shall direct and oversee all manriding operations being carried out
              above the drill floor in the derrick or mast as well as below the drill floor in the
                  cellar deck or sub structure area under the attendance of the Toolpusher. As such,
                they shall ensure all the requirements of the man-riding policies in the Health,
                 Safety, and Environmental (HSE) Manual are adhered to.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 13 از 259 ]

                          Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

        5.22   At no time during any operation should any person hang any type of weight from
                the control handle for the Electro Magnetic Brake Controller.

        5.23  The Driller shall ensure that proper maintenance is carried out on all drilling
              equipment according to the preventive maintenance schedule including all greasing
                 tasks on rig floor.

        5.24  The Driller shall operate and maintain any other equipment not outlined in these
                 instructions in accordance with equipment operating manuals and the Planned
              Maintenance System.

        5.25  The Driller shall insure that all drilling handling tools are inspected, in satisfactory
                operating condition and sufficient/correct size for the task to be performed.

        5.25   Written handover notes shall be utilized for tour change.

  6.0   RECORDS

         6.1    Trip Sheet, on board, current well program

         6.2   IADC Logbook, on board, current well program

         6.3    Job Safety Analysis, QHSE Dashboard, indefinitely

         6.4    Permit to Work, on board, six months

         6.5    Tour Change Handover notes, on board, current well program

         6.6    Calibration records, on-site, current

         6.7    All test charts associated with the well testing equipment, on board, current well
              program

  7.0   REFERENCES

         7.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)

         7.2   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)

  JACKUP OIM/PIC RESPONSIBILITIES REGARDING FIXED PLATFORM OPERATIONS

   1.0 PURPOSE

      This procedure describes Jack up OIM/PIC responsibilities regarding fixed platform operation
       for ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEM to establish a policy regarding the requirements for
      assuming responsibility as the OIM/PIC (Offshore Installation Manager/Person in Charge)
      over platform operations.

   2.0 SCOPE

      This procedure shall apply to  all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
       operations.

   3.0 RESPONSIBILITIES

      3.1 The OIM/PIC shall notify the Rig Manager of any requests received by the Operator's
          Representative onboard the rig to assume responsibility over any platform operations and
           shall not assume responsibility over any platform operations unless specific approvals are
          obtained by the Rig Manager.

      3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible through the Operations chain of command to the Regional
         Vice President for:

              3.2.1 Contacting ADES Operations and HSE Management upon receipt of any requests
                     that violate ADES Operation Manual submitted by the Operator for the rig
               OIM/PIC to assume responsibility over any platform operations.

              3.2.2 Ensuring a Letter of Agreement is duly executed by the Operator outlining the
                     limits of liability for assuming such responsibility, and to notify the OIM/PIC of
                   the requirements of such agreement.

              3.2.3 Operators QHSE document for the platform shall be reviewed and assessed by
            ADES QHSE department. If deemed necessary, a bridging document will be
                   created to cover personnel and operations conducted on the operator's platform.
   4.0 PROCEDURES

      4.1 The OIM/PIC will not assume responsibility over platform operations without having
         complete and total charge of all operations being conducted. The ADES Operations and


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 15 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────

       HSE Management shall be contacted any time a request is submitted by the Operator for
          having an ADES employee acting as the platform OIM/PIC.

       4.2 All liability issues associated with acting as the OIM/PIC over the platform or complex
            shall be clearly defined in the Drilling Contract or a signed Letter of Agreement attached
           to the Drilling Contract prior to any ADES employee assuming any of the Operator's
         OIM/PIC.

       4.3 The OIM/PIC prior to assuming responsibility shall have in place the following procedures:
               3.3.1 Security procedure for personnel embarking / dis-embarking the platform.
               3.3.2 Evacuation procedure of personnel and making safe of the current operation.
               3.3.3 Emergency response procedure of platform operations.

   5.0 RECORDS

     None

   6.0 REFERENCES

     None

 CRANE OPERATIONS

  1.0   PURPOSE

         This procedure describes crane operation for ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS to
          establish company policies for the operation of crane

  2.0   SCOPE

         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

  3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

      3.1 The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) is responsible for implementation of this
          policy.

      3.2 The OIM is responsible to manage entry of vessels into the 500-meter zone.

              •  This safety zone is that circular area about the MODU out to 500 meters where
                   permission to enter must be obtained.

              •  The OIM is ultimately responsible for controlling entry and exit of supply / crew
                    vessels in the safety zone.

              •  The OIM may establish agreements with the Operator on how these vessels are
                     to be managed. If the Operator is managing the vessels, information on this
                       traffic is to be communicated to the OIM and may also be communicated to the
                 Crane Operator

      3.3 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for:

              3.3.1 Ensuring rig cranes are operated in accordance with the requirements set forth in
                     this standard. Exceptions to this standard may be taken by the OIM/PIC, if in his
                   opinion, the consequences of not doing so in a specific situation will present less
                   of a threat to the safety of rig personnel or the rig than would be created if this
                  standard was complied with. This is only done through approved dispensation
                   process.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 17 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                         Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                 Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 9

              3.3.2 Determining when adverse weather or any other environmental conditions give
                  cause  to discontinue crane operations, as well as when they may be  re-
               commenced. The OIM/PIC shall take into account any environmental limits
                  published by the crane manufacturer.

              3.3.3 Ensuring crane records are maintained in accessible files.

              3.3.4 Crane Operator (s) shall normally be the only persons allowed to operate ADES,
                     drilling rig cranes.

                  Designating Approved personnel other than the Crane Operator (s) are as follows:

                       - Certified assistant crane operator (s) as per company policy.
                       - A person undergoing training to become a Crane Operator (supervised)
                       - Approved manufacturer’s inspectors or qualified company mechanics
                       - Qualified OIM / Tool pusher (must have a Crane Operator’s license)

                Approved personnel to operate cranes in addition to the Crane Operator (s)
                  assigned to the rig. Such personnel shall be designated after:

                    •  Both the OIM/PIC and the assigned Crane Operator are satisfied that an
                          individual has reached certain competency levels required to perform
                            either limited or unlimited operations of a particular type of crane, and the
                          individual to be designated meets the prerequisites of Job Descriptions OF
                          Assistant Crane Operator; - OR Crane Operator Trainee; AS appropriate.
                    •  The  individual  that  is  being  designated  has  received  a  physical
                        examination before being hired.
                    •  The designation of such individuals to operate cranes to hold a valid crane
                          operator license/certificate from approved third party for the type of crane
                        used on the rig.
                    •  The OIM/PIC shall maintain an up to date list of all personnel assigned to
                          the rig that are designated crane operators, along with their letters of
                           designation.
      3.4 The Crane Operator is responsible to the OIM/PIC for:

              3.4.1 Advising the OIM/PIC when operating and environmental conditions deteriorate
                    to an extent such that discontinuing crane operations should be considered, and
                    for monitoring such conditions until such time as he may recommend to the
              OIM/PIC that crane operations could be resumed safely, generating or revising
                      lifting plans as required.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 18 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 9

              3.4.2 An assigned Banks man (i.e., Flagman) shall be present to direct the Crane
             Operator each time the crane is to be operated. The Banksman shall not be involved in
             handling the loads (load handling will be done by a minimum of two (2) roustabouts /
               riggers). The Banksman shall wear a hi-visibility vest identifying him as the designated
           Banksman.

              3.4.3 The Crane Operator (or lift supervisor if carried) shall inspect each lifting sling
               prior to use to ensure against:

                      •   Incorrect color-coding or markings
                      •  Excessive wear to the wire rope.
                      •  Broken strands in the wire makeup.
                      •  Improper wire rope clip arrangement for sling eyes.
                      •   Incorrect size and out of date sling for the intended lift.
                      •  Defective hooks or shackles used in the hookup assembly.
                      •  Must have the same size shackles (if using two or more) on a lift and
                        must be the proper size shackles for the load being lifted.
                      •  The Crane Operator shall require the use of tag lines on all lifts made
                            unless local regulation prohibit such use. Handgrip knots shall not be
                           placed in tag lines. The knots can become entangled in other equipment
                             creating a hazard. Two tag lines should be used when picking up
                             tubular/casing in windy conditions to control the load.

              3.4.4 Ensuring all of the requirements of standards are complied with at all times.

              3.4.5 Being aware and cognizant of crane load capacities, and not attempting a lift
                  exceeding 100 % of the safe working load of the weakest part of the load path, or
                   the crane capacity itself. This decision must take into account an allowance for
                dynamic forces. These would include such things as wind conditions, rig and
                 workboat vessel movement during lifting and landing loads, as well as the listing
                   of floating rigs as it affects the crane's slewing capacity and load radius. If these
                dynamic conditions cannot be estimated with any degree of confidence, a 20%
                   derating factor shall be used. If the Crane Operator is still not confident that this
                   derating factor will provide an adequate factor of safety for the lift, he shall
                   consult with the OIM/PIC prior to the lift and consider altering the lift plan with
                  a view towards decreasing the weight of the loads to be handled to minimize the
                     risk of overload, including all static and running wire ropes, slings, the crane
                         itself, and other materials in the load path. A detailed lifting plan to be submitted
                 and approved prior to commencing the lifting operation.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 19 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 4 of 9

              3.4.6 Ensuring the upper and lower stop limit devices for the boom, anti 2-block limits,
                  slewing limits (if fitted), and emergency stops are tested at some time during each
               work shift. This shall be performed under the supervision of the Crane Operator
                on duty with assistance from competent rig crew members. For upper and lower
              boom limits that are activated by limit switches, the Crane Operator shall ensure
                     that these devices are tripped by hand to simulate an actual stop limit rather than
                  running the boom itself into the stops. The same would apply to slewing limits (if
                        fitted), as well as other devices that can be accessed safely. Anti 2-blocking
                   devices for the headache ball and block/hook assembly shall be activated by
                  slowly pulling them into the tripping device. Some hydraulically-powered cranes
                    are equipped with a relief valve that opens and dumps hydraulic system

                   pressure when the headache ball or block runs into a mechanical stop. This shall
                 be tested and proven to be set properly. (Note: Some crane operators have
                  expressed concerns regarding pulling the headache ball or lift block into the
                 mechanical stops on these types of cranes, thinking that such practices are
                damaging to the wire rope or other components in the load path. Investigation
                    into this revealed that no damage will take place and that this practice is approved
                by the crane manufacturer. If any crane operator still has a concern about this,
                   contact Technical Service Support with further details).

              3.4.7 Maintenance supervisor to investigate and implement whatever modifications
                  necessary to allow the relief valve pressure setting to be determined during these
                       tests. Defective stop limits shall be repaired or replaced and proven functional
                    prior to resuming crane operations with the crane in question.

              3.4.8 Ensuring that he remains at the controls of the crane until the load is landed safely,
                 and the hook is disengaged from the load and positioned such that uncontrolled
                 motion of the block or headache ball does not pose a danger to rig personnel or
                   the rig and its equipment.

              3.4.9 Directly supervising personnel who are Crane Operator Trainees or training to be
                   designated crane operators while they are operating the cranes.

              3.4.10 Ensuring tag lines are in use for  all loads at  all times, unless otherwise
                  determined to be a hazard as determined by a Job Risk Assessment (JRA) and the
                OIM/PIC.

              3.4.11 Ensuring that all personnel involved in crane operations are familiar with and
                  demonstrate their competency in the use of proper hand signals.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 20 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 4 of 9

              3.4.12 Ensuring the crane is shut down immediately after an abnormal load has been
                    incurred, the OIM/PIC has been informed, and visual inspections are carried out
                    to the components in the load path that have incurred an abnormal load. Abnormal
                   loads include, but are not limited to those that exceed 100% of the Safe Working
               Load rating of the crane, crane pedestal, static and/or running ropes, slings, whip
                      lines, headache balls, blocks, sheaves, boom or whip line boom tips as applicable.
                Such an inspection is also required whenever load path components specified
                 above are subjected to dynamic conditions, such as those incurred during heavy
                   seas that cause high spike loading or sudden off-loading of components in the
                  load path, even though the static load is below 100% of the Safe Working Load
                    rating for the component (s) exposed to such loading.

              3.4.13 Ensuring when the crane boom is not cradled, and when no one is at the crane
                    controls:

                      •  The boom is left in a position such that it does not cause an interference
                          with other cranes or other operations
                      •  All power to the crane is shut down or disconnected

                      •  That all slings are unhooked from the headache ball or lift block and
                         stowed
                      •  That the headache ball and lift block are left in a position such that
                            uncontrolled motion will not cause a hazard to rig personnel or the rig
                        and its equipment

              3.4.14 Ensuring a JRA is carried out prior to all lifts exceeding 70% of the Safe
                Working Load of the crane, crane pedestal, and/or running ropes, pendant lines,
                     slings, whip lines, headache balls, blocks, sheaves, boom or whip line boom tips
                   as applicable. This does not preclude conducting JRAs for lifts under 70% of the
                  Safe Working Loads specified above when they are of an unusual nature,
                   including but not limited to awkwardly sized, fragile, or hazardous loads, lifts to
                 be conducted in adverse environmental conditions, or where it may be necessary
                    to access stacking loads, in which case it will only be approved equipment that
                 can be stacked under a PTW and an approved detailed lifting plan.

              3.4.15 Ensuring that all loads are fitted with lifting hardware and slings that are certified
                    to have a SWL in excess of the load to be handled prior to conducting the lift,
                   including allowances  for  sling angles, and other  factors  that may induce
                    additional loads that could exceed the SWL of the lifting hardware and slings to
                 be used. In addition, the Crane Operator shall ensure the slings and other lifting
                 hardware are visually inspected for damage or wear and tear that would reduce


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 21 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

                   the SWL including any pad eyes that the slings may be attached to. The Crane
                  Operator shall also ensure the rigging is configured in such a manner as to avoid
                 any slings from coming into contact with interferences or sharp edges that may
                    cut into or damage the slings while under load.

   4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

      4.1 Crane booms shall be cradled in the boom rest, and all power shall be secured to the
          crane whenever:

              4.1.1 The cranes are not in use for an appreciable amount of time, i.e. several hours.
               The decision to cradle or not cradle the boom in any given situation rests with the
                  assigned Crane Operator. However, the OIM/PIC may override the Crane
                   Operator's decision.
              4.1.2 Maintenance activities are carried out to the crane, except when such maintenance
                    requires the boom to be elevated prior to cradling the boom, all slings shall be
                unhooked from the block and/or headache ball hook and stowed.
              4.1.3 At 35 miles per hour (mph) (35 Knots) or greater sustained winds.

      4.2 Boom rests or cradles shall be located such that:

              4.2.1 The boom shall not interfere with the operating radius of another crane

              4.2.2 The boom shall not present a hazard to helicopter operations

              4.2.3 Overriding the boom angle stop limits is not necessary in order to cradle the boom.
                        If a boom or its rest cannot be located as described, the boom shall be positioned
                    to avoid interference with other crane operations and helicopter landings and
                    take-offs with all power secured to the crane, and all slings removed from the
                hook while it is unattended. In some circumstances, it may improve the efficiency
                   of crane operations to have more than one boom rest for a given crane boom, or
                 have a portable boom cradle to avoid interferences described above when the
              boom is cradled.

      4.3 Crane operations shall be suspended whenever someone calls a "time out" or when the
        OIM/PIC determines that operating and/or environmental conditions present a safety
         hazard to rig personnel or the rig and its equipment. These conditions may include, but are
          not limited to high winds, sea conditions affecting rig or workboat motions, lightning,
         heavy rain, sleet ,snow, fog, icy conditions, sand storms, and ambient temperatures
         below/above the minimum/maximum design operating temperature of the crane. Crane
          operations shall resume after the OIM/PIC has determined that environmental conditions


────────────────────────────────────────────

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

         have improved to an extent that the cranes can be operated without presenting a hazard to
           rig personnel or the rig and its equipment.

      4.4 All rig personnel are required to call a "time out" immediately to the Crane Operator or the
        OIM/PIC when an observance is made of the condition of the crane itself, how  it is
          operated, or any damage to the slings, shackles or other loose gear that causes them to
          believe that a hazard may exist to rig personnel or the rig and its equipment. The Crane
         Operator and the OIM/PIC will cease crane operations whenever a "time out" is called, and
         conduct a proper investigation so the OIM/PIC can make a final ruling.

      4.5 All crane ropes (including running ropes and static pendant lines), slings, shackles, and
          other loose gear shall be removed, destroyed, and replaced with certified material in good
          condition whenever either the Crane Operator or the OIM/PIC believes the condition of
         such material could pose a hazard to rig personnel or the rig and its equipment.

      4.6 When reeling off line, the Crane Operator shall ensure a minimum of five wraps, or half of
          the drum, whichever is greater, remain on the drum as part of daily checks.

      4.7 All winch line (load, boom, and fast) cables shall be changed out every two years, unless
        damage is found, in which case they shall be changed out immediately.

      4.8 Pennant line cables need to be changed every five years, unless damage is found, in which
          case they shall be changed out immediately.

      4.9 The following records shall be maintained by the barge master for each crane:

              4.9.1 Failure Reports.

              4.9.2 Daily Crane Report Check List:

                      •  Record of maintenance routines required to be performed on a daily
                             basis
                      •  Daily inspection and testing of stop limits

              4.9.3 Weekly Crane Report Check List:

                      •  Record of maintenance routines required to be performed on a weekly
                             basis
              4.9.4 Certificates: Scanned images of the most recent certificates issued for:
                      •  Crane Surveys and Load Tests conducted for/witnessed by the
                              certifying authority

                      •  Crane wire and stingers
                      •  All loose lifting gear (headache balls, wedge socket terminations,
                             blocks, hooks, dead-end clamps, etc.)
                      •  Copies of most recent certificates for the calibration of the load-
                     moment indicators
              4.9.5 Historical Records:

                      •  Records of repairs performed as well as replacement of components
                               installed in the load path, including the crane pedestal
              4.9.6 Regulations:

                      •  Scanned images (pdf or jpg files) of the applicable editions of API
                        Spec 2C and RP2D
                      •  Applicable rules or regulations pertaining to the current crane
                                certificate issued by ABS, DNV, or other authority.
              4.9.7 Operating Procedures

                      •  As required by the most recent revision of this standard

      4.10 Only the Crane Operators assigned to the rig, and other personnel designated by the
        OIM/PIC shall operate the cranes.

      4.11 At a minimum, all cranes shall be fitted with the following devices with all associated
          instrumentation and displays:

              4.11.1 A load-moment indicator for main hoisting operations

              4.11.2 A load indicator for whip-line hoisting operations Such equipment shall be
                  maintained in serviceable condition at  all times. Crane operations shall be
                 suspended whenever this equipment is not functional and shall not be resumed
                      until repairs or replacements are carried out, and the load indicators are calibrated,
                 and function tested satisfactorily.
      4.12 Personnel baskets
              4.12.1 Personnel basket can be used under these conditions.

                      4.12.1.1. The OIM, Crane Operator, Operator Representative, and boat Captain
                                shall determine the safe sea height to transfer personal onto or from
                         motor vessel. The final decision shall be by the OIM.
                     4.12.1.2 This determination shall factor in combined sea height, included swells
                         and roll period, sea current and size of motor vessel. Also the ability of


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 24 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS

                              the Boat Captain to hold the vessel steady enough to load or unload
                             personal safely.
                     4.12.1.3 The crane operator shall make a dry run with the personnel basket
                            before transferring any personnel onto or from motor vessel.
                     4.12.1.4 The crane to be certified for personnel lifting and emergency lowering
                            device to be checked prior to personnel transfer.
                     4.12.1.5 A safety (secondary retention wire with sufficient capacity to installed
                        on the personnel basket before commencing the list.
                     4.12.1.6 Only certified personnel basket to be used for the transfer under permit
                               to work system.
               4.12.2 A Job Safety Analysis shall be completed with all parties prior to the
                     operation.
               4.12.3 Personnel working on the boat deck must wear an approved work vest.

               4.12.4 Crane Operators shall not allow more line than necessary to feed out on load
                      lines to ensure that pickup lines, load blocks, hooks, load lines, etc., do not strike
                   personnel working the supply boat decks.

               4.12.5 Personnel basket shall not be used to transport personnel when the wind speed
                  exceeds 35 mph except for dire emergency.


              4.12.6 One man shall not be sent from the MODU to work alone on a supply boat
                   deck.

                     4.12.6.1 At least two men shall be sent.

                     4.12.6.2 Both shall have experience in such work and must wear work vests.

      4.13 Crane Safety Devices for the protection of personnel, hoisting equipment, and the
           operations, various crane safety requirements shall be used as follows:

              4.13.1 Crane boom and load lines shall be activated by “power up” and “power
                down” control.

              4.13.2 Each crane winch line shall be equipped with some type of anti-two-block device
                     to keep from running ball or block into boom tip.
              4.13.3 The boom, swing gear, and each load line drum shall be equipped with a
                “Dog” and / or internal brake to prevent load creeping.
              4.13.4 Safety locks shall be on each control lever to prevent accidental engagement of
                     the wrong control.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 25 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 200
                                Policy:  CRANE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 9 of 9

              4.13.5 All moving parts of the power train shall be adequately guarded due to the area’s
                    close quarters where personnel are required to maneuver.
              4.13.6 Boom stops shall be on the crane to prevent pulling the boom past high center.
              4.13.7 Booms shall be equipped with a shut-off switch for high and low angles (if fitted
                              / available) and checked as fully operational before each use.
              4.13.8 Lubrication fittings shall be positioned so personnel shall not have to come in
                    contact with moving equipment while servicing the unit.
              4.13.9 Engine exhausts shall be routed or covered to prevent burn injury to personnel.
             4.13.10 Fuel tanks shall be positioned where spills shall not run onto the exhaust
                    manifold or the engine.
             4.13.11 Offshore cranes shall not have gasoline engines.
             4.13.12 Cranes subject to operating in a gaseous atmosphere shall be equipped with
                     spark arrestor mufflers or exhausts with water sprays.

             4.13.13 Crane walk-around shall have handrails and toe boards the same as any other
                      elevated walking surface.
             4.13.14 Each crane shall contain the counterweight specified by the manufacturer while
                       in operation.
             4.13.15 Crane equipment shall be stopped before performing adjustments, repairs, or
                       servicing.
             4.13.16 Crane manufacturer’s preventative maintenance programs shall be followed.
                   Replacement parts must meet the specifications of the original equipment.
             4.13.17 Personnel performing repair work on crane equipment shall be provided a safe
                      place in which to work.

             4.13.18 All slings shall be inspected and lubricated on a routine basis and recorded in
                      the sling register.

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES
        None

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 9

  TUBULAR HANDLING

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines on safe and efficient tubular handling.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) is responsible for overall
                implementation of this policy.

          3.2   The Crane Operator is responsible to implement the specific requirements of this
                  policy.

   4.0  PROCEDURE (General)

          4.1    Box, pin faces, and shoulders provide the seal areas for the joints when screwed
                  together.

          4.2    Proper makeup torque is essential to prevent downhole drill string failures such as
                washouts, pin and box fatigue cracks, and split boxes.

          4.3    Tubulars must be tailed with the aid of rope when going from V-door to mouse hole
                 or tailing from pipe rack to rotary.

          4.4     Drill collars shall be tailed, depending on the size of the drill collar, with a rope, air
                    hoist, or crane.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Care and Maintenance)

          5.1    All facilities and installations shall define a care and maintenance program for
                  tubulars under their control.

          5.2    Tubulars shall be inspected every Drill Pipe (DP) 100,000 feet, 50,000 bottom hole
               assembly (BHA), or per Operator’s request at Operator’s expense.

                  5.2.1  At a minimum, tubulars shall be inspected according to the standards
                         established in ADES tubular inspection criteria.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 27 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 9

                  5.2.2  However, many Operators require their own specific higher levels of
                          inspection. Our criteria are DS1, Level 3.

          5.3     Plastic and / or metal thread protectors shall be used on all tubulars.

          5.4    Original inspection and maintenance record shall be maintained on the current
                 inventory on the installation.

          5.5  A completed updated inventory of  all tubulars on the  installation  shall be
                maintained daily.

          5.6   The Rig Manager and the mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU) shall maintain or
               have access to the most current copies of the International Association of Drilling
                 Contractors (IADC)  Driller’s Manual,  as  well  as  tubular-related American
                Petroleum Institute (API) Specifications.

          5.7    Tubulars shall be protected from corrosion during long-term storage.

          5.8   As tubulars are needed in each region, company stocks of spare and excess tubulars
                   shall  be  used  to  the  extent  practical,  taking  into  consideration  tubulars
                   specifications, conditions, priorities, urgency of need, location, and mobilization.

          5.9   The Location Operations Manager, or his designee, is the designated focal point for
                  tubular matters.

          5.10   Supply Chain shall advise whether or not company stock is available.

                 5.10.1  If available, Supply Chain shall assist in coordinating the transfer of the
                         tubulars required.

                 5.10.2  If not available, the region shall coordinate the purchase of tubulars needed.

                 5.10.3 Order specifications shall meet or exceed ADES tubular specifications.

          5.11   Ensure all box and pin thread protectors remain on the tubulars (drill pipe, casing,
               and collars) until they are added into the drill string.

          5.12  MODU personnel shall keep thread lubricant on the threads of tubulars to prevent
                     rust.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 28 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 9

   8.0  PROCEDURE (Making a Connection with Top Drive on Drill Pipe)

          8.1    While making a connection, the Driller shall reciprocate the drill string as many
                 times as necessary to ensure the pipe is free in the hole.

          8.2   Where fitted, typically the iron roughneck will be used to make up and break out
                  pipe. However, shall tongs be used, ensure that both sets of tongs are used to break
                 out or make up drill pipe connections.

          8.3    Prior to stabbing the new joint into the string, the threads shall be cleaned and pipe
               dope generously applied for proper makeup.

          8.4    Should tongs be required, one tong at a time should put on the pipe to be torqued
                 up, then one at a time to be removed.

          8.5    There must be at least two people pulling the slips. One man pulling by himself is
                  prohibited.

          8.6     If new drill pipe is made up, it shall be doped, torqued up, backed out, threads
                checked, re-doped, and torqued to the correct amount, unless drill pipe connections
               were made and break at factory.

   9.0  PROCEDURE (Tubular Handling General)
          9.1  A risk assessment (Job Safety Analysis) shall be performed prior to any tubular
                  operations.

          9.2    All personnel shall stay clear of the catwalk when tubulars are being moved on or
                   off of the drill floor.

          9.3    Tubulars must be tailed into the mousehole by the use of an air hoist, crane, or rope
                  to avoid hand injuries.

          9.4   Whenever possible, personnel should not stand on layered pipe to roll joints
                  together. Pipe should be rolled from the ends.

          9.5    Tubulars should always be rolled or adjusted using a pry bar.

          9.6   When positioning pipe, never allow your feet to get under the drop path of the pipe.

          9.7   When horizontally moving tubulars, a double wrap sling must be made prior to each
                  use. The slings must be discarded if evidence of any defects is found.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 29 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 4 of 9


          9.8    Stanchion posts shall be used at all times when stacking pipe. They should be
                  visually inspected prior to use. Chocks should only be used to secure top partial
                rows.

          9.9    Air hoist operators should never oppose the direction of the block (i.e., never raise
                 as the block is descending and never lower as the block is coming up).

          9.10  The V-door shall be fitted with an appropriate back stop to ensure that the pipe does
                 not slide back down.

          9.11  The air hoist shall be thoroughly inspected in accordance with planned maintenance
              (PM) routine (i.e., structural attachment, mounting bolts, brakes, wire rope, and end
                 connections). A visual inspection just prior to use shall also be done.

          9.12   Short Service Employees (SSEs) shall be supervised as per HSE 100.25.

          9.13  Whenever a nylon strap is used, a double half hitch should be put in 4” below the
                 upset and the competency of the person cinching the pipe should be verified by the
                    Driller. A permit to work is required when using nylon and other synthetic straps.

          9.14  When casing is sent to the MODU and not stripped, the use of casing hooks (to
                 offload only) is not acceptable and a safe alternative is to be found.

          9.15  When storing drill pipe, hevi-weight and collars, they shall be cleaned, doped, and
               have thread protectors on both the box end and pin end.

          9.16   Keelhaul Method

                 9.16.1 Tubulars are brought in one at a time to the top of the V-door by the rig
                         crane.

                 9.16.2 The air hoist or the block elevators shall be attached to the forward end of
                         the joint to be lifted.

                 9.16.3 The weight is then transferred from the crane to the forward lifting device
                              (air hoist or block elevators) and then tailed in by the rig crane as the joint
                               is being lifted.

                 9.16.4 Tubulars can be moved in multiples to be placed in the V-door, using the
                       Keelhaul Method if they do not exceed the recommended amounts.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 30 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 5 of 9

          9.17  When keelhauling in collars greater than 6½”, a lift nipple or appropriately rated
                         lift cap shall be utilized.

   10.0  PROCEDURE (Movement of Tubulars to the Catwalk [Horizontal])

          10.1  When moving tubulars (on board the MODU) a double wrap sling (rated for load)
                with taglines shall be used. Even when using a spreader bar, the slings must be
                double wrapped, with exception of handling chrome tubing.

          10.2  When moving bundled pipe (horizontally) with the deck crane, the following
                should never be exceeded:

                 10.2.1 3 joints of drill collars (depending on crane safe working limits [SWL])

                 10.2.2 3 joints of hevi-weight (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 10.2.3 6 joints of 5” drill pipe (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 10.2.4 9 joints of 3½” drill pipe or tubing (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 10.2.5 25 joints of tubing (depending on crane SWL limits)

   11.0  PROCEDURE (Movement of Tubulars from the Catwalk to the V-Door)

          11.1   Either an air hoist or a crane can be used in this process.

          11.2   Drill pipe, heavy-weight, and collars up to 6½” can be moved to the V-door (from
                  the catwalk) by the rig air hoist with individual lift caps or a single joint elevator
             made up to each joint to be lifted.

                 11.2.1 A maximum of six (6) lift caps at one time may be used but it shall not
                      exceed the SWL of the air hoist.

                 11.2.2 The pipe shall then be pulled up the V-door and secured by an appropriate
                       backstop so it shall not slide back down.

          11.3    If applicable, a multi-part sling shall be used to attach to each lift cap used in the
                     lifting process.

          11.4   Each leg of the multi-part sling shall be fitted with a positive locking safety latch.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 31 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 6 of 9

          11.5   Drill pipe, hevi-weight, and collars up to 6½” can be put in the V-door utilizing the
                 crane and the keel hauling method.

                 11.5.1 Approved rigging must be used, combined with the attachment of a rig floor
                             air hoist to guide and prevent sliding down V-door.

                 11.5.2  It shall then be secured and lowered to V-door back stop.

          11.6   Collars larger than 6½” shall be moved to the V-door one at a time utilizing the
                Keelhaul Method.

          11.7   All tubing and casing to be put in the V-door, with the use of the rig crane shall not
                exceed limits as set below.

                 11.7.1 12 joints 1½” to 3½” (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 11.7.2 6 joints when greater than 3½” to 7” (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 11.7.3 3 joints of 7” to 9-5/8” (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 11.7.4 2 joints when greater than 9-5/8” to 16” (depending on crane SWL limits)

                 11.7.5 1 joint when greater than 16” (depending on crane SWL limits)


   12.0  PROCEDURE (Movement of Tubulars from the Catwalk “Directly” to the V-Door
         or Rotary / Mousehole)

         (Note: this entire section is not applicable on rigs which are fitted with pipe racking
               systems and pickup elevators.)

          12.1  By use of a lift cap and swivel, or single joint elevators, drill pipe, hevi-weight, and
                   collars up to 6½” can be lifted by the rig air hoist, one at a time, directly to the
               mousehole or rotary.

          12.2   Collars larger than 6½” can be moved directly to the mousehole or rotary by the
                Keelhaul Method.
          12.3  1¼” tubing up to 9-5/8” casing can be lifted by the rig air hoist directly to the
               mousehole or rotary, one at a time, using either a single joint elevator or a lift cap
               and swivel. Any other methods shall need to be approved using a Request for
                 Dispensation (ADM 115).


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 32 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 7 of 9

          12.4   9-5/8” to 26” casing can be lifted to the V-door by use of the rig crane and the
                Keelhaul Method. From the V-door  it can be put in the rotary by use of the
                 appropriate lifting device.

          12.5   In the case of drive pipe with pad-eyes, appropriately rated slings shall be used
                along with the Keelhaul Method.

   13.0  PROCEDURE (Movement of Tubulars from the V-Door to the Mousehole / Rotary)

          13.1   Either an air hoist or a crane can be used in this process.

          13.2   Drill pipe, hevi-weight, and up to 6½” collars shall be lifted one at a time using a
                         lift cap and swivel or a single joint elevator. Any other methods shall need to be
                approved by using a Request for Dispensation (ADM 115).

          13.3   All tubing sizes (generally 1¼” to 3½”) shall be lifted by use of a lift cap or single
                    joint elevator. Any other methods shall need to be approved by using a Request for
                 Dispensation (ADM 115).

          13.4   All casing (except drive pipe) shall be lifted by the use of single joint elevators.
             Any other method shall need approval using a Request for Dispensation (ADM
                 115).

   14.0  PROCEDURE (Movement of Tubulars from the Rotary / Mousehole to V-Door /
         Catwalk)

          14.1   Drill pipe and hevi-weight shall be laid out one at a time using a lift cap and swivel
                 or single joint elevators. Any other method shall need approval using a Request for
                 Dispensation (ADM 115).

          14.2   Drill pipe and hevi-weight can be lowered using the rig air hoist directly to the
                catwalk by the use of a lift cap and swivel or a single joint elevator.

          14.3  A lift nipple shall be made up to each drill collar larger than 6½”. They shall be
                lowered to the V-door one at a time using the rig elevators (it is acceptable to lower
                      drill collars with the air hoist if an appropriately rated lift cap and swivel are used).
          14.4   Drill collars can be lowered directly to the catwalk using the crane to tail them out
               and the rig air hoist to transfer the weight from the elevators to the crane sling.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 33 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 300
                                 Policy:  TUBULAR HANDLING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 8 of 9

          14.5  1¼” tubing to-9 5/8” casing shall be lowered to the V-door or directly to the catwalk
              by use of a lift cap and swivel, or single joint elevators. Any other methods shall
               need to be approved by using a Request for Dispensation (ADM 115).

          14.6   Casing should be lowered by the reverse Keelhaul Method, directly to the catwalk
                 using the crane to tail them out and the rig air hoist to transfer the weight from the
                  elevators to the crane sling.

   15.0  PROCEDURE (Recommended Steps for Laying out Collars)

          15.1  As appropriate, each rig may incorporate this section into rig-specific procedures
                 or work instructions as appropriate and in accordance with the rig capabilities.

          15.2   Drill collars shall be moved from the rotary / mousehole by use of the rig elevators.
              They shall be lowered by the block to the V-door one at a time.

          15.3  When the collar to be disconnected is sitting in the rotary slips and collar clamp, a
                         lift nipple shall be installed at the rig floor and snugged up with a chain tong.

          15.4  The Driller shall lower the elevators and attach them to the lift nipple on the next
                   collar to be laid out and the collar clamp shall be removed.

          15.5  The Driller shall lift the collar to the next break point where the Floorman shall set
                  the slips and install the drill collar clamp.

          15.6  The collar shall then be broken out and one part of a crane cable sling shall be
                 attached to the tail of the collar to be laid out.

          15.7  The Driller shall lower the collar to a position where a Floorman shall attach the air
                    hoist.

          15.8  The air hoist shall be tightened to a point where it accepts the weight of the collar.

          15.9  The elevators shall then be released and the second part of the crane cable sling
                   shall be attached to the collar.

         15.10 The crane shall then accept the weight of the collar.

         15.11 The air hoist cable shall then be removed and used to support the removal of the lift
                  nipple.

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 310
                       Policy:   DRILL STRING AND CASING LOADING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 2

  DRILL STRING AND CASING LOADING

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines on loading drill strings and casing
           tubulars.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) is responsible for overall
                implementation of this policy.

          3.2   The Crane Operator is responsible to implement the specific requirements of this
                  policy.

   4.0  PROCEDURE

          4.1   Use tag lines with no knots in them to guide in the casing.

          4.2   The Crane Operator shall not leave a running crane unattended.

          4.3   When tying onto the drill pipe or casing, keep the slings spaced out the same on
                both ends.

          4.4   No one shall walk under an overhead load.

          4.5     If a helicopter is approaching the mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU) to land and
                        if the Crane Operator cannot lower the crane boom, he should lower the load to the
                 deck, turn the crane boom away from the heliport, then turn off the crane and stand
                 outside the cab or away from the controls.

          4.6    Clear off the area of the pipe rack or barge where the drill string, casing, or
               equipment is to be set.

          4.7    Ensure no tripping or pinch point hazards exist in the area where the pipe shall be
                   set or moved from.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 36 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                          Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                        Policy No. OPS 310
                      Policy:   DRILL STRING AND CASING LOADING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed
                                                          Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                  Page 2 of 2

         4.8   The Crane Operator shall ensure the proper strength of slings is used for the loading
                or unloading process.
         4.9   The barge/boat should be moved next to the MODU crane/location and securely
                  tied up.

        4.10    If picking up one joint of pipe or casing,  it should be wrapped twice before
               unloading from vessel.

        4.11  Once the load is ready to be moved, the “designated Banksman”, either on the rig
                or the supply vessel, should signal the Crane Operator to lift the load. Radios can
                 also be used for communication.

        4.12  The Crane Operator should keep up with the number of joints he has moved; this
                information may be recorded in a tally book.

        4.13  As the load is moved, the Crane Operator should ensure the load does not pass over
              any person.

        4.14  When the pipe or casing is lowered to the set down area, the Roustabouts or
               Floorhands should grasp the tag lines and maneuver the load into place. Remember:
             do not place your hands on the pipe or casing.

        4.15  When loading casing you should stagger the joints  (if possible) so that the
                 protectors can be removed and the thread cleared prior to running it. Also, all casing
               should be numbered, measured, recorded, and marked as it is brought onto the rig.

        4.16  Once the pipe or casing is set down (especially at the end of rows), it shall be
              chocked so that it cannot roll off and cause hazards. You should stop loading before
                the top of the pipe stops.

  5.0   RECORDS
       None

  6.0   REFERENCES
       None
