                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 320
                                  Policy:   RUNNING CASING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 5

  RUNNING CASING

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to provide a standard for running casing.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) is responsible for overall
                implementation of this policy.

          3.2   The Driller is responsible for immediate supervision of all rig floor activities and
                  to implement the specific requirements of this policy.

   4.0  GENERAL

          4.1    Pipe shall be strapped on the trip out of the hole prior to running casing.

          4.2  A casing tally and running order shall be available in the doghouse previous to
                running casing.

          4.3    Contract-required equipment for running casing shall be inspected ready for
                  operation. This includes  all elevators,  slips, spiders, crossovers,  etc. Casing
                   centralizers shall be on the drill floor or fitted prior to operation.

          4.4    Float equipment to be run (if any) is to be inspected.

          4.5    Lifting equipment shall be inspected by the Crane Operator, and ready for
                  operation. Casing shall be lifted to the catwalk by use of positive-locking casing
                hooks. For rigs so fitted, casing up to 20” shall be lifted to the catwalk by use of the
                knuckle boom crane.

          4.6    Chain shall not be used in casing running operations. Always inspect the pickup
                   lines and all equipment before the job and during the job.

          4.7  A casing fill-up line or Top Drive System (TDS) casing filling tool shall be put in
                  place. Preferably the fill-up line shall be powered with a centrifugal pump equipped


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 38 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 320
                                  Policy:   RUNNING CASING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 5

                with a bypass line. The fill-up line shall have an opening closing valve at the
                 discharge end. Casing shall be filled upon reaching the last casing shoe.

          4.7    Casing running thread protectors shall be installed on casing while on the catwalk.

          4.8   The rig floor shall be clear of all equipment and tools not necessary for the casing
                running operation.

          4.10   Casing stabbing board (or basket, if so fitted) shall be in in good working order.
                Stabbing board (or basket, if so fitted) inspection shall be completed and reviewed
              by the Toolpusher on duty. An inertia reel fall arrest shall be installed to the
               permanent derrick structure. The casing stabber shall utilize a full body harness in
                 conjunction with personal fall arrest as per HSE 100.16

          4.11  The casing power tongs shall be fitted with a snub line. If a platform is required for
                  the operation of the power tongs it shall be in a position that does not interfere with
                  the operation of the slips.

          4.12   Casing running operations shall be preceded with a pre-job safety meeting. A Job
                 Safety Analysis shall be performed and reviewed by each applicable crew member.

          4.13   Before the rigging up operation starts, allow time to clear the area to place the
                equipment.

          4.14  The hydromatic shall be engaged at the start of the job and adjusted for the weight
                 of the string as it increases.

          4.15  Make sure that the Emergency Shutdown (ESD) is working by testing during
                annual Class or Flag inspections.

          4.16  Keep the casing protectors in a bag or basket.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Well Control Considerations)

          5.1     If casing rams are used, bonnets shall be tested prior to operation.

          5.2  A circulating head shall be available on the drill floor complete with crossover to
             make up to casing. A low torque valve shall be made up in the assembly and in full-
               open position.
          5.3    Float equipment must be inspected for integrity. Float equipment if run shall not be
                 disabled in any manner to allow casing to fill through float.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 39 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 320
                                  Policy:   RUNNING CASING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 5


          5.4  A record of the mud tank level shall be made for comparison with the calculated
              volume increase due to steel displacement.

                  5.4.1  The Driller, Shaker hand, and Derrick hand should record information in
                            their tally books (or other rig-based method) for comparison when needed
                          (these records do not have to be retained).

                  5.4.2  Records of mud tank levels, primarily the pit tank, are recorded on the Pit
                   Volume Totalizer (PVT) monitoring system.

          5.5   The Driller shall keep the hole full at all times. Casing shall be run at a rate that
                   shall not create excessive surge pressures.

          5.6    Take care not to drop any objects in the casing while filling.

          5.7     If using a fill up tool to fill up the casing while running in the hole, you should
                lower the pop off valve at the mud pump and adjust to the rating pressure of the fill
              up tool.

   6.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Running Casing)

          6.1    Personnel shall remain clear of the path to the rotary while casing is being moved
                towards the rotary center.

                  6.1.1  There shall be no personnel on the V-door stairway during the movement
                        of casing in the V-door. (This requirement is not applicable on rigs not using
                       a V-door.)

                  6.1.2   If the pipe racking system is unavailable or not fitted, casing shall be tailed
                          to the rotary by means of a tail rope. When using a tail rope to tail in the
                          casing, inspect regularly. It should be tied high on the V-door poles so if it
                               is picked up high it shall not jump out of the tail rope.

                  6.1.3  The tail rope shall not in any way be attached to the hand, arm, or body of
                         the operator. There shall be no loops or knots in the tail rope.

                  6.1.4  The Driller shall not pick up casing from the catwalk without full visibility
                        of the V-door area.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 40 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 320
                                  Policy:   RUNNING CASING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page4 of 5


                  6.1.5  When removing regular  drill pipe elevators from the links, lower the
                         elevators onto the rotary table once the elevator keeper bolts have been
                      removed. Spread bails, raise the block out of the way and remove elevators
                       with air hoist.

                  6.1.6  When starting to pick up casing, ensure casing collar and elevators do not
                        catch or snag on derrick beams or girts, as this shall allow the other end of
                         the casing to swing up in the air and come down.

          6.2     If casing shall be picked up from the V-door by means of single joint elevators,
                 ensure the proper size is used. On rigs so fitted with PSR and hydraulic catwalk,
                 primarily casing 20” or less in outside diameter will be picked up from the catwalk
                  via the pipe racking system.

                  6.2.1  Due to the many dropped casing joint incidents associated with side door
                          elevators, they shall not be used to pick up casing from a horizontal to a
                           vertical position without a risk assessment and approval process.

                  6.2.2  Once the drill pipe elevators have been set aside, the casing elevators can
                      be placed on the rotary table and lower the block to attach the links.

                  6.2.3  Keep water on the V-door and catwalk to help the casing slide.

          6.3   The movement of casing joints shall be controlled at all times. Casing joints shall
                 not be left unsecured in the V-door. There shall be either a “casing stop” installed
              on the catwalk or a line shall be attached to the joints.

          6.4    Casing thread protectors shall be removed from the drill floor.

                  6.4.1  Care shall be taken when they are transferred back to the catwalk. Throwing
                          protectors to the pipe deck is not allowed.

                  6.4.2   If casing dope is to get onto the deck, keep a brush and bucket of soap handy
                          to keep the walkway clean.

          6.5    Personnel working the drill floor shall be mindful of hand placement at all times
                while running casing.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 41 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 320
                                  Policy:   RUNNING CASING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 5 of 5


                  6.5.1  Floor men shall not place their hands on the casing threads or near the box
                        of the joint in the hole. The Driller shall not lower casing joints to the rotary
                       without full visibility.
                  6.5.2  The casing stabbing task shall be done as a team effort by those present
                      around the rotary. Hands shall not be placed on or around the pipe’s threads
                        or on top of the collar protruding from the casing spider.

          6.6   The Power Tong Operator shall not engage the tongs until such time as all hands
                  are clear of the joint. In the event that manual backup tongs are in use, the backup
                 tongs shall be applied and tensioned before the power tongs are engaged. Two
                 persons shall operate manual casing back up tongs.

                  6.6.1  Only the Casing Tong Operator shall operate the casing tong unless another
                       person is authorized or appointed to do so.
                  6.6.2  Before starting the casing job, the person operating the casing fill-up line
                           shall ensure the assembly is properly placed so it shall not interfere with
                         operations. If a shut off valve is being used, ensure a bleed off line is open
                    somewhere along the line, so you shall not “dead-end” the pump.
                  6.6.3  Due to the size of the backup tong, two individuals shall place it on the
                      lower joint of casing and make the tong bite before the power tong is
                       engaged; ensuring power tongs are not putting pressure on the arm of the
                      back up tongs.

          6.7   The Casing Stabber shall not pull the pin on the single joint elevators until the
                 threads have engaged. He shall ensure the elevators are securely latched before
                  signaling the Driller to pick up on the casing.

          6.8    Single joint elevators shall not be used in the place of a safety clamp above the
                 casing slips while the string is light.

   7.0   RECORDS
          7.1    Casing Tally and Running Order, on-site, current
          7.2     Lift equipment inspections, on-site, current
          7.3   Emergency Shutdown tests (during annual Class or flag State inspections), on-site,
               one year
          7.4     Pit Volume Totalizer monitoring records, on-site, current well

   8.0   REFERENCES

       HSE 100.16, Fall Protection

[ صفحه 42 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 330
                           Policy: HANDLING DRILL PIPE AND BHA
Revision: 04-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Lotfy
Issued: 17. July.2022
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 4


  HANDLING DRILL PIPE AND BHA

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to define the controls that are required to ensure the safe
          maintenance, handling, and use of drill pipe and bottom hole assembly (BHA components).

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

        The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher are individually
          responsible for implementing this procedure during their tour.

   4.0  GENERAL

          4.1   The movement of tubular goods shall be under control at all times. Care shall be
                 taken to safeguard personnel and equipment while handling, storing, and using
                  tubular goods.
          4.2    Correct rigging and slinging methods shall be applied at all times while handling
                  tubular goods. Ensure tubulars are double wrapped.
          4.3    Persons  loading,  offloading, and  transferring  tubular goods  shall not place
                themselves between  objects and suspended  loads. Ample  distance  shall be
                maintained from suspended loads to guarantee safety of the individual should the
                 load shift or swing for any reason.
          4.4    Tubular goods of different types, diameters, or lengths shall not be lifted in the
              same load.
          4.5    All loads shall be flagged by the dedicated Banksman, lifted, and landed as per
                 crane operations requirements (HSE 100.21).

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Storage)

          5.1   No tubular goods shall be stored in such a manner as to create an unsafe condition.
                Egress routes shall not be blocked, tripping hazards shall not be created, and
                   firefighting and emergency equipment shall not be blocked by stored tubular goods.
          5.2    All drill pipe, tubing, and Bottom Hole Assembly (BHA) components shall be
                equipped with thread protectors. Thread protectors shall be of hard plastic


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 43 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 330
                           Policy: HANDLING DRILL PIPE AND BHA
Revision: 04-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Lotfy
Issued: 17. July.2022
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 4


           construction. Thread protectors shall remain in place until such time that pipe, tubing, or
          BHA components are in use.

          5.3   Used drill pipe and BHA components shall be cleaned internally and externally
                 before being taken out of service for storage. Pins and boxes shall be thoroughly
                 cleaned and doped, and protectors put in place. If stored for an extended period,
                measures must be taken to protect them from deterioration due to exposure to the
                 elements.
          5.4    Pipe stored in pipe bays should not be stacked higher than the stanchion posts.
                Stanchions in pipe bays shall be inspected for corrosion / cracks at the base of the
                 stanchion prior to placing pipe in bay.
          5.5    Pipe racks shall be maintained in an orderly condition with joints placed so that an
                 equal amount of overlap is evident at both ends. Pipe stored on racks shall have
             wood stripping placed between tiers. Pipe shall be secured in place to prevent
                   rolling of pipe from racks:
                  5.5.1  Pipe with an outside (OD) less than seven inches (7”) should be securely
                       blocked with one-inch (1”) blocks nailed to the stripping.
                  5.5.2  Pipe with an OD of seven inches (7”) or greater should be securely blocked
                       with two-inch (2”) blocks nailed to the stripping.
          5.6   The space around pipe racks and bays must have no obstructions that might cause
                 personnel to stumble or fall as they load or unload pipe.

          5.7    Handling pipe on racks should be done from the side; standing on racked pipe is
                 highly discouraged.
   6.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Transporting)

          6.1    All personnel are directed to keep well clear of tubular goods in the process of being
                  transported. Use tag lines at all times, keep hands off loads, and stay out from under
                 crane loads.
          6.2    All tubular lifts shall be done in such a manner as to balance the load to as near a
                  horizontal plane as possible.
          6.3    Tubular goods shall be sent offshore in pre-wrapped bundles to prevent excessive
                exposure to individual lifts. The same is true for tubulars being offloaded from the
                mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU).
          6.4    pre-wrapped  bundles  of  tubular goods  shall have weights  calculated  with
                corresponding slings selected to remain within the safe working load of the slings.
                 Collars with an OD greater than 8” shall be handled individually.
          6.5    Individual joints of casing shall be handled with slings and tag lines. The use of
                 casing hooks is prohibited.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 44 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 330
                           Policy: HANDLING DRILL PIPE AND BHA
Revision: 04-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Lotfy
Issued: 17. July.2022
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 4


   7.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Transfers to Rig Floor)

          7.1   The floor service crane shall place all tubular goods on the catwalk for transfer to
                  the V-door. Transfer to the V-door shall be done using a floor utility hoist. For
          MODUs fitted with a raised catwalk machine or pipe conveyor, transfer of tubular
               goods shall be done by crane.
                  7.1.1  Thread protectors for drill pipe shall remain in place during transfer. Box
                     end thread protectors for drill collars shall be removed on the catwalk.
                  7.1.2  In all cases, personnel shall not approach the V-door, catwalk machine, or
                        load being lifted until such time that the load has been landed, tension is off
                         the air hoist line, and the landed load is stable.
          7.2     Drill collar shall be fitted with a color-coded lifting nubbin / cap while on the
                catwalk / V-door conveyor / catwalk machine.

                  7.2.1   Lifting caps should be certified for the weight they are to lift.

                  7.2.2 A chain tong shall be used to tighten the lifting cap into the collar. Each
                             lifting cap shall be “bumped up” with a hammer and chain tong.

                  7.2.3  Collar shall not be lifted more than one at a time into the V-door. For
               MODUs fitted with a catwalk machine, more than one collar can be landed
                            at one time, dependent on the load limitations of the machine itself.
                  7.2.4  Box end thread protectors shall be collected and stored for future use.

          7.3    Casing shall be transferred to the V-door or catwalk machine using a six- or eight-
                   part braided steel choker. The choker shall be double wrapped below the casing
                   collar and pulled tight before hoisting to V-door. Casing shall be transferred to the
               V-door one at a time. For MODUs with a catwalk machine only one joint of casing
                   shall be landed at any one time.

          7.4     Stabilizers, reamers, test tools, etc., shall be placed on the catwalk and transferred
                  to the rig floor as they are needed depending on the catwalk machine limitations, if
                     fitted. Rigging and lifting practices shall be observed as per HSE 100.20.

   8.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Makeup of Joints)

          8.1   Makeup of drill pipe joints and drill collars into the drill string and BHA shall be
               done utilizing the mouse hole. All tubular goods hoisted to the rig floor shall be
                   tailed with a tail rope. Tail ropes shall not be nylon rope


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 45 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 330
                           Policy: HANDLING DRILL PIPE AND BHA
Revision: 04-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Lotfy
Issued: 17. July.2022
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 4


                  8.1.1  For MODUs with the facility, tubular goods will be placed straight into the
                        top drive elevator or transferred to the mousehole via rig floor air winch or
                        pipe racker single joint elevator (SJE) as appropriate. Catwalk machines
                         incorporating a tailing arm do not require tubulars to be tailed in with rope.
          8.2     Drill pipe shall be transferred from the V-door to the mouse hole using lifting caps
                connected to either a shackle or positive locking hook. The hook or shackle
               assembly shall include a swivel connection. The pin thread protectors shall be
              removed prior to lowering into the mouse hole with the pipe still elevated.
                  8.2.1  For MODUs with the facility, this shall be done with the top drive elevator
                        or racking machine SJE or air winch. Pin thread protectors should remain
                          in place when the tubular is lowered into the mousehole to protect the
                          threads. If removed to recover a drift it should be replaced before lowering.
          8.3     Drill collars should be hoisted to the mouse hole using a shackle connected to the
                     lifting cap. The lifting assembly shall include a swivel connection. The lifting cap
                   shall be checked for tightness prior to hoisting to the mouse hole. The pin thread
                  protector shall be removed prior to lowering into the mouse hole with the collar still
                  elevated.
                  8.3.1  For MODUs with the facility this shall be done with the top drive elevator
                        or racking machine SJE or air winch. Pin thread protectors should remain
                          in place when the tubular is lowered into the mousehole to protect the
                          threads. If removed to recover a drift it should be replaced before lowering.
          8.4    Personnel shall remain clear of the area between the mousehole and the rotary table
                    at all times when mousehole connections are being made.
          8.5     It’s prohibited to use Stopper attached to Air tugger wire in Mouse hole while M/U
                 or B/O BHA, Safety Clamp and inspected C-plate can be used instead.
          8.6    At all times it is mandatory to use a safety clamp while M/U or B/O a BHA.
          8.7   Mouse hole should be equipped with BTM Spring.
          8.8   Mouse hole should be designed to withstand at least 200 % of the heaviest joint
                 used.
          8.9     It’s prohibited to start Spin-out thread until slack down the whole weight over
             Mouse hole Spring / Safety clamp.
          8.10  Mouse hole welding’s should be included in the bi-annual MPI inspection.

   9.0   RECORDS
        None

   10.0  REFERENCES

          10.1  HSE 100.20, Lifting Gear
          10.2  HSE 100.21, Mechanical / Hoisting Operations

  RACKING SMALL OD TUBULAR

   1.0   PURPOSE

          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
           operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       4.0 PROCEDURES

       4.1 A mid-level height support shall be provided when racking tribble stands of tubulars
          smaller than 3/" OD.

       4.2 Laying down tubular smaller than 3" OD on rigs without mid-level height support.

       4.3 The drill string shall be broken down to ensure that drill string components heavier than
           the per-foot-weight of the tubing are not located above a bottom joint of tubing in any stand
            that is racked back.

Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

  SELECTION AND USE OF THREAD COMPOUNDS, DOPING PRACTICES, AND MAKE-UP OF
  ROTARY-SHOULDERED CONNECTIONS

   1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide a standard for the selection, application, and use of thread compounds and
        make-up of rotary-shouldered connections on all tubular goods used on ADES rigs.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
      3.1 The OIM is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring that the make-up of connections,
         and the application and use of thread compounds specified in this standard comply with
          the requirements specified herein.

   4.0   PROCEDURES
      4.1 Whenever it is necessary to completely remove thread compound from connections, an is
           o-paraffinic solvent shall be used in accordance with. Never use petroleum solvents like
         Varsol or diesel. These products are not compatible with either Jet-Lube or Best-O-Life
          products and are not environmentally friendly. If iso-paraffinic solvents are not available,
         a degreaser product that is environmentally friendly shall be used.
      4.2 Connections shall be cleaned and doped while Running in the Hole (RIH). This will ensure
           that contamination of the thread compound (commonly called "dope") with drilling fluids
           will be minimized. Cleaning methods shall be devised on the rig such that all remaining
           traces of drilling fluid and solids are removed from the connection prior to the application
          of thread compound prior to make-up.
      4.3 Do not use dope that is contaminated with drilling fluid. Ensure the lid on the dope
          container in use on the rig floor is always secured onto the top of the container when it is
          not in use. When possible, slug pipe while Pulling Out of Hole (POOH). This will minimize
          the amount of drilling fluids that will be present on the connection when the stand is set
          back. This will also minimize corrosion of the exposed connection and will make cleaning
          the connection easier and more efficient when RIH. During wet trips, clean-off as much of
          the drilling fluid as possible that is on the pin connection as the stand is being set-back or
         immediately thereafter. This will save time when cleaning and doping the connection prior
           to make-up. Box connection at the top of the stand should be cleaned and doped while
      POOH to ensure box connection thread is ready in case while tripping in open hole


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 48 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                               Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                   Policy No. OPS 350
                             Policy: SELECTION AND USE OF THREAD COMPOUNDS,
                     DOPING PRACTICES, AND MAKE-UP OF
                    ROTARY-SHOULDERED CONNECTIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                            Page 2 of 6


          connecting TDS to circulate or back-ream a tight spot is required, it will also save even
        more time to clean and dope the connections while RIH.
      4.4 Thread compound shall be liberally applied to all surfaces of the threads and shoulders of
        box connections prior to make-up. For double-shouldered connections, such as the HT,
        XT, XTM, CTM, and DSTJ connections, special attention shall be paid to ensure that both
          shoulders of the pin and box connections are clean, and that dope is applied to box
          connection and shoulder. The primary cause of high break-out torque of these connections
             is the failure to properly clean and dope both shoulders of the pin and box.
      4.6 Connection breaks on drill pipe stands shall be rotated on all short and full round trips.

      4.7 For normal drilling conditions, drilling torque should not exceed 80% of drill pipe
        recommended make-up torque. Make-up torque of connections shall not exceed the
          specified maximum recommended make-up torque value. An exception to this rule is when
           actual drilling torque approaches 90% or exceeds the maximum recommended make-up
          torque value (there should be NO exceptions to this rule, it is the OPCO sole responsibility
           to select size and type of drill pipe / drill pipe connection based on the required drilling
          parameter, hence, expected torque generated by the formation reaction to type of drilling
            bit and string friction against hole low side, drilling torque approaches or exceeds drill pipe
        make up torque means that this type of connection requested by OPCO is not suitable for
          the operation- this paragraph needs to be revisited), or when it is proven that connections
          are making-up down hole while drilling. In these circumstances, make-up torque may be
          increased to a higher value by multiplying the designated friction factor of the thread
        compound by the maximum recommended make-up torque value (previous paragraph
         needs to be revisited by a tubular specialist, increasing drill pipe make-up torque above
        recommended by manufacturer is considered a bad oil field practice, as it might result in
          excessive pin stretch beyond thread alloy yield point causing pin weak twist-off) .In order
           to determine whether connections are making-up down hole, the following procedure shall
         be followed:
              4.7.1 While RIH, select at least 10 joints at random, and center-punch the pin and box
                   across the shoulder from each other after make-up, and record same in the pipe
                       tally.
              4.7.2 While POOH after drilling examines the connections that have been center-
                punched before they are broken. If the center-punch on the pin has moved to the
                        left of the center-punch on the box, then the connection has made-up further while
                      drilling. The best practice to determine if connections are making-up down hole,
                  connect tong-pull sensor to back up tong while POOH and monitor break-out
                    torque, if it exceeds 85% of used make-up torque, then the connection has made-
                up further while drilling


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 49 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                               Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                   Policy No. OPS 350
                             Policy: SELECTION AND USE OF THREAD COMPOUNDS,
                     DOPING PRACTICES, AND MAKE-UP OF
                    ROTARY-SHOULDERED CONNECTIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                            Page 3 of 6

              4.7.3 The selection of thread compounds specified below shall be based on drilling
                   condition severity. Moderate service is defined as drilling conditions where the
              maximum drilling torque is less than 80% of the maximum recommended make-
                up torque value. Severe

                  service is defined as drilling conditions where the maximum drilling torque is
                    greater than 80% of the maximum recommended make-up torque value.
              4.7.4 Thread compounds shall also be selected based on the type of drilling fluid in use.
                  This selection is primarily based on whether the drilling fluid in use is oil-based
                   or water-based. As such, the following thread compounds are approved for use
                on this basis:
                      •  For all drill pipe, drill pipe crossover subs, and heavy-weight drill pipe
                   (HWDP) with NC38, NC50, 5-1/2 FH, and 6-5/8 FH connections:
                                      •  Moderate Service: Jet-Lube 21
                                      •   Severe Service: Jet-Lube Kopr-Kote or Best-O-Life Copper
                                      •  Supreme Special Blend Note: For oil-based drilling fluids where
                                     there is a compatibility issue with the above thread compounds,
                                  Jet-Lube Extreme shall be used.
                      •  For all drill pipe and drill pipe crossover subs, and Top Drive main shaft
                        components  (IBOPs  and  saver  subs)  with  double-shouldered
                           connections such as HT, XT, XTM, CTM, or DSTJ connections:
                                      • Moderate Service: Jet-Lube Kopr-Kote
                                      • Severe Service: Jet-Lube Extreme
                      •  For  all  drill collars, BHA components, and Top Drive main shaft
                         assembly components (IBOPs and saver subs) that feature API rotary
                           shouldered connections such as NC38, NC46, NC50, 6-5/8 Reg, and 7-
                           5/8 Reg connections:

                                      •  Moderate Service: Best-O-Life Copper Supreme Special Blend
                                   or Jet-Lube Kopr-Kote.
                                      •   Severe Service: Jet-Lube Extreme or Jet-Lube Pow'r-Kote Note:
                                For oil-based drilling fluids, Jet-Lube Extreme shall be used.

              4.7.5 Compatibility between different thread compounds due to the difference in grease
                  bases used to make Jet-Lube and Best-O-Life thread compounds specified in this
                    standard, and the proprietary nature of their respective formulations, these
                  products shall not be considered as being compatible with each other unless
                 proven otherwise. Therefore, it is strongly recommended to standardize on the
                   thread compounds to be used on the rig, and any time a rig changes from Jet-Lube
                    to Best-O-Life, or vice versa for any reason, the old compound shall be


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 50 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                               Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                   Policy No. OPS 350
                             Policy: SELECTION AND USE OF THREAD COMPOUNDS,
                     DOPING PRACTICES, AND MAKE-UP OF
                    ROTARY-SHOULDERED CONNECTIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                            Page 4 of 6

                  completely removed from the rig, and all connections shall be cleaned prior to
                  applying the new compound. Iso-paraffinic solvents should be used to remove
                   thread compounds of all types from connections in accordance with Maintenance
                 Manual. Never use petroleum solvents like Varsol or diesel fuel to clean
                   connections.

            These products are not compatible with either Jet-Lube or Best-O-Life products and
                    are not environmentally friendly.

              4.7.6 Thread Compound Friction Factor API standards require each thread compound
                  manufacturer to designate each product with a "friction factor". The friction factor
                        is used as a multiplier against the recommended make-up torque value. For
                  example, the API recommended maximum make-up torque for a new NC50
                  connection that has a 6-5/8" OD x 3-1/4" ID is 31,025 ft.-lbs. If the designated
                      friction factor of a thread compound that is to be used on this connection is 1.0,
                  then no adjustment of the maximum recommended make-up torque value is
                   necessary. Alternatively, if the friction factor of the thread compound to be used
                        is 1.15, then this can multiplied against the recommended torque value to yield a
                   higher maximum value as shown below:
                                   31,025 ft.-lbs. x 1.15 = 35,679 ft.-lbs

                   Therefore, friction factors with a value that is higher than 1.0 will allow a higher
                make-up torque to be applied to the connection, and factors less then 1.0 will
                       restrict the make-up torque to a lower value. The application of the friction factors
                  over 1.0 should only be used in severe drilling conditions where actual drilling
                   torque is known to approach or exceed the normal maximum recommended
                make-up torque value, or it has been confirmed that drill string connections are
                making-up downhole while drilling. The friction factors of the thread compounds
                    specified in this standard are specified below:

                       Thread Compound    Friction Factor
                               Jet-Lube 21              1.15
                            Jet-Lube KoprKote          1.15
                            Jet-Lube PowrKote          1.15
                             Jet-Lube Extreme         1.15 (1)
                           Best-O-Life Copper          1.10
                         Supreme Special
                               Blend

[ صفحه 51 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                               Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                   Policy No. OPS 350
                             Policy: SELECTION AND USE OF THREAD COMPOUNDS,
                     DOPING PRACTICES, AND MAKE-UP OF
                    ROTARY-SHOULDERED CONNECTIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                            Page 5 of 6

    (1) The friction factor of Jet-Lube Extreme can be increased to 1.25 for some connections when
    encountering extremely severe drilling conditions. Please consult with the Mgr. Fleet Standards
                      before using this friction factor for this thread compound

              4.7.7 Thread Compound Selection Considerations with Oil-Based Drilling Fluids;

                  Oil-based muds can adversely affect the performance of thread compounds by
                   severely lowering the designated friction factor of the compound. In the past, this
                  has resulted in over-torqueing connections by as much as 30%, causing pin
                  connection fatigue failures

                  while drilling. The photos below show the fatigue failures that occurred to HT-
                55 connections:





              As of the date of revision of this standard, Jet-Lube Extreme has performed better
                  than other thread compounds with this type of drilling fluid. Tests can be
                 performed by the thread compound manufacturer to determine whether there are
                   compatibility issues or not. Regardless, the best way to ensure this does not occur
                        is to establish procedures on the rig floor to be followed by all drilling crews to
                  ensure that the connections are thoroughly cleaned of all drilling fluid before
                  applying thread compound while RIH.
              4.7.8 Corrosion Control
                 Thread and shoulder surface pitting is one of the leading causes of rejection of
                  connections during inspection. Part of this is caused by the corrosiveness of the
                      drilling fluids in use, such as water-based, polymer, high-chloride fluids that have


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 52 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                               Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                   Policy No. OPS 350
                             Policy: SELECTION AND USE OF THREAD COMPOUNDS,
                     DOPING PRACTICES, AND MAKE-UP OF
                    ROTARY-SHOULDERED CONNECTIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                            Page 6 of 6

   a neutral pH range. In addition, tubular inspection standards have become more stringent with
   regard to pitting of thread and shoulder surfaces. Unfortunately, this is causing more re-facing and
   re-cutting of the connections, which in turn, shortens the life-cycle of our tubular goods. Most
   corrosion occurs while the pipe is set-back or racked due to greater oxygen availability. The photos
  below depict the kind of corrosion that is caused by allowing the drilling fluid deposits to remain
  on the connection’s surfaces for too long:





               The best way to prevent corrosion during this time is to remove as much drilling
                     fluid as possible by washing the connection surfaces with fresh water after the
                   stand is set back and applying a preservative. Jet-Lube 769 is recommended as a
                    preservative to be used for this purpose. Jet-Lube 769 is a light oil that is
                  compatible with all Jet-Lube thread compounds that can be easily sprayed onto
                   the threads and shoulders. For pipe that is racked in horizontal pipe rackers while
             POOH, cleaning the connection and applying the preservative should be done
                    prior to fitting the thread protector just prior to racking the stand. While this will
                  impact trip times, it will prevent corrosion pitting, as well as the potential of a
                  connection washout while drilling. In addition, the time required to clean and
                dope the connection while RIH will be minimized.
              4.7.9 Both Best-O-Life and Jet-Lube products can be provided  for both high
                  temperature and low  temperature  environments  to meet  unusual  drilling
                    conditions.

        None

  REQUIREMENTS FOR HANDLING NITROGEN AND OTHER HAZARDOUS
  SUBSTANCES

   1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy, guidelines, and procedures for the handling, installation,
         and use of the systems listed that are temporarily installed on ADVANCED ENERGY
      SYSTEM Rig.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
      3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring compliance with this policy.
      3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible to Regional Management for ensuring that all appropriate
         Operator and third party personnel are aware of their responsibilities required by this policy
          as well as those referenced, and shall obtain written agreements to this effect.
      3.3 Third parties, who are contracted by the Operator for providing labor, equipment and
          materials to install the systems, shall be responsible for providing and installing the
         containments specified below as appropriate and to the satisfaction of the OIM. Such third
           parties are also responsible for providing written procedures and qualified personnel to
         perform their expected duties in an efficient and safe manner to prevent the hazards and
           risks identified as a result of the risk assessment required.

   4.0   PROCEDURES

      4.1 Prior to the installation of any system, the Rig Manager shall interface with the Operator
         and their third party who is supplying such equipment and systems for the purpose of
          obtaining detailed information, including but not be limited to:
              4.1.1 Size, weight (both full and empty), and foot print dimensions of each piece of
                 equipment that is to be founded on the rig
              4.1.2 Specifications of the hoses, piping, or chicksans that are intended to be used to
                 convey liquid N2, well stimulation fluids, or other hazardous media
              4.1.3 Specifications for the system intended to be used to control of the flow of
                  hazardous media
              4.1.4 Specifications for the instrumentation to be used to monitor pressure, temperature,
                 and flow of hazardous media


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 54 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                               Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                   Policy No. OPS 360
                                      Policy: REQUIREMENTS FOR HANDLING
                NITROGEN AND OTHER HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                            Page 2 of 7

              4.1.5 A written description of how the system is to be operated
              4.1.6 Written description of the safety features of the system, including but not limited
                    to pressure relieving devices, alarms and/or fail-safe shut-downs activated due to
                  exceeding pre-set maximum temperatures or pressures, etc.
              4.1.7 Specifications for any physical interface with rig systems or equipment, including
                 any required electrical power supplies, compressed air, fuel, water, etc.
      4.2 Upon receipt of the information outlined, the Rig Manager shall distribute same to the OIM
         with instructions to review such information. Management of Change MOC process to be
         used to determine the optimum location for each piece of equipment in the system in
         accordance with the policies specified in this standard.
      4.3 Once the location of the equipment has been determined, the equipment and the routing of
          interconnecting piping and electrical power and control cabling shall be laid out on a rig
          general arrangement drawing.
      4.4 Required supplies of rig electrical power, compressed air, fuel, and water shall be listed,
         along with the specifications of such interface, such as volts, amps, flow rates, etc.
          Specifications for such connections, such as piping, valves, and appurtenances, flanges or
        hammer unions for piping connections, hose size, type, and pressure rating, the size and
          type of electrical cabling to be used, designated circuit breakers for electrical power
           supplies, and any hazardous area compliance requirements.
      4.5 An assessment shall be made as to whether the installation of this equipment will
          temporarily impact the rig's Safety Plan, and whether temporary revisions to it are required
         based on, but not necessarily limited to the following:
              4.5.1 Revisions to access or egress routes for personnel that are required to respond to
                 an emergency or abandon the rig may be required. Note: If such revisions are not
                     satisfactory, or could result in confusion by personnel while reacting to an
                 emergency, the arrangement or location of equipment shall be revised to mitigate
                 any anticipated confusion or adverse conditions. Any modified egress or access
                    routes shall be shown on drawings that shall be posted next to the rig's Safety
                  Plan
                     4.5.2 Firefighting and lifesaving equipment and systems shall be reviewed to
                   determine whether the temporary installation of the equipment will prevent or
                 impede the use of such systems in an emergency. If this is determined, the
                  proposed location or arrangement of such equipment shall be revised to ensure
                      that the  rig's lifesaving and  firefighting equipment may be used without
                       restriction. In the event this  is not possible, supplementary lifesaving and
                      firefighting equipment may have to be added to the rig during the time such
                    temporarily installed equipment and systems is in use.

[ صفحه 55 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                              Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                                Policy No. OPS 360
                                    Policy: REQUIREMENTS FOR HANDLING
                NITROGEN AND OTHER HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

             4.5.3 Where water outlets are not available in the immediate area for use in dealing
                 with spillage of liquid N2 or other hazardous media, arrangements shall be
                   specified for the addition of required outlets and hoses as required.
             4.5.4 Rig structural interface with the systems shall be reviewed including but not
                  limited to supporting the load of equipment, and securing it in a manner such that
                such support will be adequate. "Adequate" in this sense means that the design of
                  foundations, tie-downs, and other restraints shall take into account the static
                weight plus the dynamic forces induced by rig survival conditions specified in
                  the rig's Operating Manual, or other dynamic forces that could be reasonably
                   anticipated.
     4.6 In the event that ADES rig or shore-based personnel are not qualified or able to perform
         the technical reviews specified above, qualified third parties shall be employed to perform
        such reviews, and issue a report containing installation specifications that meet the intent
         of the requirements set forth herein.
     4.7 If a qualified third party is contracted to perform such technical reviews, a copy of their
         report shall be sent to ADES Maintenance Dept. It is important to take into consideration
          that a minimum of 30 days is required for a proper engineering review to be performed. If
          insufficient notice is received from an Operator of his intent to install any system, the Vice
         President, Operations shall be notified immediately for resolution.
     4.8 A meeting shall be arranged between the appropriate ADES, Operator, and third-party
         representatives to review the requirements for installation, testing, and commissioning of
         the system. Any modifications to the installation plan shall require technical review to
        ensure that all applicable rules and regulations are met, along with any requirements issued
       by reviewing qualified third parties.
     4.9 Once a final plan for installation is mutually agreed between ADES, the Operator, and the
          third party provider of the equipment and systems to be temporarily installed, risk
        assessments shall be carried out in accordance with the requirements and the procedures
         specified in HSE Manual such risk assessments shall be performed against a selection of
        equipment or system failure scenarios. The selection of such scenarios shall be based on
         the probability of occurrence, along with failure consequences. Examples of such scenarios
          shall include but are not necessarily limited to the following:
                 •   Spillage on deck or overboard
                 •  Human contact with spillage
                 •  Lack of oxygen in enclosed spaces or areas with poor ventilation

         Policies for mitigating actions and required drills for the above risks are outlined in this
         standard. Other actions identified to prevent, mitigate, and respond to other failure
         scenarios identified as a result of the actual risk assessments carried out shall be defined
        with personnel responsibilities identified. All training required for personnel assigned to
        any actions prescribed to help prevent, mitigate, or respond to equipment or system failures
          shall also be identified and carried out prior to system installation.

                                    Policy: REQUIREMENTS FOR HANDLING
                NITROGEN AND OTHER HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES
Revision: 01-00                                                       Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

     4.10 Drills required by this standard shall be implemented on or before the commissioning of
        any equipment or systems.
     4.11 Before testing and commissioning any systems, all rig personnel and third parties shall be
         instructed on the procedures, precautions, and hazards involved with handling, installing
        and operating the system installed. For example, if the system stores or conveys cryogenic
         liquid N2, personnel shall be instructed of the following:
            4.11.1 The temperature of liquid N2 at atmospheric pressure is -196° C (-385 deg. F),
                and any physical contact with liquid N2 will cause permanent injury.
            4.11.2 Contact between liquid N2 and steel structures will most likely cause damage to
                     steel materials due to the rapid cooling effect of such exposure.
            4.11.3 To avoid an oxygen-deficient atmosphere, intentional nitrogen venting shall
                  only take place in a well-ventilated area.
            4.11.4 Where spillage of liquid N2 takes place accidentally, personnel in the vicinity
                     shall evacuate immediately to a safe area, and shall wait for Emergency Team
                  respondents to mitigate the hazards and declare the area safe before re-entering
                   the area.
     4.12 The location of system tanks, hoses, chicksans, and piping on the rig shall be carefully
          selected in accordance with the following guidelines:

            4.12.1 The length of hose, chicksans, and piping shall be kept to a minimum
            4.12.2 Areas subject to being below routine crane lifts shall be avoided, based on the
                  requirements in accordance with below
            4.12.3 Areas subject to the spillage of acids or liquid N2 shall be conducive to the
                   erection of protective coverage of rig structure and equipment in accordance
                  with below

            4.12.4 The area around tanks, hoses, chicksans, and piping that are used to store or
           convey acids, liquid N2, or other hazardous media shall be such that it allows it to be
             effectively and quickly isolated from casual entry by the barriers required by below
            4.12.5 The areas where tanks, hoses, chicksans, and piping that are used to store or
                convey acids, liquid N2, or other hazardous media are located shall be have an
                  adequate water supply to allow pressurized hoses to be provided for immediate
                  use in accordance with below
            4.12.6 The area selected for installation shall be as remote as possible to avoid
                 encroachment by casual personnel traffic

     4.13 The area beneath all tanks, hoses, chicksans, and piping that convey acids, liquid N2, and
          other hazardous media shall be prepared to contain any spillage of such media as follows:
            4.13.1 Cover the steel deck with timber (scaffold boards)
            4.13.2 Build up an outer perimeter of at least 4 inches in height with scaffold boards.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 57 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                              Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                                    Policy: REQUIREMENTS FOR HANDLING

            4.13.3 Drape heavy duty, water-tight tarpaulin material or sheeting, such as Visqueen™
                    (or equivalent) over the entire area to create a watertight barrier. Scaffold boards
              may be placed inside the perimeter to secure the water-tight tarp or sheeting
                   material in position and to prevent chafing or damage of the tarp or sheeting.
            4.13.4 material that could come into contact with equipment skids or other structure or
                 equipment with sharp edges
            4.13.5 Fill the containment area with water to a depth of 2 to3 inches to provide a means
                   of diluting acids or other hazardous media, and to provide a heat sink for spilled
                    liquid N2.
              NOTE: The above are minimum requirements. Other methods of protection
              may be utilized, but they shall be reviewed and approved on a technical basis
                by the party responsible for conducting such reviews.
     4.14 A charged water hose or fire hose shall be provided for immediate use by personnel in
          case of spillage of acids, liquid N2, or other hazardous media. All personnel, including
           third parties, who are assigned the responsibility for using such water hoses in the event
          of a spill shall receive prior instructions on what Personnel Protective Equipment (PPE)
          to wear, and how to use such water
         hoses in such a manner as to avoid spreading spilled hazardous media to other areas.

     4.15 Barriers with appropriate signs shall be erected and maintained around equipment and
         systems that contain hazardous media to prevent casual entry of non-essential personnel
        from encroaching or entering into these areas.
     4.16 For each crane lift that must be performed above pressurized tanks, piping, hoses, or
         chicksans containing hazardous media, the OIM shall ensure the following requirements
          are met before the lift is attempted:

            4.16.1 A specific lift plan shall be generated that includes a Job Risk Assessment (JRA)
            4.16.2 A Permit to Work (PTW) shall be issued only after the lift plan and JRA is
                 reviewed and approved by the OIM
                   In instances where there are a series of uninterrupted lifts that are intended to be
                    carried out over tanks, piping, hoses, or chicksans containing hazardous media,
                        all of the lifts may be performed under the same JRA / PTW. If, during the course
                   of conducting such lifts, environmental conditions change, or there is a work
                      shift change where different personnel are going to be involved in performing
                   the remaining lifts, the OIM shall ensure that a tool box meeting is held with all
                    participating personnel, and that the JRA is reviewed and revised as required,
                and a new PTW is issued when any changes to the original lift plan or JRA is
                made.
     4.17 All tanks that are to be used for containing hazardous media that are to be taken onboard

     4.18 Rig shall be fitted with a certified, multi-part sling that is rated in excess of the total weight
          of the tank when it is filled with the hazardous media intended. Such multi-part slings
           shall be fitted with a master link and shall be rated for leg angles up to 45 degrees. The
          slings shall remain fitted to the tank during the entire time it is onboard the rig. The slings
           shall be of sufficient length to allow ready and safe access to the master link for the
         purpose of engaging it for lifting the tank with a crane. In the event of leakage of hazardous
        media from the tank, the multi-part sling will allow the tank to be quickly and efficiently
            lifted with a rig crane and suspended overboard for whatever period of time is required if
         such action is deemed necessary by the OIM.
            4.18.1 Chicksans, hoses, or piping used for conveying acids, liquid N2, or other
                 hazardous media shall be provided by the customer in all cases, and shall be
                    pressure-tested after installation up to the working pressure of the system as a
                    part of commissioning the system prior to actual use. The preferred test media
                    for all hoses, chicksans, and piping shall be fresh water. ADES shall not supply
                   hoses, chicksans, or piping used to convey such hazardous media, nor shall any
                  piping systems or hoses that are a part of a permanent installation on the rig be
                 used for the conveyance of such hazardous media.
            4.18.2 Rotary hoses made to meet API Specification 7K shall not be used during well
                    stimulation, under-balance drilling, or any other type of operation that would
                 expose
                      this hose to pressurized gases of any kind. If rotary hoses are going to be exposed
                    to any pressurized gases during these kinds of operations, they shall be
                 manufactured in accordance with API Specification 17B. Such hoses shall in all
                   cases be provided by the customer.
            4.18.3 Emergency drills shall be developed and performed by appropriate personnel at
                    intervals to be designated by the OIM. Such  drills shall include, but not
                   necessarily  be  limited  to  the  scenarios  itemized  in  this  standard. The
                 development of these drills shall, as a minimum, take into account the following:

                   4.18.3.1 Method by which notification of spillage of hazardous media shall be
                             established, such as sounding of the General Alarm and/or making an
                       announcement on the public address system, such as, "A liquid nitrogen
                                spill has occurred  at  (location). All personnel  shall immediately
                          evacuate this area immediately. All emergency team members please
                            report to their designated muster area for further instructions."
                   4.18.3.2 Emergency Team muster areas shall be specified, along with the storage
                            location of all required equipment and materials required to deal with
                           the hazards identified in all of the failure scenarios revealed as a result
                           of completing the risk

                    4.18.3.3 Assessments required by this standard, including all required personal
                             protective equipment.
                    4.18.3.4 Emergency team members  shall be assigned  responsibilities  for
                             delivering  all equipment that  is required to deal with the hazards
                               identified for all failure scenarios, including its proper use.
                    4.18.3.5 Standby instructions shall be developed, written, communicated, and
                              verified
                         During drills to ensure that all personnel clear the area, that non-

                        emergency team members demonstrate  their  ability to respond in
                          accordance with previously established procedures.
                    4.18.3.6 Emergency Team Leaders shall ensure that a person is assigned the
                              responsibility for checking oxygen levels at the site to ensure they are
                           within  safe  limits. An example of equipment used  for sampling
                          atmospheres for oxygen levels. Precautions shall be identified and taken
                               that are appropriate  for  all of the hazards  identified  in the  risk
                          assessment or encountered. For example, personnel assigned the task of
                           applying a water deluge onto liquid

              N2 spills shall wear self-contained breathing apparatus to mitigate the risk of
                    asphyxiation due to displacement of oxygen by N2 gas.

              4.18.4 It should be noted that stacking tanks that are intended to contain hazardous
                 media on top of each other onboard the rig is against local laws due to past
                      failures of tie-downs. This should not be done on any other ADES rig for the
                same reason, regardless of the rig's location, notwithstanding local laws to the
                     contrary. However, if stacking tanks is absolutely required due to lack of deck
                    space, an assessment of the method of tie-downs or other means of securement
                      shall be carried out as a part of the technical review process. The adequacy of
                   design of such tie-downs and securement shall be based on withstanding rig
                     survival mode conditions In addition, technical review of stacked tanks and
                  equipment shall include an assessment of the means of access to all components
                    required to operate, inspect, test, and maintain the equipment.

  WORKING IN THE DERRICK

   1.0   PURPOSE

        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines when personnel are working in the
           derrick.

   2.0   SCOPE

          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
           operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) is responsible for implementing this
                 procedure during their tour.

          3.2   The Driller is responsible for personnel working in the derrick.

   4.0  GENERAL

          4.1    All work done in the derrick  shall be done with observance to  fall  arrest
                 requirements as detailed in policy HSE 100.16.
          4.2    All work done in the derrick requiring personnel hoisting shall be done with
                observance to the policies covering man riding operations: HSE 100.29.
          4.3  A risk assessment shall be completed when it is contemplated to work in the derrick
                   in times of threatening weather (thunder, lightning, and windstorms) when within
               one mile of the mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU). If so, a permit to work shall
                be completed.
          4.4    During drilling and tripping operations a simultaneous operations Job Safety
                 Analysis and Permit to Work shall be carried out prior to commencing derrick
                   inspections, dropped object surveys, or any other remedial work in the derrick.
          4.5    All tools and equipment carried into the derrick shall be secured by means of a
                   safety line, covered pouch, or other means to prevent loss of control and possible
                 dropping.
          4.6    Depending on the nature of the task to be performed in the derrick (potential for
                dropped objects) the drill floor shall be barricaded.
                  4.6.1  Signs shall be placed at drill floor access points.
                  4.6.2  An announcement shall be made over the public address system of persons
                       ascending the derrick and further announcement when the task aloft has
                      been completed and personnel have descended to the floor


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 61 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 400
                                    Policy: WORKING IN THE DERRICK
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2of 3


          4.7   The Driller shall maintain a logbook of each individual tool, item, or piece of
         equipment taken into the derrick. Individuals intending to ascend into the derrick shall fill
          the log book. The Driller and the individual shall sign off on each entry.

          4.8    Equipment installed in the derrick shall be entered into the Derrick Log. The derrick
                 log book shall be maintained in the Driller’s doghouse.

          4.9    There  shall be no hot work done  in the derrick without approval through
              Management of Change (MOC) (ADM 015).

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Monkey Board)

          5.1    Derrickmen trainees shall have an orientation period in which they work with the
                  regular experienced Derrickman until such time that they, the Derrickman and the
                     driller are confidant the prospective Derrickman can competently fulfill the job
                 functions of the position of Derrickman through the Competency Assurance
                program.

          5.2   The Derrickman’s safety belt shall remain at the entrance to the monkey board
             when not in use. The Derrickman shall don the safety belt before going onto the
             monkey board and shall not remove it until he is exiting the monkey board.

          5.3   The monkey board shall be equipped with a hands-free communication system
                allowing communication between the Driller and the Derrickman.

          5.4   The monkey board shall always remain visible to the Driller either through direct
                   line-of-sight or closed-circuit television (CCTV) monitor.

          5.5    During crew change, as the Derrickmen are handing over. The Driller shall not
             move the blocks from  their lowest position  until such time  that the  relief
               Derrickman is prepared to commence work.

          5.6   The monkey board shall be equipped with a pneumatic winch which shall be used
               and operated remotely by the Derrickman when pulling or running Bottom Hole
              Assembly (BHA). Sheaves shall be properly secured to the monkey board.

          5.7    Individual stands of drill pipe shall be secured in the monkey board either with rope
                 or closing gates.

          5.8  BHA components shall be secured in the monkey board by closing gates.


────────────────────────────────────────────

          5.9   The monkey board shall be kept free of clutter and any potential dropped objects.

  7 RECORDS

          6.1    Derrick Logbook, on-site, one year
          6.2    Dropped Objects survey, on-site, two years

          7.5   HSE 100.23, Dropped Objects Prevention
          7.6   HSE 100.29, Manriding Operations
