  DERRICK TUGGER SHEAVE

   1.0   PURPOSE

         This procedure describes Derrick Tugger Sheave requirements of ADVANCED
      ENERGY SYSTEM to provide inspection and maintenance policies, procedures, and
          guidelines for derrick sheave blocks.

   2.0   SCOPE

         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

          3.1   The OIM is responsible to the Rig Manager to ensure:

              3.1.1 The senior Mechanic is aware of this standard.

              3.1.2 Job Risk Assessments (JRAs) are developed, reviewed and, completed by all
                    participants prior to carrying out the inspection and maintenance procedures
                    specified in this standard.

          3.2   The senior Mechanic is responsible to the OIM to ensure that the maintenance and
                  inspection requirements of this standard are carried out, and that all personnel
                 assigned to perform such maintenance and inspections are supervised and
                  instructed in the proper procedures.

   4.0   PROCEDURES

          4.1 Visual inspections shall be performed every 6 months to coincide with lifting gear
          inspections as required by ADES Standard to verify the following:

              4.1.1 All retaining devices, such as split pins (cotter keys), lock-wire, etc. shall be
                    visibly in place, and intact.
              4.1.2 Sheaves shall turn freely without binding, and show no signs of wire cutting
              4.1.3 Cheek plates shall not show signs of wire cutting and shall not be deformed.
              4.1.4 All fasteners shall have visible means of retainment with lock wire, R-clips, etc.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 64 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 402
                                    Policy: DERRICK TUGGER SHEAVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 3

              4.1.5 Sheave block swivel mechanisms shall turn freely without binding. If separate
                   swivels are employed, such as the Crosby S Type Swivel, they shall also turn
                     freely without binding.
              4.1.6 The primary means of attachment between the derrick sheave block and the
                    derrick structure shall not be visibly deformed, or show other signs of damage or
                    deterioration that would threaten the holding capacity of the attachment and
                 hardware
              4.1.7 The safety hobble sling and all attachments shall show no visible signs of damage
                   or deterioration, and all fasteners, shackle pins, etc. shall have a visible means of
                   retainment, such as lock wire, split pins, or R-clips.

              4.1.8 Whenever discrepancies are discovered during the course of visual inspections,
                   the derrick tugger sheave shall be repaired or replaced.
      4.2 Lubrication of the sheave bearing and derrick tugger sheave swivel feature shall be
         performed every month to coincide with the visual inspections specified above.
      4.3 All derrick tugger sheaves and safety hobble slings shall be inspected and replace as
          required with spare units that are in serviceable condition every 2 years. The schedule for
            this inspection shall be established in PMS system.
          Derrick tugger sheaves that are removed from service shall be completely disassembled for
           inspection. Components that are found to be worn, damaged, or deteriorated shall be
          replaced with new components. All bearings and sheave pin locknuts shall be replaced
          regardless of condition. All components that pass the initial visual inspection shall be
         examined via wet magnetic particle inspection (MPI). Any component found with any
          cracks of any size shall be rejected and disposed of, and replaced with new OEM
         components. Dry MPI may be used in lieu of wet MPI if the correct materials are not
          available to perform wet MPI. For non-magnetic components, a dye penetrant inspection
           shall be performed.
      4.4 All components rejected from further service shall be destroyed and disposed of and
          replaced by new components provided by the original manufacturer.

      4.5 Repairs to components by welding, brazing, or burning are prohibited. Heating bearings
           for a shrink fit is permitted as long as the temperature specified by the derrick tugger sheave
      OEM or the bearing manufacturer is not exceeded.

      4.6 All derrick tugger sheaves that do not comply shall be replaced with those specified in that
          standard when the bi-annual disassembly inspection specified above of this standard is
          performed.

      4.7 Existing safety hobbles shall also be replaced with those that comply with API Standard,
          including the pad-eye attachment to the derrick if it has a Safe Working Load (SWL) that


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 65 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 402
                                    Policy: DERRICK TUGGER SHEAVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 3

             is less than 3 tons. Any new or replacement pad-eyes required to attach the safety sling to
          the derrick will be installed and logged in the preventive Maintenance System PMS.

      4.8 The primary means of attachment between the derrick tugger sheave and the derrick shall
         have already been evaluated to ensure that it has a SWL that is at least twice the stall pull
           rating of the tugger/hoist at the first wrap on the drum. If the SWL of the derrick attachment
             is less than this, it shall be replaced.

      4.1 "Snubby" breakaway devices shall be inspected monthly if the condition of the breakaway
          devices is discovered to be not acceptable in accordance with these procedures, the device
           shall be replaced. If inspection reveals that the device has been pulled out of its sheath and
          the orange flags are showing, it shall be replaced.

   5.0   PROCESS AND DEFINITIONS

          Derrick sheave blocks covered by this standard are defined below:
                 •  Rig floor tugger sheave blocks — These are used with rig floor tuggers that
                       are not designated as man-riding tuggers.
                 •  Man-riding tugger sheave blocks — These are used exclusively with man-
                        riding tuggers installed on the rig floor.
                 •  Tong hang-off sheave blocks — These are used to hang-off tongs installed on
                       the rig floor.

   5.0   RECORDS

        None

   6.0   REFERENCES

        None

                                      Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS

  TRIPPING OPERATIONS
   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to define standards for tripping operations.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher are individually
                 responsible for implementing this procedure during their tour.

          3.2   The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of tripping
                    activities on the rig floor.

   4.0  PROCEDURE (General)
          4.1   On completion  of  drilling  operations,  prior  to commencement  of  tripping
                 operations all alarms and limits must be set and verified on the pit volume totalizer
              (PVT) and Flowmeter. The anti-collision system and the upper and lower limits
                   shall be verified against the crown saver toggle.

          4.2   The order to begin pulling out of the hole shall come from the OIM / Tool pusher
                only after the Tool pusher has gone to the rig floor and verified that all necessary
                 preparations have been made. The OIM / Tool pusher shall remain on the rig floor
                  in a supervisory position until such time that:

                  4.2.1  Flow check has been made

                  4.2.2  Slug has been pumped

                  4.2.3   First five stands have been pulled and hole has taken proper volume of mud.
          4.3   The Driller shall receive a written trip program previous to tripping operation.
          4.4   The Driller must have clear, written instructions as to the method of well control
                procedures to  initiate in the event of a well control situation. Well control
                  instructions shall be posted in the dog house.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 67 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 405
                                      Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 8


          4.5   Any time the process of tripping pipe is stopped / interrupted, an inside blowout
                 preventer (BOP) / fully opening safety valve (FOSV) shall be made up to the string.
          4.6    Alarms shall be checked and operating during tripping operations.
          4.7  A wiper rubber should be used when tripping out of the hole.
          4.8    Pre-job safety meetings shall be conducted during the evolutions of tripping
                  operations.
          4.9   Manual handling and lifting of tools and equipment shall be kept to a minimum
                through the use of hoists. Proper lifting practices shall be used.
          4.10  The Driller shall operate at a reasonable and practicable pace giving consideration
                  to the following parameter.
                 4.10.1 The abilities of the crew
                 4.10.2 Downhole conditions
                 4.10.3 Safety of personnel and equipment
          4.11  New drill pipe strings shall be “broken in” (make and break) either during the
                manufacturing process or prior to use. All tubulars in the drill string shall have
             make up torque applied in accordance with American Petroleum Institute (API)
             Recommended Practice (RP) 7G, or manufacturer’s recommendations.
          4.12   Torque measuring equipment shall be in good working order and calibrated yearly.
              The maximum allowable make up torque and maximum pull in the case of stuck
                      drill string in the hole shall be governed by size, type, and grade of pipe.
          4.13  The trip tank with continuous fill is the preferred method during tripping operations
                  to ensure that fluid in the hole is monitored.
                 4.13.1 The annulus shall be filled with fluid before the change in mud level
                        decreases the hydrostatic pressure by 75 pounds per square inch (psi) (500
                         kilopascals [kPa] or 5 bar) or every five (5) stands of drill pipe, three (3)
                        stands of heavy-weight drill pipe (HWDP), or one (1) stand of drill collars.
                 4.13.2 The pit from which the hole is being filled shall be isolated from all other
                            pits and the volume be closely monitored.

          4.14  ADES approved thread compounds shall be utilized, that meet the performance
                 requirements of American Petroleum Institute (API) Bulletin 7A1, for rotary
                shouldered
                 connections is a minimum company standard. Original equipment manufacturer
            (OEM) specifications are to be considered.
          4.15  The Driller shall only pull the drill string over the 80% posted maximum allowable
                 values under the direct supervision of the OIM / Tool pusher (in consultation with
                  the Rig Manager) up to a maximum of 90% of the API RP 7G used tubular
            maximum allowable pull table. OEM tubular specifications are to be considered.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 68 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 405
                                      Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 8

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Well Control Considerations)

          5.1    Before the Driller begins to pull the string, the mud must to be in good condition
                   for the trip. This normally requires that the mud volume of the well be circulated
                bottoms up to clean the hole.
          5.2    There must be no sign of formation flow into the well bore.
          5.3    There shall be no sign of fluid loss to the formation.
          5.4   Mud weight variance in and out must be within acceptable limits of the Application
                   for Permit to Drill (APD) as approved by the regulatory body.
          5.5    There must be a Full Open Safety Valve (stabbing valve) in open position with the
                 closing wrench available on the floor. Necessary crossovers for installation on all
                 threads down hole must be on the floor clean and ready.
          5.6   The trip tank must be properly routed and filled. The drill string shall remain
                  stationary while filling / emptying the trip tank during tripping operations.
          5.7   The PVT must be working, with loss and gain values set; the alarm must be enabled.
          5.8    Prerecorded well information must be updated to the present situation.
          5.9  A trip sheet must be ready with calculated mud volumes entered. Mud volume taken
                   shall be monitored every five stands or at predetermined hydrostatic head reduction
                  values.
          5.10  Flow checks shall be made at predetermined intervals and as necessary to monitor
               and verify well conditions during a trip.

   6.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Equipment)

          6.1   The necessary tools and equipment for completing a trip for any reason must be
                ready and available to the drill crew.

          6.2   Among the items for consideration by the Driller are:

                  6.2.1  The crown-o-matic must be function tested;
                  6.2.2  The drawworks brakes must be checked and adjusted, if necessary.
                  6.2.3  The weight indicator must be functioning properly.
                  6.2.4  Tongs, slips, and elevators must be inspected and prepared.
                  6.2.5  The derrick and crown must be visually checked for loose items.
                  6.2.6  Safety lines, air hoists and lines must be checked.
                  6.2.7  The mud bucket seal should be in good condition and must be routed back
                          to the bell nipple or flowline. The trip tank must be lined up to empty into
                         the active mud system.
                  6.2.8 A wiper rubber must be available.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 69 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 405
                                      Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 4 of 8

                  6.2.9  Ample tool joint compound for drill pipe and collar joints shall be available
                    on the floor.
                 6.2.10 All lifting subs and crossovers must be color-coded, clean, and prepared for
                          use.
          6.3    Attention and risk assessment (Job Safety Analysis) shall be used during tripping,
                   particularly during nighttime and adverse weather conditions.

          6.4    Ensure that the rig floor is clear of obstructions.

          6.5   Use adequate matting to provide safe footing.

          6.6   Check all equipment, slips, tongs, elevator, etc., to ensure they are in proper
               working order before each trip, and clean and prepare for the next trip.

          6.7   Keep rotary bushing locked while drilling / tripping.

          6.8    Constantly monitor pump strokes, pit volume, trip tank, and mud weight to detect
                anything unusual or out of the ordinary while tripping.

          6.9    Secure the Driller’s mechanical brake in such a way to prevent the drawworks drum
               from turning whenever the Driller’s position is left unattended, e.g., chain for
               manual brakes.

                  6.9.1  For Cyber MODUs, the Driller is to apply the park brake when out of the
                       cyber chair and confirm it is functioned on the screen prior to leaving his
                           post.

   7.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Documentation)

          7.1   The process of the trip shall be recorded as required.

          7.2   Among the considerations for documentation on the rig floor are:

                  7.2.1 A pipe tally must be updated and available.

                  7.2.2 A trip sheet must be prepared with calculated pipe displacement values. A
                             trip sheet shall be kept for a record of displacement every five (5) stands.

                  7.2.3  Prerecorded well control data entered on kill sheet or otherwise posted in
                    dog house.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 70 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 405
                                      Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                 Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 5 of 8

                  7.2.4  Trip instructions (program) available.

                  7.2.5  The geolograph and / or other recording instruments must be functioning.

   8.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Tripping Out of the Hole)

          8.1    Individual stands of drill pipe shall be numbered by the drill crew as they are set
                 back. Progress of trip shall be noted on pipe tally.

          8.2   The Driller shall document drag and sticking as pipe is being pulled; any events of
                  over-pull shall be documented in the Driller’s Log. Drill pipe stands shall be
              marked to indicate drag or sticking as they are set back.

          8.3   Flow checks: As a minimum, the Driller shall perform flow checks:

                  8.3.1   Just off bottom, previous to pumping slug

                  8.3.2  At the lowest casing point

                  8.3.3  Previous to pulling Bottom Hole Assembly (BHA) through the BOPs

          8.4   The Driller shall monitor the volume of mud taken to replace displaced pipe volume
                    at all times. In the event the hole is not taking the calculated amount of mud, the
                   Driller shall inform the OIM / Tool pusher, perform a flow check and report results
                  to the Rig Manager.

          8.5   The trip tank pump must be running, keeping the hole full at all times pipe is being
                  pulled. Actual volume of mud taken shall be documented and compared to
                  calculated mud volume required to replace displaced pipe volume. As pipe is being
                 pulled from the hole

          8.6    During tripping operations the drill floor shall be kept clear of obstacles. Tools shall
               be returned to storage when no longer in use. The floor shall be washed down as
                 necessary to prevent slippery walking surface hazard. Care shall be taken to keep
                 area around rotary clear of any objects that may fall through the bushings.

          8.7   Upon pulling the first collar to the surface, a flow check shall be made. A pre-job
                meeting for pulling the BHA shall take place among the drill crew. A Job Safety
                Analysis for handling BHA shall be prepared and discussed.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 71 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 405
                                      Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 6 of 8

          8.8  BHA components shall not be pulled into derrick or set back with gumbo, clay, or
                 other formation material caked onto blades, rollers, bodies of components. Such
                  material shall be cleaned from components before being hoisted. The safety clamp
                   shall not be left on a joint of collar and hoisted into the derrick. Attention shall be
                 taken to prevent any incident of falling objects.

   9.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Pipe Out of the Hole)

          9.1   The trip tank pump shall be left running, circulating across the bell nipple while out
                 of the hole. Volumes shall be monitored at all times.

          9.2   The hole shall remain covered with a proper cover, designed for purpose, while out
                 of the hole. The mouse hole shall remain covered at all times it is not in use. Keep
                  the hole covered when there is no drill string, casing, or wireline in the hole.

          9.3   The shuck shall not be used when the mousehole (length of casing) is not in place.
                      If the mouse hole shuck is to be used for operations other than temporarily storing
                      drill pipe (e.g. hoisting, running chicksan lines, hanging off wireline tools, etc.), the
                 job shall be risk assessed to consider the potential for a dropped object.

          9.4    All tools and equipment no longer required for continuing operations shall be
                 cleaned and stored. The rig floor shall be washed down and prepared for continued
                  operations.

          9.5  A pre-job safety meeting shall be conducted with Job Safety Analyses completed
                   for any operation or tasks that are to be performed while out of the hole. In the event
                 intended operations are conducted by third-parties, the lead supervisor of the third-
                  party operation  shall conduct  the pre-job  safety meeting with  all involved
                 personnel.

   10.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Tripping in the Hole)

          10.1   Previous to tripping the drill string back into the hole, the Drill Crew shall convene
                   for a pre-job safety meeting to discuss and plan the trip back in. Job Safety Analyses
                   shall be completed for handling of BHA components. Upon completion of BHA
                running a pre-job safety meeting shall be held, the floor shall be prepared for
                running drill pipe.

          10.2  The trip tank pump shall remain in operation during the trip back in. The volume
                 of mud being received from the hole must be monitored.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 72 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 405
                                       Policy: TRIPPING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 7 of 8


          10.3    If a float is above the bit, it shall be necessary to fill the drill string at 15-stand
                    intervals, to prevent differential sticking of the drill string, reciprocation may be
                  necessary. If the Operator requires fewer fills, the drill string shall be filled at not
              more than 25-stand intervals.

          10.4  The Driller shall be aware of any notations indicating tight spots in the hole.
               Running pipe back through these areas shall be done so cautiously.

          10.5   Depending upon hole conditions, a bottoms-up circulation should be performed to
               remove background gas and prepare the well for further down-hole operations.

   11.0  PROCEDURE (General Precautions)

          11.1  Check reports, discuss with the Operator representative any hole problems such as
                    tight spots, ledges, etc., before the trip starts.

          11.2  The use of a rope to tail-in the drill pipe is a very good and safe practice.

          11.3   Obtain verbal confirmation from the Derrick man that he is in the safety harness
               and the harness is properly secured before tripping or any time work is being
                performed that requires the Derrick man to latch any tubulars into the elevators.

          11.4  Have the drill fluid properties checked on a regular basis and verified every 30
                minutes while circulating before a trip. Record to verify that the mud weight was
                  the same going in the hole and coming out the hole.

   12.0  RECORDS

          12.1   Trip Sheet with Pipe Tally, on the rig, two years

          12.2   Torque calibration records, Oracle or PMS system as applicable, indefinitely

          12.3   Driller Log, on-site, two years after last entry

          12.4   Trip program, on-site, current

          12.5   Well control data, on-site, duration of well plus one year

          12.6   Well control instructions, on-site, current

                 Policy: USE OF LINK TILT FUNCTION WHILE TRIPPING
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 2


       USE OF LINK TILT FUNCTION WHILE TRIPPING

   1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy regarding safe operating practice when using Top Drive or
        Power Swivel Link Tilt devices.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
      3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring compliance with this
          standard.

      3.2 The Driller is responsible to the OIM/PIC to advise if his visibility of the traveling
         equipment is impaired such that interference between the traveling equipment and any
         equipment or attachments in the derrick, including but not limited to monkey and/or
          stabbing boards, alternate stabbing/work baskets, stabbing arms, etc. which cannot be
          detected in sufficient time to avoid such an interference.

   4.0   PROCEDURES
      4.1 All rigs fitted with a Top Drive or Power Swivel shall be surveyed to ensure there is
         adequate clearance between the traveling equipment and interferences specified in this
          standard when the link tilt is in the extended position. If not, the interference shall be cut
         back or adjusted as required to provide adequate clearance.

      4.2 For rigs where the interference cannot be cut back to provide sufficient clearance, the
          following actions shall be carried out:

              4.2.1 The Driller shall ensure that the Link Tilt function is sufficiently positioned to
                  ensure interference with any derrick equipment is avoided.

              4.2.2 If the Driller's visibility is impaired to the extent that he cannot ensure that no
                    interference will be incurred, a video camera shall be procured, installed and
                  maintained such that the Driller has good visibility during tripping operations.

              4.2.3 The Driller shall stop all tripping operations if and when he loses visibility of any
                    derrick installed equipment and shall not resume tripping until his visibility of the
                 equipment is restored.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 75 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 407

                 Policy: USE OF LINK TILT FUNCTION WHILE TRIPPING
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 2


              4.3 The Driller must be aware of clearances between the top drive and equipment in
                   the derrick while raising and lowering.

              4.3.1 The link tilt shall not be extended while moving the top drive past the monkey
                   board.

              4.3.2 The Driller shall observe the movement of the top drive on the camera monitor
                  while the top drive is positioned at or traveling past the monkey board.

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES
        None

                                    Policy:  CEMENTING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  CEMENTING OPERATIONS

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to define the measures and controls for cementing operations.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher are individually
                 responsible for implementing this procedure during their tour.
          3.2   The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the drill
               crew and drilling operation on the rig floor which includes cementing activities on
                  the rig floor.
          3.3   The OIM is responsible to ensure that third-party equipment is inspected prior to
                   installation (HSE 100.28).

   4.0  GENERAL
          4.1    Third-party personnel aboard ADES mobile offshore drilling units (MODUs) shall
               conform to the expectations of the ADES Management System requirements.
          4.2    All  third-party  personnel coming aboard ADES MODUs  shall  receive an
                  orientation (HSE 100.24 as applicable).
          4.3   The OIM / Tool pusher shall ensure that Safety Data Sheets (SDS) have been
                provided by the cementing contractor for all products sent to the MODU. MODU
               and third-party chemicals are to be added to the chemical inventory in HSE
                Dashboard.
          4.4    Cementing Operations shall be conducted under the Permit to Work System.
          4.5    Prior to the cementing operation, a planning meeting (pre-job safety) shall be held
                with all personnel that are directly involved with the cementing operation to ensure
                   that key personnel understand the job and their particular responsibilities. The
                cementing procedure should be reviewed and verified that job responsibilities and
                  safety precautions are clear to all personnel.
          4.6    Third-party personnel shall not operate ADES equipment, such as man rider air
                   hoists, etc.
          4.7    Ensure cement manifold and lines have been tested prior to any cementing or testing
                 of equipment with the cement unit.
          4.8     If the casing string / liner is to be rotated or reciprocated during the cementing job,
                 ensure that the top drive cement head / manifold is of sufficient rating to support
                  the casing and landing string weight plus 100,000 pounds over pull.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 77 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 420
                                    Policy:  CEMENTING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 4


          4.9    All temporary lines such as chicksans and coflex hoses shall be secured by whip
                checks prior to pressure testing. All chicksan lines or other flexible lines are to be
                 secured with safety chains / shackles of sufficient strength on both ends of each
                chicksan link and a solid structure at both ends of the chicksan string.
          4.10   All personnel must remain clear of the pump fluid end and all high pressure piping
               and manifolds during cementing and testing operations.
          4.11   Connections shall not be hammered on with pressure in the piping system.
          4.12   Warning signs are to be posted; caution off area. Caution signs are required when
              pumping at high pressures.
          4.13   Announcements over public address (PA) system should be announced before
                 pressuring up on unit or lines.
          4.14  The Operator Representative and OIM / Tool pusher shall consider suspending
                 operations if environmental conditions endanger the safety of personnel and/or
                equipment.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Well Control Considerations)

          5.1    Ensure that circulating swedges (Casing x Drill Pipe and Casing x male half of
                 chiksan union) are available on the floor for the appropriate size of casing threads.
                Ensure function testing of these valves and document on the morning report and on
                  the International Association of Drilling Contractors (IADC) report.
          5.2    Ensure that mud has been conditioned with gas back down to background levels.
                Other parameters must be at acceptable levels.
          5.3   When using an unweighted spacer in cement  job, ensure  that reduction of
                  hydrostatic pressure is not sufficient enough to allow an influx to enter anywhere
                  in the entire wellbore.
          5.4    High pressure piping system is to be bled down upon completion of cementing
                  operations. Check with pump operator to make sure system is bled down after the
                completion of cementing operation.
          5.5    During high pressure piping system bleed-down, all valves must be open to ensure
                   that no isolated pockets of trapped pressure exist.

   6.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Rig Pumps)
          6.1    Depending on the cementing procedure to be applied, use of the rig pumps may be
                  called for. But as a matter of contingency, rig pumps should be at the ready for
                cementing operations thus avoiding the necessity to react in a possibly unsafe
              manner to an unexpected event.
          6.2     If cement is to be displaced with the rig pumps and the surface line volume is not
               known, line up the pumps to the drill floor stand pipe manifold.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 78 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 420
                                    Policy:  CEMENTING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 4


                  6.2.1  Open the bell nipple bleed-off line, close off Top Drive System (TDS) hose
                         valve, and pump slowly until returns are observed into the bell nipple.
                  6.2.2  Zero stroke counters on the Drillers’ panel and remote choke manifold prior
                  to start.
          6.3    Ensure the rig pump is ready to finish the cement displacement if the cement unit
                 encounters a problem and vice versa. Have the ability to switch from the rig pumps
                  to the cement pumps as needed.
          6.4    At no time shall cement be mixed or pumped through the rig’s mud system,
                 including the stand pipe, stand pipe manifold, choke, choke manifold, blowout
                 preventers (BOPs), TDS, kill line, test line, or choke line.
          6.5    All  pressure  relief valves must be  calibrated and  the  calibration  date not
                exceedingly more than 12 months from the last calibration date.
          6.6    Bulk cement storage and transfer facilities are of vital importance to the success as
                 well as the safety of the cementing operation. Equipment failure or emergency
                   repair shall unnecessarily expose rig personnel to hazards.
          6.7    Ensure that the transfer facilities from the P-tanks to the cement unit are operating
                  correctly with no moisture in lines. Ensure P-tanks have been fluffed with clear air
                  prior to transferring.

   7.0  PROCEDURE (Safety Precautions)
          7.1    Stand clear before cementing unit is started.
          7.2   Check that drive belt guards, fan guards, and other protective guards are in place
               and secured.
          7.3   The engine transmission should be in the neutral position prior to starting the
                cementing unit.
          7.4    All personnel should be aware of hot surfaces and stay clear, i.e., exhausts, piping,
                  transmission, and engine.
          7.5   The engine exhaust pipe insulation should be installed and properly maintained.
          7.6   The cement unit and surrounding area must be washed down upon completion of
               cement or test operation.
          7.7    Customer-provided cementing personnel are to be the only operators of the
                cementing unit.
          7.8   Check to ensure that pressure  relief valves are installed during cement unit
                  operation. Ensure they are not plugged or frozen.
          7.9     If drill crew personnel are requested to take cement slurry weights, a calibrated mud
                  scale must be provided by the Cementing contractor for this purpose.

                                     Policy:  CEMENTING OPERATIONS

    8.0 RECORDS

           8.1    Chemical Inventory, HSE Dashboard, current
           8.2    Third-party equipment checklist, on-site, one year after date of equipment removal
           8.3 SDS sheets, on-site, current

                                     Policy:  WIRELINE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  WIRELINE OPERATIONS

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to define the standard for wireline operations.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher are responsible for
                implementing this procedure during their assigned tour.
          3.2   The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the drill
               crew and drilling operation on the rig floor which includes wireline activities on
                  the rig floor.
          3.3   The OIM is responsible to ensure that third-party equipment is inspected prior to
                   installation (HSE 100.28).

   4.0  GENERAL
          4.1    Preparation for planned wireline operations shall normally include preparing the
              mud, a wiper trip, and strapping out of the hole. It is important that the Driller has
               an accurate pipe tally and gets an accurate strap while coming out.
          4.2    Service company wireline units shall:

                  4.2.1  Be equipped with drip pans for pollution prevention.

                  4.2.2  Be equipped with proper lifting eyes in good condition

                  4.2.3  Be in compliance with Class  I, Division  II, Group D as per American
                      Petroleum Institute (API) specifications.
          4.3    Service company personnel shall receive an HSE orientation upon arrival (HSE
                 100.24).
          4.4    Prior to commencement of wireline operations, the mobile offshore drilling unit
            (MODU) and service company supervisors on board shall conduct a Job Safety
                 Analysis.
          4.5    Personnel coming aboard  shall be informed of ongoing wireline operation,
                   restricted areas, and potential hazards. Daily pre-tour safety meetings shall include
                  wireline operations as a discussion topic.
          4.6    During wireline operations, the drill floor, wireline unit and any areas between the
              two where the wireline is in tension shall be designated as restricted areas and


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 81 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 425
                                     Policy:  WIRELINE OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 3

                 accordingly cordoned off. Access points to the drill floor shall be posted with signs
               and barriers indicating wireline operations, authorized personnel only.

          4.7    Public address announcements shall be made at the start, during the operation at
                  frequent intervals and at the completion of the wireline operation.
          4.8    Communications between the wireline unit and the rig floor shall be established,
                    tested, and maintained throughout the operation.
          4.9   The wireline service company personnel shall be responsible for safe rigging up of
                  wireline equipment. ADES personnel shall act in a supporting role, operating
           ADES equipment such as utility and personnel hoists.
          4.10   Ensure that all personnel on the drill floor have a proper Self-Contained Breathing
                Apparatus (SCBA) in the event of emptying sample chambers with a potential to
                  contain H2S (HSE 300.11). All nonessential people shall be restricted from the drill
                    floor.
          4.11   Wireline operations shall be carried out in a manner which ensures the safety of all
                  personnel, equipment, and the environment.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Well Control Considerations)

         Well control considerations in wireline operations shall vary widely depending on the
           operation, the condition of the drilling fluid, etc. At a minimum, the following must be
          observed:
          5.1    Wireline operations shall be conducted using sound well control methods. Devices
                such as lubricators, stuffing boxes, or dual blowout preventers (BOPs) shall be
               employed during wireline operations.
          5.2   The trip tank shall be utilized to keep the well full of fluid. Wireline displacement
                 values shall be recorded by the driller at 15-minute intervals. Fluid levels in the
                 well bore shall be monitored using the trip tank.
          5.3    Should a well control situation develop, the well shall be shut in. The OIM / Tool
                  pusher, shore-side Rig Manager, and Operator representative shall immediately be
                    notified. The Rig Manager shall notify ADES Senior Management.
          5.4    In the event operations require that wireline be run through drill pipe a fully opening
                   safety valve (FOSV) shall be installed on the drill pipe. A means of cutting the
                  wireline in case a well control situation should develop shall be provided.
          5.5    Wireline lubricators shall be bolted and flanged to top of annular and tested for
                 remedial operations.
          5.6    During explosives handling and wire line activities involving explosives, power to
                  the top drive shall be isolated, locked out, and documented on a Permit to Work
               and associated Job Safety Analysis.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 82 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                                     Policy:  WIRELINE OPERATIONS

Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 3

   6.0  PROCEDURE (Controls: Crane Operations)

          6.1    Crane operations are to be kept to a minimum during wireline operations.

          6.2  A Permit to Work and Job Safety Analysis shall be required for crane operations
                while wireline operations are in progress (simultaneous operations [SIMOPS]).
          6.3    Loads shall never be lifted over a wireline.
          6.4    Only the designated, fully qualified Crane Operator shall operate a crane during
                  wireline operations. Assistant Crane Operators shall not take the controls at these
                  times.

   7.0   RECORDS

          7.1    Third-party equipment checklist, on-site, one year after date of equipment removal

          7.2    Wireline displacement values, on-site, current

   8.0   REFERENCES

          8.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)

          8.2   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)

          8.3   HSE 100.24, HSE Site Orientation Induction

          8.4   HSE 100.28, Third-party Personnel and Equipment

          8.5   HSE 300.08, Respiratory Protection

          8.6   HSE 300.11, Hydrogen Sulfide

          8.7   API RP 500: Classification of Locations for Electrical Installations at Petroleum
                    Facilities Classified as Class 1, Division 1 and Division 2

          8.8   API RP 14F: Design Installation and Maintenance of Electrical Systems for fixed
               and Floating Offshore Petroleum Facilities for Unclassified and Class 1, Division
              1 and Division 2 Locations

                            Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                            Policy No. OPS 430
                                        Policy: JARRING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1 of 2


   JARRING OPERATIONS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the standard for jarring operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
           operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Night Tool pusher are
                  responsible for implementing this procedure during their assigned tour.
           3.2   The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the rig
                   floor and drill crew.

    4.0  GENERAL

           4.1   The drill crew should make every effort to prevent pipe from becoming stuck in the
                   hole. Maintaining drilling fluid properties, being aware of tight spots, making
                 connections in good order, etc., are important to avoid stuck pipe.
           4.2    Prior to jarring operations an announcement shall be made over the public address
                system that jarring is about to commence. Barriers to the drill floor shall be erected
                and all nonessential personnel shall leave the drill floor.
           4.3    Personnel shall not be in the mast or derrick when pulling or jarring on stuck pipe.
           4.4    Subsequent to jarring operations, the drill crew shall inspect the derrick, monkey
                  board, crown, and top drive to ensure all objects are well secured.

    5.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Jars)

           5.1    Normally, the jars should be run into the hole in the neutral position, usually at
                 midstroke of the mandrel.
           5.2     Jars shall be secured at the rotary by means of a safety clamp.
           5.3   The drill crew is directed to avoid placing their hands on the jars while handling
               them into the hole. This is particularly true of the mandrel area of the jars.
           5.4    Drag on drill pipe must be closely monitored by the Driller to ensure the jars are
                  not inadvertently cocked. Tight spots or debris in the hole may induce sufficient
                 drag to enable the jars to fire.
           5.5   The Driller must be aware of the jar settings,  i.e., the amount of tension or
                compression required to trigger the jars.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 84 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 430
                                       Policy: JARRING OPERATIONS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2


          5.6   The Driller shall always monitor the weight indicator closely for drag; the Driller
               must be alert to the possibility of firing the jars while tripping.
                  5.6.1  The OIM / Tool pusher shall inform the Driller as part of his daily written
                           instructions, of the maximum allowable overpull.
                  5.6.2  This information shall be provided to the Operator’s representative as well.
          5.7    Elevators should not be removed from the drill pipe while pulling out of the hole
                    until the drill pipe has stabilized after setting the slips.
          5.8   On each trip the jar should be visually inspected for any indication of damage,
                 excessive wear, or leakage.
                  5.8.1   If the jar is to be unused for a period of time, flush the bore with fresh water
                           to remove all drilling fluids.
                  5.8.2  Wash down the tool mandrel and protect tool joints with thread compound
                      and protectors.
                  5.8.3  Never use the mandrel for a tong area or a lifting/tie down point.
          5.9     If jars are to be racked back in the derrick they must be placed on the bottom of the
                   stand.
          5.10    If the jar is to be placed on the top of the stand to be racked back, ensure the space
                 out does not allow the mandrel clamp to be in the monkey board fingers.
          5.11  When using an automated racking system to handle a stand including a jar, ensure
                  the gripping head of the racking arm is not at the jar mandrel.
          5.12    If ton-mile accumulation is exceeded during jarring operations, the drill line must
                be slipped and cut at the earliest opportunity.
          5.13   Subsequent to completion of jarring operations an announcement shall be made
                 over the public address system that jarring operations have terminated.

   6.0   RECORDS
          Derrick inspection record post-jarring activity, on-site, one year

   7.0   REFERENCES

        None

  FLUIDS CONTROL

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to define the controls that are required to ensure the safe
          handling, mixing, and use of  drilling, completion, and hydrocarbon-based fluids in
          execution of the drilling operation.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
          3.1   The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Night Tool pusher are
                 responsible for implementing this procedure during their assigned tour.

          3.2   The Driller is responsible for immediate supervision of the drilling operation
                 including monitoring fluids in the hole.

   4.0  GENERAL

          4.1    Safety Data Sheets (SDS) shall be maintained on board the unit with the OIM,
               Medic, and / or assigned person. SDS for all mud additives shall also be
                maintained in any area where chemicals are used or stored.

                  4.1.1  Personnel shall be knowledgeable of the harmful effects various drilling
                           fluid additives can have if not properly handled.

                  4.1.2  Personnel  shall be knowledgeable  in  the proper  personal  protective
                      equipment (PPE) to wear for the various chemicals mixed into the drilling
                             fluid. PPE and other safety measures shall be in place before the job starts.

                  4.1.3  Personnel shall be knowledgeable of the methods of cleaning up and
                        disposing of drilling fluid additives and drilling fluid.

          4.2    All seals, elements, and other rubber goods including mud pump pistons must be
                compatible with hydrocarbon-based drilling fluids. Premature equipment failure is
                 assured in the event that they are not in place when oil-based mud (OBM) is in use.

          4.3    Oil spill pollution prevention kits should be of an adequate number, located in
                 proper areas to address emergency cleanup of OBM.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 86 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 440
                                          Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 12

          4.4    There shall be a system by which individual tank fluid levels can be monitored from
                  the Driller’s console (Pit Volume Totalizer).

                  4.4.1  The system shall be equipped with both audible and visual alarms in the
                        event of a loss in the total mud system. Alarms shall remain enabled.

                  4.4.2   Pit system Master Valves shall be isolated to prevent discharge and
                       environmental impact.
          4.5    All dump lines on drilling fluid pits shall be equipped with two valves the outer
                 valve being a  gate valve equipped with a locking device  to  prevent any
                  unintentional or accidental release of drilling fluid into the environment. Dump
                 valves (or any overboard lines) shall be operated under the permit to work system.
          4.6    All transfers of drilling fluid shall be directed by the Driller, no transfers shall be
              made without his knowledge.
          4.7      It is sometimes necessary to use OBM, corrosive, or non-environmental fluids on
                   certain wells.
          4.8   Working with OBM and completion fluids, appropriate PPE as per MSDS sheets
               and Job Safety Analysis.
          4.9    Extra precautions should be considered when using oil-based or any fluid harmful
                   to the environment.
          4.10   All overboard valves and equipment shall be locked / monitored and only removed
                under a permit to work.
          4.11  ADES practices and utilizes pollution prevention at all times (HSE 200.02.)
          4.12  Pumping of flammable liquids with a cement unit or mud pumps shall not be done
                  unless the Rig Manager, or person designated by them, has been satisfied that
                 adequate precautions have been taken and written permission given.
          4.13   Piping and containers are to be purged after use to remove flammable liquid.
          4.14   Pre-job meeting shall be held with all personnel that shall be or have the potential
                   to be affected by the task or unplanned events associated with the task.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Drill Floor)

          5.1   The drill floor must be kept as free as possible of drilling fluid. Use of water to
                  clean the drill floor while using OBM, if allowed at all, must be kept to an absolute
             minimum due its contamination of OBM.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 87 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 440
                                         Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                   Page 3 of 12


          5.2  A mud saver valve shall be in place while drilling. The valve shall be closed
                anytime the drill string is disconnected while making a connection, dropping ball,
                    etc.
          5.3  A catch pan shall be installed under the rotary table / rig floor. It shall be routed to
                  the flow line.
          5.4   The mouse hole should be equipped with a drain line to the active system.

   6.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Mixing and Treatment Areas)

          6.1   The Derrickman or Shaker Man shall keep shaker screens clean optimizing
                  capacities of the shale shakers.
          6.2    Centrifugal pumps should be equipped with mechanical  seals.  If  not,  the
               Derrickman shall make frequent checks to keep pump packing from leaking.
          6.3    All pumps, hoppers, and drilling fluid treatment equipment including centrifuges,
                 desanders, desilters, etc., shall be equipped with containment combing at least 3”
                  in height to contain any drilling fluid lost through overspray, leaks, ruptures, spills,
                    etc. Combing containment shall be cleaned regularly.
          6.4   The mixing and treatment areas shall be equipped with proper containers of
                   sufficient number for the disposal of absorbents, contaminated, gloves, rags, etc.

   7.0  PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Transfer of OBM from Supply Vessels)

          7.1    Oil pollution prevention kits (as per Shipboard Oil Pollution Emergency Plan
               [SOPEP], Oil Pollution Emergency Plan [OPEP], etc., as applicable) shall be
                 located at the transfer point.
          7.2    Individual units shall have site specific procedures for the transfer of premixed
                    drilling fluids from supply boats to the unit. The OIM / Tool pusher shall ensure
                   that a mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU)-specific OBM and fuel transfer
                procedure is developed, approved, and implemented on board the MODU.
          7.3    Transfer procedures shall include at a minimum (see example template section
                 below):
                  7.3.1  Product to be transferred
                  7.3.2  Expected volume to be transferred
                  7.3.3  Expected destination of fluid, the maximum capacity of the tank and its
                         current level


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 88 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 440
                                         Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                   Page 4 of 12


                  7.3.4  The rate at which the product shall be pumped
                  7.3.5  The sequence of the transfer operation
                  7.3.6  Suspension of hot work (if the product is of a flammable nature)
                  7.3.7  Containment methods
                  7.3.8  Emergency shutdown

          7.4    Radio communication shall be maintained between the standby person, control
               room, and supply boat during transfers.
          7.5   The transfer procedure shall be communicated to the supply vessel prior to
              commencement of transfer. A pre-job meeting shall take place on the MODU using
                  the procedure as the topic.
          7.6   The transfer hose must be rated for hydrocarbon fluids with a certified burst rating
                 of 600 pounds per square inch (psi) or greater. The hose shall be visually inspected
                  prior to use. Hoses used to transfer hydrocarbon products shall not be used for any
                 other type of fluid. Hoses used in the transfer of environmentally sensitive fluids
                  are required to be pressure tested before use.
          7.7   The hose shall be supported at the supply boat end and transfer station ends in such
                a fashion as to avoid excess strain on the hose couplings. Break-away couplings
                   shall be installed in the hoses to prevent pollution if the hose is parted during fluid
                    transfer.
          7.8  A designated standby person equipped with a portable radio shall attend the transfer
                   station watching for leaks from the hose / connections and to shut down the transfer
                  in the event of said leaks. The standby person will communicate at regular intervals
                 during the transfer as to how much has been transferred and how much has been
                 received to ensure the fluid is being transferred as intended.
          7.9    There shall be a valve installed at the supply vessel end of the transfer hose to
                 prevent contents from spilling from hose upon disconnecting. These valves will be
                Avery-Hardoll type or equivalent.

   8.0  PROCEDURE (Example format of MODU-Specific Transfer Procedure)

          8.1    Products to be Transferred to Vessel
                  8.1.1  No. 2 Diesel Oil and cargo information
                          8.1.1.1 Liquid – light amber yellow in color.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 89 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 440
                                         Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL

                          8.1.1.2 Odor – distinctively recognizable as diesel.
                          8.1.1.3 Hazards – avoid contact with skin. Preclude presence of open flame.
                          8.1.1.4 Do not expose to open flames.
                          8.1.1.5 Contain any open spill or leak. Wash exposed skin with soap and
                                water.
                          8.1.1.6 Fight fire in open with inert gas or chemical foam. Do not use water.

                  8.1.2  Oil-Based Mud and cargo information

                          8.1.2.1 Liquid – colorless to light yellow
                          8.1.2.2 Odor – hydrocarbon
                          8.1.2.3 Hazards – avoid  contact with  eyes,  skin, or  clothing. Avoid
                                breathing vapors.
                          8.1.2.4 Do not expose to open flames.
                          8.1.2.5 Wash exposed skin with soap and water. Flush eyes with water for
                                     at least 15 minutes. If inhaled, remove to fresh air.
                          8.1.2.6 Fight fire with carbon dioxide, foam, dry chemical, and fog.

                  8.1.3  Transfer Procedures apply to all transfer of diesel fuel and oil – Based Mud.
                  8.1.4  Description of oil transfer systems.
                  8.1.5  Number of persons required to be on duty during transfer operations.

                          8.1.5.1 Onboard from barge or supply boat – two qualified men: one on
                            deck of MODU and one on deck of barge/supply boat.
                          8.1.5.2 MODU tankage: one qualified man.
                          8.1.5.3 MODU tankage to remove day tanks above main deck: one qualified
                       man at pump station; one helper at remote day tank.
          8.2    Duties

                  8.2.1  The qualified person in charge shall be the Rig Mechanic (for diesel fuel
                           transfers) and the Derrickman (for OBM transfers) or as otherwise assigned
                    by the OIM.
                  8.2.2   It is the Rig Mechanic’s / Derrickman’s responsibility to make up hose
                        connections, operate transfer pumps, shut down transfer pumps in case of
                         leak or spill, and to ensure that spill or leakage is contained and isolated
                     from bilge and ballast systems.
                  8.2.3  The helper shall be the Motorman and  / or Roustabout on tour. Their
                       primary responsibility is to operate a self-stopping fuel oil transfer hose and
                          to ensure there is no spill.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 90 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                            Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                            Policy No. OPS 440
                                          Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
