           8.3    Tending Vessel Moorings during Transfer
                   8.3.1  This responsibility is assigned to the deck crew on the delivery barge or
                        supply boat.
           8.4   Emergency Shut Down Procedure
                   8.4.1  In the event of emergency, the Rig Mechanic / Derrickman must activate
                          the shutdown.
                   8.4.2  There are two shut downs for each of the two fuel oil pumps.
                   8.4.3  See MODU-specific piping diagram.
                   8.4.4   If possible, shut the supply valves at the pump station and the equalizing
                            line valve to isolate the tankage.
                   8.4.5  The pump is specifically designed to eliminate siphoning through a stopped
                     pump.
                   8.4.6  Communication between barge or supply boat and the MODU  is by
                             intrinsically safe, handheld, portable radios.
                   8.4.7  Communication between pump room and remote day tanks is by sound
                      powered telephone units.

           8.5    Topping Off Tanks

                   8.5.1  Tank filling and topping off operations may proceed by using remote liquid
                           level indicating system only if a helper (Motorman and / or Roustabout) is
                          stationed at the overflow vent of the tank being filled.

                   8.5.2   If a spill is observed the helper is to communicate via radio to get the
                            transfer stopped immediately.

           8.6   Normal Shut Operation

                   8.6.1  At  the completion  of normal  fuel  oil  transfer  operations,  it  is  the
                           responsibility of the Rig Mechanic / Derrickman to close all valves which
                      were operated during the transfer operation.
                   8.6.2  The system should be left in a closed state to prevent any spills or leakage
                        during subsequent operations.
                   8.6.3  All leakage in containment is to be passed to the dirty oil holding tanks.

           8.7    Reporting Spillage

                   8.7.1  The Rig Mechanic / Derrickman in charge of fuel oil transfer operations
                      must immediately report any uncontained leakage or discharge overboard
                           to the OIM. If the spill is contained on board then it shall be dealt with


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 91 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 440
                                          Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 7 of 12

                       immediately using the spill kits provided as part of the SOPEP, OPEP, or
                 SPCC as applicable. Refer to HSE 200.02.
                  8.7.2  OIM / Tool pusher shall make the necessary incident reports as per HSE
                        100.00.
                  8.7.3   If required, the OIM / Tool pusher shall inform the relevant authorities
                        according to Oil Spill Response legislation if there is a spill to. Guidance on
                             this reporting will be facilitated through the QHSE Department.
          8.8    Vessel Closing and Opening Procedures

                  8.8.1  The Rig Mechanic / Derrickman in charge of fuel oil transfer operations
                           shall ensure that the sounding tubes, tank cleaning openings, and any other
                         applicable tank vessel openings are properly closed during fuel oil transfer
                         operations to prevent the inadvertent release of oil or hazardous material in
                         the event of a tank vessel incident.

          8.9    Transfer Hose Markings and Compatible Products

                  8.9.1  Every diesel hose shall be marked with three (3) light brown wraps of tape.
                  8.9.2  Every mud hose shall be marked with three (3) wraps of orange tape for
                       high pressure hose and with two (2) wraps of orange tape for low pressure
                       hose
                  8.9.3  The color-coded chart can be found in the OIM / Tool pusher’s office or
                        posted on the MODU for further information.
                  8.9.4  All diesel fuel hoses shall be used for diesel ONLY.
                  8.9.5  Oil-based mud hoses shall be used for oil-based mud, water-based mud, and
                       completion fluids ONLY.

          8.10   Oil-Based Mud and Fuel Transfer (Additional Instructions)

                  8.10.1 Supply vessel must be tied securely enough to the MODU to hold for all
                       expected weather and sea conditions during oil transfer operation.
                  8.10.2 Oil transfer hoses must be long enough to allow supply vessel to move to
                         the limits of its mooring without placing a strain on the hoses or piping
                        system.
                  8.10.3 Oil transfer hose shall be equipped with a hand-operated valve and a check
                        valve which shall allow no back feed from the MODU to the supply vessel.
                      These valves shall be made up on the supply vessel end of the transfer hose.
                  8.10.4 The person in charge of the fuel oil transfer on the MODU shall ensure all
                        valves are properly aligned, all connections are secure, and oil tank vent is
                             clear.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 92 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                          Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                        Policy No. OPS 440
                                        Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed
                                                          Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                 Page 8 of 12

                8.10.5 Welding, hot work operations, and smoking are prohibited during the
                         transfer of flammable or combustible materials. Smoking is also prohibited
                       unless there  is a dedicated smoking area in the accommodation area
                       designated by the OIM.

                8.10.6 Persons in charge of fuel oil transfer operation aboard the supply vessel and
                     aboard the MODU shall agree that the preparations are complete before
                      beginning oil transfer.
                8.10.7 The persons in charge of the fuel oil transfer on board the MODU and on
                     board the supply vessel shall monitor the fuel oil transfer system closely to
                      ensure no oil leaks occur and oil tanks do not overflow.
                8.10.8 During night operations the persons in charge shall ensure there is enough
                          light to see any leaks which may occur in the fuel oil transfer system
                      immediately. In general, fluid transfers are to be conducted in daylight
                        hours, particularly if their escape to the water would be classified as a
                         pollutant.
                8.10.9 When the hose end is secured on the MODU, the person in charge on the
             MODU shall check the valve on the end of the hose to ensure  it  is
                      completely closed.

  9.0  PROCEDURE (Flammable / Combustible Liquids)

         9.1   The release of flammable liquids to the open atmosphere is a major concern any
               time that flammable liquids are pumped.
         9.2    In order to reduce the potential for hydrocarbon liquid release to the open, the
               following precautions should be observed:
                 9.2.1  The flash point of liquid to be pumped must be known
                 9.2.2  No liquids shall be pumped if its temperature is within 10 degrees of its
                         flash point without a thorough risk-assessment
                 9.2.3  Visually inspect all valves, pumps, and unions before using.
                 9.2.4  Cover all unions on hoses that shall be pressurized with a suitable barrier,
                     such as canvas covers, to prevent flammable mist in case of failure.
                 9.2.5  Cover pump fluid end to prevent flammable mist due to swab failure, etc.
                      This is important, even if the fluid is not heated to within 10 degrees of its
                         flash point.
         9.3    Prevent agitation of fluids that may cause foaming.
         9.4    Inspect all piping, gland packing, etc., that may cause air to enter into the system.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 93 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                          Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                        Policy No. OPS 440
                                        Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed
                                                          Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                 Page 9 of 12

  10.0  PROCEDURE (Flammable / Combustible Liquid Transfer Guidelines)

        10.1   Ensure a portable fire extinguisher is on hand.
        10.2  Hook up ground cable from MODU to supply vessel if applicable.
        10.3   Ensure vent to fuel tank is clear and open.
        10.4   Position the barge or supply vessel as close to the fuel take-on point as possible.
        10.5   Supply vessel must be tied up (not applicable to dynamically positioning (DP)
               supply vessels)
        10.6  Lower / stretch the fuel hose onto the supply vessel, or on some MODUs, the fuel
               hose is stretched from the fuel barge to the MODU.
        10.7  Hook up the ground cable from the supply vessel to the MODU.

        10.8  Hook the hose to the supply vessel and ensure the proper seals are utilized.
        10.9  Check the supply vessel for water in the tanks before delivery to the rig.
        10.10  Record the fuel meter reading with the Barge Supervisor. If a meter is not available,
              sound the supply vessel tanks.
        10.11 Sound the MODU fuel tanks and compare with the fuel meter if a meter was
                 available to use.
        10.12  Stop transferring all fuel internally on the MODU.
        10.13  Select which tanks are to be filled and open the necessary fuel inlet valves. Make
                sure the vents are not plugged.
        10.14  After receiving the fuel, verify the meter reading and sound the tanks.
        10.15  Close all fuel tank valves.
        10.16  If possible, blow air through the hose to remove any product, and then remove the
                 fuel hose from the supply vessel.
        10.17 Remove the ground cable.
        10.18  While taking on fuel there must be a person on the supply vessel, at take-on station,
                  rig tanks and at crane at all times if the crane is used in the fueling process.
        10.19  Fuel may be transferred to various locations and to the supply vessel fueling station.
        10.20 The following procedures should be utilized while transferring fuel:
                10.20.1  Close all engine room fuel tanks supply valves.
                10.20.2   Verify that the person receiving the fuel is ready and at the correct
                              station.
                10.20.3   Reset fuel meter to zero and record the meter reading.
                10.20.4  Turn on the fuel transfer pump and stand by until the receiver is finished
                           fueling his equipment.
                10.20.5   Secure the pump and verify that all valves are closed.

                                                          Mahfouz

  11.0  PROCEDURE (Completion Fluid Precautions)

        11.1   Completion fluids are placed in the well to facilitate final operations prior to
                  initiation of production, such as  setting screens production  liners, packers,
              downhole valves, or shooting perforations into the producing zone.
        11.2   Completion fluids are typically brines (chlorides, bromides, and formates), but in
                theory could be any fluid of proper density and flow characteristics.
        11.3   Completion fluids are used to displace drilling mud when transitioning from the
                  drilling phase to the completion phase or in well workover operations.
        11.4  The extremely dense brine solution gives the fluid its weight of about 15 or greater
              pounds / gallon, which makes the brine especially useful in holding back flammable
                   oil and gas particles in high pressure wells.
        11.5   Zinc Bromide (ZnBr2) and Calcium Chlorides (CaCl2), are two of the types of
               completion fluids that are corrosive and can cause burns. Both are also irritants to
                  respiratory, eyes, and skin.
        11.6  As a powder additive, do not breathe dust used in making completion fluids. In case
                of insufficient ventilation, wear appropriate respiratory equipment.
        11.7  Use local exhaust ventilation to keep airborne levels to a minimum.
        11.8   Per the Job Safety Analysis, wear the appropriate Personal Protective Equipment
              (PPE) which may include but is not limited to the following: splash goggles, apron
                        / slicker suit, approved dust respirator, rubber boots, face shield, and rubber gloves.
              Ensure that safety showers and eye wash stations are fully functional.
        11.9   Spills shall be cleaned up and neutralized with a dilute solution of sodium
                carbonate. All spills should be reported immediately to the OIM and in accordance
               with HSE 200.02.
        11.10  Ensure that a current SDS is available which contains first aid measures in the event
                of contact.
        11.11  For jobs in which well completion fluids are a factor, the Job Safety Analysis shall
                include discussion of the hazards of using these completion fluids, particularly Zinc
              Bromide, and eliminate or mitigate them.

  12.0  PROCEDURE (Tubular Handling Associated with Completion Fluids)

        12.1  When handling tubulars that have been used with completion fluids during well
                 operations, the following considerations shall be given, each one to be analyzed
               during the Job Safety Analysis process.
                12.1.1 When handling tubulars that have been used with completion fluids during
                       well operations, the OIM and Operator’s Representative should decide in
                          their pre-planning phase of day-to-day operations, whether tubulars shall be
                          laid down on the pipe deck or transferred directly to the workboat.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 95 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 440
                                         Policy: FLUIDS CONTROL
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                  Page 11 of 12

                 12.1.2 Picking up or laying down tubulars – both Drilling and Deck crews shall
                       conduct the Job Safety Analysis process together. Though each specific
                     crew may have their own procedural Job Safety Analysis (e.g., Drilling:
                         Pulling out of the Hole and Deck: transfer of tubulars) a simultaneous
                         operations (SIMOPs)  condition  exists and  unifying  the sequence  of
                       procedures and work conditions is required.
          12.2   For the specific task of laying down drill pipe on the pipe deck or laying down drill
                 pipe directly from the drill floor to workboat, the following considerations should
               be given when handling tubulars that have been used with completion fluids, each
               one to be analyzed during the Job Safety Analysis process.

                 12.2.1 The Job Safety Analysis process shall include review of the SDS for type
                        of  fluid, hazard  identification of potential exposure  possibilities, and
                        reduction methods to include mitigation steps of reducing the potential risk
                         as much as possible including personal protective barriers.
                 12.2.2 When pulling tubulars out of the hole, an outside diameter (OD) wiper shall
                      be used to remove as much fluid as possible on the pipe’s exterior surface
                     and solid end protectors should be installed on both the box end and tool
                            joint of each tubular.
                 12.2.3 After pulling out of the hole and using the OD wiper, a second option is to
                          rinse the tubulars only with water and install solid end protectors prior to
                        laying down on the pipe deck or transferring directly from the rig floor to
                       workboat.
                 12.2.4 Whether washed or not, the OIM shall consider collection and disposal of
                         residue from manually washing the tubulars or by the action of rainwater.
                 12.2.5 Awareness level of piping condition (residue) shall be communicated to all
             MODU personnel and workboat personnel alike during the pre-tour meeting
                        discussion and / or visual signage warning of pipe condition which is to be
                       posted on rig deck and / or workboat deck.
                 12.2.6 Workboat personnel shall be included in review of the Job Safety Analysis
                   when transferring these tubulars.
          12.3.  The Operator should ensure the Material Transfer Report (MTR) for tubulars or
               equipment exposed to completion fluids for the workboat and shore-base includes
                remarks for the condition of pipe (type residue), SDS, brief outlay of what steps
               have been done to mitigate exposure at rig site, and recommendation to close open
                 gaps.

  PERFORATING

   1.0   PURPOSE

        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance during perforation operations.

   2.0   SCOPE

         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

        The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible to ensure that this policy is
         implemented.

   4.0  PROCEDURE

          4.1    Perforating activities shall be carried out in accordance with ADES Well Control
               Manual.
          4.2    Prior to commencing well perforating operations, a risk assessment of the operation
                   shall be carried out with all personnel involved, reviewing the procedures for
                  operations, hazards, and emergencies. The Job Safety Analysis planning process
                   shall be used.
          4.3    Perforating operations must only be conducted under conditions where the well can
               be contained, monitored, and controlled.
          4.4   The restricted area shall be defined and marked.
          4.5    In an effort to prevent accidental firing of explosives, the following precautions
                   shall be taken prior to rigging up electrical perforating guns:
                  4.5.1   If the gun initiation device requires this, Radio silence shall be established,
                        including handheld “handheld ultra-high frequency (UHF) or very high
                       frequency (VHF) radios”.
                  4.5.2  Rig Manager and Operations Support are to be informed of radio silence
                            status.
                  4.5.3   Electrical welding  shall be stopped and  applicable  permit  to work
                         terminated.
                  4.5.4  Effects of electric generators and any active cathodic protection systems
                           shall be discussed with the perforating contractor and shut down  if
                         necessary.
                  4.5.5  The use of cellular phones is prohibited and shall be turned off.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 98 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 445
                                           Policy: PERFORATING
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 2

                  4.5.6  In case of doubt, all potential sources of electromagnetic radiation shall be
                        shut down.
                  4.5.7  Top Drive Systems (TDS) shall be isolated.
                  4.5.8  Perforating activities shall not be performed during electrical storms.
                  4.5.9  Announcements shall be made over the public address (PA) system at
                   commencement and upon completion of perforating operations.

          4.6     If high, surface hydrogen sulfide (H2S) or other problems are anticipated, the
                  perforation shall be carried out during daylight hours in order to avoid unnecessary
                 increased risk due to darkness.
          4.7    Well perforating may be carried out at night, but well flaring shall not be undertaken
                    at night without approval from the Rig Manager.
          4.8    Pressure control equipment, when used, shall be pressure tested to the maximum
                  anticipated wellhead pressure prior to running a tool into the wellbore.
          4.9     Pit levels shall be monitored during well perforation.
          4.10   During explosives handling and wire line activities involving explosives, power to
                  the top drive shall be isolated, locked out, and documented on a Permit to Work
               and associated Job Safety Analysis.

   6.0   RECORDS

          5.1    Permit to work, on board, six months
          5.2    Job Safety Analyses, HSE Dashboard, indefinitely.

   7.0 REFERENCES

          6.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)
          6.2   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)
          6.3  WC  1120, Handbook – Completion and Workover Running  Completions,
                  Perforated, Unperforated, and Cemented

                Policy: SIMULTANEOUS OPERATIONS (DRILLING AND PRODUCTION)

Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  SIMULTANEOUS OPERATIONS (DRILLING AND PRODUCTION)

   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance in conducting simultaneous drilling and
          production operations (SIMOPS).

   2.0   SCOPE
         This policy applies to the Drilling Division for ADES operations except as otherwise
          specified within this policy.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
        The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible to ensure that this policy is
         implemented.

   4.0  PROCEDURE (General)

          4.1    Drilling operations close to wells that are producing shall only be carried out after
                a risk assessment  is carried out and only after risk mitigation measures are
                developed in such a way that the risks are adequately controlled.

          4.2    Operations involving moving onto a platform where wells are kept flowing are not
                  permitted.

          4.3   The following issues shall be addressed:
                  4.3.1  Down-hole safety valves
                  4.3.2  Emergency shut-down (ESD) stations
                  4.3.3  Online gas monitoring
                  4.3.4  Weather limitation criteria for installation moves
                  4.3.5  Towboat availability: a minimum of three (3) tugs with a total horsepower
                        (hp) of at least 12,000 HP is required for jack-up mobile offshore drilling
                          units (MODUs) or as defined in the MODU’s Marine Operations Manual.

          4.4    Pre-move meetings shall be conducted between the Operator and ADES to address
                  policies and MODU move procedures.
          4.5    This is not an all-inclusive list of issues to be considered by management but issues
                   that shall be included in the decision-making process.
          4.6     If drilling operations are to be carried out in the vicinity of producing wells,
                 appropriate risk mitigation measures shall be discussed with the customer. The Rig
              Manager or his qualified designee shall confirm the proposed plan as acceptable.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 100 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 450
                Policy: SIMULTANEOUS OPERATIONS (DRILLING AND PRODUCTION)

Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 2


          4.7    In particular, issues including, but not limited to the following, shall be addressed:
                  4.7.1  On-line gas monitoring
                  4.7.2  ESD stations
                  4.7.3  Down-hole safety valves
                  4.7.4  Live wellhead protection from load impact damage
          4.8   The Rig Manager has the responsibility of ensuring that none of the above
                 operations are carried out without prior approval from the Senior Rig Manager.

   5.0  PROCEDURE (Standards)

       When conducting drilling operations on a platform / structure with producing wells, the
          following must be followed:

          5.1     Install an emergency shut-down station near the Driller’s console and one installed
                 near an abandon station.
          5.2    Shut in all producible wells located in the well bay area below the surface and at
                  the well head when moving the MODU on or off a platform  / structure. This
                 includes rigging up and rigging down.
          5.3    Well must be shut in when moving or skidding MODU between wells on a platform
                          / structure, nippling up or nippling down blowout preventers (BOPs), and any
               equipment handling overhead or around producing well.
          5.4    Well must be shut in when the rig is within 500 feet of the platform / structure while
              moving on and off location.
          5.5   No hot work shall be done on the MODU without a hot work permit being filled
                   out, approved, and signed by OIM / Tool pusher and Company Representative.
          5.6    Producing wells shall be monitored on regular basis for leaks.

   6.0   RECORDS
        Hot Work Permits, on-site, six months

   7.0   REFERENCES
         Rig Move Policies

────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 455
                           Policy: MAKING CONNECTIONS WITH TOP DRIVE
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 2


   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance when making connections with the top
           drive.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
        The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible to ensure that this policy is
         implemented.

   4.0  PROCEDURE

          4.1   When making a connection with a top drive, make sure that the person in the derrick
                      is ready before the Driller breaks out the top drive connection.

          4.2   Make sure that the pipe is free, work the pipe as needed to clean the hole up around
                  the bit and the Bottom Hole Assembly (BHA).

                  4.2.1   If there are tight spots  it should be noted and the OIM and Operator’s
                        Representative should be notified before the connection is made.

          4.3   Once the mud pumps have been shut off, the flow should be checked at the rotary
                 or the shakers.

          4.4     If there is no flow, stop the rotation of the drill pipe and set the slips.

                  4.4.1  The top drive shall then break the connection.

                  4.4.2  Note: make sure there is no trapped pressure in the drill pipe and then close
                         the Texas Iron Works (TIW) valve before spinning out. (As applicable to
                         the rig, close the Inside Blowout Preventer (IBOP) on the top drive which
                               is remotely operated.)

          4.5   When the connection is spun out, the threads need to be cleaned and doped.

                  4.5.1  When the threads are cleaned, visually inspect the saver sub for worn
                         threads or wash outs.
                  4.5.2  Change the saver sub as needed.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 102 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 455
                           Policy: MAKING CONNECTIONS WITH TOP DRIVE
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 2


          4.6   The Driller shall then hoist the top drive up for a stand of drill pipe.

                  4.6.1  The Derrickman or Floorman shall then latch the stand in the elevators.

                  4.6.2  After picking up the stand of drill pipe to the working height, the threads
                         are then cleaned and doped on the bottom connection.

          4.7   The Driller shall then stab the stand into the joint in the rotary (a stabbing guide
                   shall be used to stab the stand) and spin up and torque up with the top drive.

                  4.7.1  The backup tongs should be used to prevent the rotary from spinning, unless
                          there is enough string weight to prevent the pipe from spinning in the slips
                     and rotary with the rotary locked.

                  4.7.2  Note: if the top drive revolutions per minute (RPM) are too fast and torque
                               is too high,  it shall cause the backup tongs to release energy.  It is a
                     recommendation that slower RPM is used (5 to 10 RPM) and set torque
                       should be 3,000 to 5,000 (3K to 5K). (Check with the manufacturer of the
                        top drive.)

          4.8    After torque up, the Driller shall then open the TIW valve (as applicable the
                remotely operated IBOP). Turn the pumps on, then pick up the standoff the slips
               and start rotating the pipe.

          4.9    After everything is leveled out, the Driller then shall check all alarms and reset,
                 then go to bottom and start drilling.

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES
        None

  SWIVEL OPERATIONS

   1.0   PURPOSE

        The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance when conducting swivel operations.

   2.0   SCOPE

         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

        The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible to ensure that this policy is
         implemented.

   4.0  PROCEDURE

          4.1   No one should be on the swivel while it is in motion. The brake should be chained
               down.

          4.2    Rig personnel should observe to ensure the safety line and clamps connecting the
                  rotary mud hose and swivel remain intact and in the correct location.

          4.3    All tools used overhead must be secured by means of a type of safety lanyard
                 capable of suspending the tool being used if the tool is dropped.

          4.4   When being set back, crew personnel should not place hands on or near the swivel
                 or rotary bushing as they enter the rat hole.

          4.5    Rig personnel should not stand in line of the pullback line for the swivel when it is
                being pulled back.

   4.0   RECORDS

        None

   5.0   REFERENCES

        None

                                    Policy:  BULK SOLIDS TRANSFER

  BULK SOLIDS TRANSFER
   1.0   PURPOSE
        The purpose of this policy is to give guidance on the proper procedures during bulk solids
           transfers (barite, gel, and cement)

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.


   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
        The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) is responsible for implementing this policy.

   4.0  PROCEDURE
          4.1   The OIM  shall designate the Rig Mechanic, Barge Engineer (BE), or Rig
               Maintenance Supervisor (RMS) as the person-in-charge (PIC) of all bulk solids to
                 or from mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU) and supply vessel, or from tank to
                 tank within the vessel.

          4.2   The PIC shall ensure that all persons involved in the bulk solids transfer have
                performed a Job Safety Analysis prior to beginning any transfer.

          4.3   The PIC shall determine the amount of personnel required to safely transfer bulk
                   solids. The PIC and supply vessel Captain shall ensure that communications via
                  portable radios are in place and monitored throughout transfer.

          4.4    Prior to transferring bulk solids from one location to another (either on the MODU
                 or between vessels):

                  4.4.1  All valves are properly aligned

                  4.4.2  All containment devices are in place

                  4.4.3  All transfer hoses are properly secured

          4.5   The supply vessel shall be provided with and acknowledge the procedure prior to
              commencement  of  transfer  to  discuss  and  verify  work  instructions  and
                   responsibilities.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 105 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 465
                                    Policy:  BULK SOLIDS TRANSFER
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 2


          4.6   The transfer hose must be rated for dry bulk solids transfers with a certified burst
                   rating of 300 pounds per square inch (psi) or greater. The transfer hose shall be
                  visually inspected prior to use.

          4.7   When transferring to a supply vessel, the hose shall be supported at the supply
                  vessel end and transfer station ends in such as a fashion as to avoid excess strain on
                  the hose couplings.

          4.8  A designated standby person equipped with a portable radio to pump stations shall
                  attend the transfer station watching for leaks from the hose / connections and to
                 shut down the transfer in the event of said leaks.

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES

          6.1   MSDS Online, HERO Document Center

          6.2   HSE 100.11 Job Safety Analysis (JSA)

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 470
                             Policy PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION
                         OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 6


  PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS

   1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish recommended procedures for the safe handling and use of diesel drive pipe
         hammers. This standard should be viewed as only a guide. Variations in equipment,
        hammers and  special  situations may  necessitate departures from these procedures.
         However, this guide should be followed to the maximum extent possible, and any departure
         from the procedures herein must provide equivalent safety for personnel and equipment.

   2.0   SCOPE
         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   4.0   RESPONSIBILITY

          3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring the senior drilling
          supervisor, and all other personnel involved with handling and operating diesel hammers
          are aware of this standard, and that it is referenced in all work permits and Job Risk
         Assessments issued for diesel hammer handling and operation.

          3.2 The senior drilling supervisor is responsible to the Rig Manager for compliance with
            this standard.

   5.0   PROCEDURES

          4.1 Preparation
              4.1.1 Personnel shall wear all necessary personal protective equipment, including hard
                     hats, safety boots, eye protection and hearing protection.
              4.1.2 The derrick must be thoroughly inspected to secure or remove any loose material.
              4.1.3 Hammer fuel and oil containers shall be kept in safety cans of no greater capacity
                  than five gallons. Fill spouts or funnels should be provided to eliminate spillage.
              4.1.4 Firefighting equipment, capable of handling a fire in the derrick should be made
                  ready and appropriate personnel briefed.
              4.1.5 If an air hoist is to be used to assist in handling the hammer, it should be inspected
                    to confirm it is in satisfactory condition.
              4.1.6 Any man-riding  operations  to be  in compliance with Man Riding HSE
                   Procedures.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 107 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 470
                           Policy PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION
                      OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 6


              4.1.7 A pre-drive safety meeting is to be held prior to initiation of any work. All
                  personnel that will be involved in the operation will attend. The Tool pusher will
                    chair the meeting.
              4.1.8 The hammer crew will be limited to an experienced Hammer Operator and one
                      assistant. All other non-essential personnel, with the exception of the Driller or
                 Tool pusher, shall remain off the floor during driving operations.

      4.2 Movement and Lifting
              4.2.1 Thoroughly inspect the hammer prior to moving or lifting.
                 •  Ensure the protective cover is in place over the end of the piston cylinder
                    and secure it with chains to the suspension plates.
                 •  Ensure the exhaust port covers are in place and secured
                 •  Ensure the piston locking screw is securely in place
                 •  Secure the lead locking plate
                 •  Properly sized slings and the shackles must be used.
                 •  For the weights of various size hammers. One and one-fourth inch slings with
                       the eyes formed by a Flemish loop about an extra heavy-duty thimble and 1-
                      3/8" bolt type anchor shackles are required.
                 •  Always lift and transport the hammer at an angle, with the top of the hammer
                          at a slightly higher elevation than the bottom.

              4.2.2 Rigging up the hammer on the catwalk.
                 •   Fill the diesel and fuel tanks. Make certain the safety chains for the filler caps
                       are in place.
                 •  Grease the hammer throughout with the proper grease
                 •  Apply a light coating of grease to the trip unit starter guides
                 •  Make up the control ropes to the fuel pump. Make up a control rope to the trip
                          level. It is recommended that 5/16" nylon rope in three different colors be
                     used in continuous lengths of a minimum of 100 feet. When the control ropes
                       are secure to the fuel pump and trip stop cables, double knot each rope and
                         finish with duct tape or equivalent to prevent fouling. If control ropes are on
                       the hammer from a previous job, remove the tape and inspect the cable loop,
                     rope and knots. Replace as necessary and re-tape.
                 •  Function the fuel pump control through its full range of movement to confirm
                         that each position "catches".
                 •   Pull the stop rope to ensure the fuel shut off valve is free.
                 •  Lubricate the fuel valve and pump with WD 40.
                 •  Thoroughly inspect the hammer lead for cracks.

[ صفحه 108 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 470
                           Policy PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION
                      OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS

                 •   Inspect the guides to see they are properly adjusted and that all bolts are tight.
                 •   Ins
                 •   pect the anvil plate top and bottom for cracks. Ensure that the cushion set is
                        in good condition and inspect the cushion guide for cracks.
                 •  Remove the exhaust ports caps.
                 •  Remove the protective cylinder cover.
      4.3 Picking the Hammer Up
              4.3.1 Without a Crane

                 •  With the hammer lying on the catwalk, connect 60' long slings from the
                   hook bell ears to the top of the lead.
                 •  Snub the bottom of the hammer with the catwalk air tugger and ease the
                 hammer up to the floor.
                 •  When the hammer is vertical under the travelling blocks, set it on the rotary
                        table and remove the 60' pick-up slings.
                 •  Lower the hook and connect the lead slings and trip sling.
                 •  Remove the combustion chamber plug and put it in your pocket to avoid
                          loss.
              4.3.2 With Crane

                 •  Set the hammer through the "V" door in a near horizontal position.
                 •  Connect the lead slings and the tip sling to the hook.
                 •  Pick the hammer up while slacking off with the crane.
                 •  When the hammer is vertical under the travelling block, slack the blocks off
                    and set the hammer on the rotary table. Disconnect the crane.
                 •  Remove the combustion chamber plug and place it in your pocket to avoid
                          loss.
      4.4 Elevating the Hammer in Derrick

              4.4.1 When driving pipe offshore, with the distance between the rotary and the seabed
                known, pick up the hammer prior to the last joint of conductor pipe to be picked
                up before the shoe touches the seabed, i.e., do not count on penetration.

              4.4.2 Prior to picking the hammer up off of the rotary, ensure the piston is at the bottom
                   of its stroke and the piston locking screw is set.

              4.4.3 With the hammer connected to the bridle and hanging vertical, remove the piston
                   locking screw. Remove the hammer-to-lead locking plates.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 109 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 470
                           Policy PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION
                      OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 4 of 6

      4.5 Rigging up in the Derrick
          4.1.1  Connect the bridle to the hook

          4.1.2  In addition to the lead slings, it is recommended that 4" x 12', four ply nylon slings
                  (not shown) be used as safety lines parallel to the lead slings. Connect these to the
              same shackles that secure the lead slings. Tape the nylon safety slings to the lead
                  slings with 2" duct tape each 2 feet to ensure the slings do not foul while
               hammering. Also, provide tape at the contact points between the sling thimbles and
                  the shackles to prevent the thimbles from sliding to the side of the shackle which
                  will avoid binding, resulting in uneven pick up.
          4.1.3  All shackles should be made up with the nuts to the front and outside of the lead to
                allow visual inspection while hammering. Use properly sized cotter pins to secure
                  the shackle bolts through the nuts. Do not use welding rods or other material that is
                 loose in the bolt hole as it can be cut, allowing the bolt to back out.
          4.1.4  On rigs equipped with Top Drives, remove the top drive pipe handler and install a
                  protector on the main shaft pin. The conductor handling slings should now be
                 attached to the solid body elevator link adapter.

      4.6 Driving Pipe
          4.6.1  When the conductor touches the seabed, or the bottom of the cellar, mark the pipe
               and observe the rate of penetration. When the conductor stops moving mark it
                  again. As a rule of thumb on offshore rigs, this depth will usually be equal to half
                 of the spud can penetration, depending on the weight of the string. If the depth of
                  the conductor penetration is less than half of this depth, exercise extreme caution.
               Observe the mark on the conductor for no less than ten minutes to ensure no free
              movement will occur. Set the hammer on the conductor, and again, wait for ten
                minutes to observer for free movement. When the conductor has set ten minutes
                with no free movement, with the combustion chamber plug already removed, the
                   fuel pump in "0" fuel setting prepare for the first dry blow.
          4.6.2  Clear the rig floor non-essential personnel, stationing the Driller on the brake, the
                  Assistant Hammer Operator on the tight rope, and the Hammer Operator at the V-
                 door. Pull the rope down as far as it will go and ease off on the brake. Keep the trip
                rope pulled down until the latch engages the piston. With the trip rope still pulled
               down, ease up on the trip wire one foot. At this time release the trip rope. The
                  Assistant Hammer Operator must now clear the floor. Continue to ease up on the
                     trip sling. The Hammer Operator should take note that the lead slings are not
                hanging up around the lead and that the trip sling is taking the weight of only the
                   piston.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 110 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 470
                           Policy PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION
                      OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 5 of 6

          4.6.3  Watch the lead for any upward Movement, while the Driller watches the weight
                  indicator for gain. If a movement of the lead or a gain in weight is observed, pull
             down on the trip rope to stop the trip, then slack off of the trip sling and try again.
              With the piston moving up, the trip pawl lever will come past the stop for the pawl
                  lever releasing the piston. At this time the Driller should practice slacking off the
                brake to keep up with the conductor

             when the hammer is fired later. Observe the penetration with the dry blow. This
                procedure must be repeated until there is less than one inch of penetration per blow
                   for three consecutive dry blows. This is very critical to stop free falls or conductor
                  runs. Do not cut this detailed procedure short. When you are satisfied that the
                conductor is set and that there will be no more free movement, prepare to start the
              hammer. Install the combustion chamber plug. To set the fuel pump in No.1
                   position, pull one time on the right hand rope. Follow the same procedures when
                dry blows were delivered earlier. The Assistant Hammer Operator must leave the
                  floor before the piston is released. Only the Driller and Hammer Operator should
               be in the work area. When the piston is released the Driller must slack off the brake
                  to maintain slack in the lead slings. If the hammer fires while tension is on these
                  slings they are likely to break. The Hammer Operator should have the control ropes
                  in hand and be prepared to pull the stop rope. As the hammer fires, the penetration
               must be observed at all times, especially while driving the first joint. If any free
              movement  of  the conductor  is observed,  the hammer must be shut down
                immediately. Again, wait for ten minutes with the weight of the hammer on the
                 conductor. When no free movement is observed set the fuel pump to the "0"
                  position and deliver dry blow until there is less than one inch of penetration per
                blow, with no free movement. Do not remove combustion chamber plug during this
                   stage. Do not go up in the derrick. The conductor may fall at any time. When the
                conductor is secure, set the fuel pump to the No.1 position and start hammering.
              The conductor must again be observed for any free movement, which if noted, dry
               blows should be repeated. Continue driving in the No.1 position until the conductor
                  takes more than 100 blows per foot. With the piston down, move the fuel pump
                  control into the No.2 position. Continue driving until more that 200 blows per foot
                  are required. While the piston is down, move the fuel pump control into position
               No.3 and drive until 250 blows per foot are necessary. Then move the fuel pump
                  control into position No.4 and drive until 300 blows per foot are required. When
                    this point is reached, pull the stop rope and shut down the hammer. Follow the chart
                   for the proper blows per foot for different size conductors. When driving pipe,
                always assume a position on the floor to enable a quick shut down. Always stop the
             hammer by pulling the stop rope and always restart the hammer in the same fuel
             pump position.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 111 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 470
                           Policy PROCEDURES FOR THE SAFE OPERATION
                      OF DIESEL CONDUCTOR DRIVE HAMMERS

          4.6.4  When using elevators to run the conductor, always disconnect and lay down the
                  elevators before starting the hammer.
          4.6.5  When using padeyes to run the conductor, cut the padeyes off on the first joint that
                  the hammer will have to be fired upon, or until the conductor has no free fall when
                  driving. This will depend upon the free movement noted during dry blows. If the
                conductor goes through the rotary table, the rig can always be jacked up or down
                  to make the next connection. Exercise caution when removing the elevator or
                 padeyes.
          4.6.6  Pick up the hammer from the conductor and disconnect the slings from the
                 conductor. The conductor must be free standing when the pad eyes are cut. Use
              good judgment while observing this procedure. If the conductor is close to the
                  rotary table and free fall occurs,
                          it may be impossible to pick up the string to cut off the pad eyes. If so, jack down
              do not over pull.
          4.6.7  Offshore jackups must use the lower conductor guide with the proper size inserts.
            When running the conductor with make-up connections, be sure to remove the
                    inserts. When a connection passes though the guide, all pad eyes must be even with
                  the conductor so it will not hang up on the guide.
          4.6.8  When driving a conductor with couplings, a drive sub must be used. Do not connect
                  the drive sub to the hammer while driving the first joint until the conductor has no
                   free movement. The drive shoe can hang up if there is a free fall. This will put full
                weight of the string on the lead slings and they will break.
      4.7 Securing and Laying the Hammer Down

          4.7.1  Allow the hammer to cool down.
          4.7.2   Install the piston locking screw
          4.7.3   Install the hammer to lead locking plate
          4.7.4   Install the exhaust port caps
          4.7.5   Install the protective cover
          4.7.6  Inspect the lead for any cracks. Inspect the complete hammer for any damage and
                 note same for later repair.
          4.7.7  Cut off the conductor at the proper height and lay it down.
          4.7.8  Lay down the hammer in the reverse order of picking it up.

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES
        None

                                       Policy DRIVE PIPE ANGLE
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1of 1



  DRIVE PIPE ANGLE

   1.0   PURPOSE

        To establish procedures and guidelines for the installation of drive pipe in a true vertical
           position.

   2.0   SCOPE

         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
          operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

        The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring compliance with this policy.

   4.0   PROCEDURES

      4.1 In order to prevent unnecessary abnormal wear to casing, wellheads, B.O.P. stack, bell
           nipple, rotary bushings, swivel, top drive, and the drill string, drive pipe shall be as close
           to true vertical as possible when measured with a 4-foot long carpenter's bubble level.

      4.2 Upon tagging the sea floor with drive pipe and prior to commencing diesel hammer
          operations, the angle will be checked using a 4-foot long carpenter's bubble level. If the
          pipe is not true to vertical, it shall be picked up and reset into the mud line until true vertical
             is achieved. If allowed by the Client and operational parameters, the rig floor shall be
         skidded to achieve a true vertical orientation of the drive pipe when strong currents are
         encountered or when centering the drive pipe in a subsea template.


   4.0   RECORDS

        None

   5.0   REFERENCES

        None


────────────────────────────────────────────

                                Policy: MAXIMUM ANGLE OF BOP STACKS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1of 1



  MAXIMUM ANGLE OF BOP STACKS

        1. PURPOSE

        To establish a standard for the maximum allowable angle of BOP stacks.

        2. SCOPE

         This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

        3.  RESPONSIBILITIES

         The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring the requirements of this standard are
          complied with.

        4. PROCEDURES

       4.1 For surface BOP stacks, the maximum recommended offset angle is one (1) degree.

       4.2 BOP stack angles greater than one (1) degree that cannot be corrected, or when the Operator chooses
           not to take productive time required to reduce the angle of the BOP equal to or less than one (1)
           degree, the Operator shall be requested to accept (in writing) the responsibility for damage
           sustained to the BOP stack and related equipment as a result of continuing drilling operations
          without correcting this angle. A sample form letter is included on page 2 of this policy as a guideline
            to be used by the Rig Manager.

       4.3 In the event that the Operator Representative(s) refuse to accept responsibility the Rig Manager
         must refer this situation immediately through Operations chain of command to Vice President for
            resolution.

   5. RECORDS
     None

   6. REFERENCES
     None

  FUNCTION TEST 13 5/8” BOP AFTER RIG UP

   1.0   PURPOSE
          This work  instruction describes “Function Test 13 5/8” BOP  after  rig up” operation  for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS to assure that the operation is completed in a safe and effective
          manner.

   2.0   SCOPE
          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

            3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
                 implementing this procedure.
           3.2     Driller is responsible of leading this operation
           3.3     Drilling crew is responsible of performing this operation in accordance to the below stated
                    instructions

   4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Job Preparation and Tools:

      •  Communication Radios
      •   Flash Lights
      •   All Accumulator pressure gauges should be in good condition and be working properly
      •   All accumulator bottles must be charged
      •   All hydraulic lines must be installed and be properly secured
       4.2 Safety Procedures Required

      •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential dropped
           object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height, hand injury,
             slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side loading on lifting gear).
      •  Review work instructions, JRA, rig specific procedures and assure compliance with procedures
         from ADES Well Control Manual.

      •  Ensure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
           assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained between all
           crews.

    In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.

      •  Only Senior Mechanic is allowed to charge and pressure up the accumulator unit.
      •  Only Tool pusher is allowed to operate the accumulator unit.
      •  Crews must be clear of the BOP area till the final charging of the accumulator unit.
   Notes

       •  These work instructions are prepared by the team and can be changed to add more specific steps to
           provide the best possible instructions.
       •  They should be reviewed before each task to prevent any unsafe act or condition and to train the
          crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
       •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
       •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the JRA and
           capture best practices and lessons learned.
       4.3 Work Instructions

       1-  Charge the accumulator till fully required pressure is reached
              •  Make sure that the reading in the unit pressure gauges is the required
              •  Make sure that both pumps stop after charging
       2-  Visual inspection of the hydraulic lines from the outlet of the manifold till the BOP connection and
             fittings is a must to make sure there is no leak
       3-  Confirm that all lights in the remote panels on the rig floor and at the OIM office are working
           properly
       4- Open BOP 4 Way valves (Annular – Top Ram – Shear Ram – Bottom Ram – Kill HCR – Choke
        HCR)
       5-  Give instructions to the A/D to observe the BOP during the function test and report using
          communication radios
       6- DF / SDF must observe the function test at the rotary table and use flashlights if required
       7- DF/SDF to give instructions to the Driller to start function testing the TOP RAM
              •   Driller to close the top ram and confirm closing it to the DF / SDF.
              •  DF / SDF to observe the top ram till fully closed
              •   Driller is to communicate with the A/D to confirm that the function test is going well

  PICK UP AND RIH WITH PRODUCTION STRING

   1.0   PURPOSE

          This work instruction describes “Pick up and Trip Production String (13% CR Insp.)” operation for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS to assure that the operation is completed in a safe and effective
          manner.
   2.0   SCOPE

          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1 The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
          implementing this procedure.
       3.2 Driller is responsible of leading this operation
       3.3 Drilling crew is responsible of performing this operation in accordance to the below stated
            instructions

   4.0   PROCEDURES

           4.1     Safety Procedures Required
      •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential dropped
           object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height, hand injury,
             slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side loading on lifting gear).
      •  Review work instructions, JRA, Rig specific procedures and assure compliance with procedures
         from ADES Well Control Manual.
      •   Insure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
          assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained between all
           crews.
      •   In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.
   Notes

          •  These work instructions are prepared by the team and can be changed to add more specific
               steps to provide the best possible instructions.

                         Policy: PICK UP AND RIH WITH PRODUCTION STRING
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

          •  They should be reviewed before each task to prevent any unsafe act or condition and to train
               the crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
          •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
          •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the JRA and
               capture best practices and lessons learned.
       4.2 Procedures

       4.2.1   Prepare all handling tools (Slips – Spider Elevator – Third Part Tongs – Air Hoists – 2 ea.
               Single Joint Elevator – Shackles with Safety Pins – Double lifting Wire Slings – Safety Clamp
            – Swivel – Snubbing Line – Safety Valve according to tubing size) and make sure they are
                fully inspected by the DF / SDF and the AD before the Pick-up and RIH
       4.2.2  R/UP Third Party handling tools and tongs and make sure that they are in a good condition
       4.2.3   Pick up Artificial Lift Assembly as per program and make sure the proper slips and safety
             clamp used is the proper one for each joint
       4.2.4  R/UP Stabbing board and make sure it is in a good condition
                4.2.4.1 Make sure that there is a good communication between the person on the stabbing
                     board and the driller
       4.2.5  Good communication among driller, tong crew and Artificial Lift crew all the time while RIH.
       4.2.6   Pick up Tubing joint with single joint elevator from catwalk, and landing to the mouse hole
                (Drift all joint and make sure that the drift passed through and is outside of joint and use only
             one draft for the job).
       4.2.7   Latch the single joint elevator attached to Third Party spider Elevator to joint in mouse hole
            (Make sure the double lifting sling attached to spider elevator is clear from the rotary table to
              prevent it from being under the slips hand, which can result in lifting the slips from the master
              bushing while driller pick up the joint from the mouse hole to prevent string drop down well).
       4.2.8   Pick up the joint to the required position and slack off into string at rotary table and tie up with
              Third Party tong (Only Third Party operator to handling the task is not by drill crews).
       4.2.9   Slack off the TDS Till spider elevator passes under the top off tool joint, Stabbing board person
                to latch close position after confirming the latch center is clear from tool joint, Stabbing board
              person to give confirmation to driller.
       4.2.10 Remove the safety clamp, then the driller starts picking up and RIH, Make sure cable is clear
             and person operate the cable reel to slack off and keep an alert all the time to prevent any
               tension on the cable, third party to install securing cable clamp after covered the hole to avoid
              anything dropped in the hole.
       4.2.11 Land the joint in the slips, install safety clamp till have proper weight (The handling procedures
              should be followed as instructed above steps under supervision from SDF/DF and good alert
               via drill crews and deck crew till pick up Production Packer).

       4.2.12  Pick up packer with single joint elevator with assistance from Crane, Lifting up till passed V-
            Door safely.
       4.2.13  Slack off Packer joint and screw slowly into joint at rotary , Slack off spider elevator via person
            on stabbing board and latch the packer joint , Tie up with weather ford fully torque as required.
       4.2.14  Connect lower and upper QCI By third party.
       4.2.15  Continue pick up and RIH the reaming joints above the packer with the same procedures till
              p/up hanger assy.
       4.2.16  Pick up Tubing HGR assembly Connect lower QCI Make sure the correct direction before land
               the HGR.

       4.2.17  Pick up and make up landing joint screw into tubing HGR, Slack off and land HGR into T.H.F
            (Make sure HGR In center of the Bill nipple and BOP Stack).
       4.2.18  Screw and tie HGR lock down screw bolt Make sure HGR in Position, do not forget to install
          BPV inside hanger before N/D x-mass tree.
       4.2.19  General Housekeeping work place and clean storage all tools has been used

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES
        None

  POOH & LAY DOWN PRODUCTION STRING

   1.0   PURPOSE
          This work  instruction  describes “POOH & Lay Down  Production  String”  operation  for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS to assure that the operation is completed in a safe and effective
          manner.
   2.0   SCOPE
          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
                 implementing this procedure.
           3.2     Driller is responsible of leading this operation
           3.3     Drilling crew is responsible of performing this operation in accordance to the below stated
                    instructions

   4.0   PROCEDURES

   4.1     Safety Procedures Required
      •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential dropped
           object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height, hand injury,
             slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side loading on lifting gear).
      •  Review work instructions, JRA, Rig specific procedures and assure compliance with procedures
         from ADES Well Control Manual.
      •   Insure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
          assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained between all
           crews.
      •   In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.
   Notes
          •  These work instructions are prepared by the team and can be changed to add more specific
               steps to provide the best possible instructions.
          •  They should be reviewed before each task to prevent any unsafe act or condition and to train
               the crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
          •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
          •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the JRA and
               capture best practices and lessons learned.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 121 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                       Policy: POOH & LAY DOWN PRODUCTION STRING
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2of 2

       4.2 Procedures
           4.2.1   Prepare all handling tools (Slips – Spider Elevator – Third Party Tongs – Air Hoists – 2 ea.
                  Single Joint Elevator – Shackles with Safety Pins – Double lifting Wire Slings – Safety
               Clamp – Swivel – Snubbing Line – Safety Valve according to tubing size) and make sure
                  they are fully inspected by the DF / SDF and the AD before starting POOH.
           4.2.2  R/UP Third Party handling tools and tongs and make sure that they are in a good condition
           4.2.3   Pick up Landing joint from Derrick with single joint elevator using man riding winch and
                 lower the landing joint screw into tubing HGR ,Release single joint and latch the landing
                     joint with elevator( Make sure pin thread is good condition and clean lubricated with dope).
           4.2.4    Fill up the annulus with fluid; Check the bill nipple for any leak.
           4.2.5   Close Annular or ram and test BOP against hanger.
           4.2.6   Pull Production string & unset Production Packer in present of OIM & Co. Man on R/F,
                Observe the Annulus to check fluid level.
           4.2.7   After unset the Packer wait for 15 min. observing well then Pick up string till HGR Reach
                 Rotary table (SDF/DF to observe the HGR Till pass the riser and BOP, Bell nipple.
           4.2.8   Set slips, third party to remove cable clamp, Control line fitting and Elect. Cable plug,
               Unscrew the landing joint, HGR, Stand rack landing joint use man rider permit and check
                          list.
           4.2.9  POOH Production String, third party to remove elect. Cable, clamps, lay down each joint
                    to mouse hole make sure to install tubing protectors before lower tubing to mouse hole.
           4.2.10  After lower the joint in mouse hole pick up joint with single joint elevator to V.Door then
                    to cat walk (good communication between air winch operator, deck crew and reel operator
                   ,catwalk to be clear when L/D joint on V.Door, be sure of safety pin of single joint elevator
                       is in when in used).
           4.2.11  Continue Lay down tubing till reach production packer to drill floor, Unscrew packer and
                   lay down same using crane and air winch.
           4.2.12  Continue POOH Lay down tubing to mouse hole using single elevator , Person on Cable
                     reel to be alert all the time and to keep good communication with drill crew while drill pick
                up string.
           4.2.13 Use safety clamp once string Weight is low till last joint of the assembly.
           4.2.14  Continue POOH Follow up all above steps till Artificial Lift Assembly reach the drill floor.
           4.2.15 R/UP Reda pump handling tools make sure third party handling then disconnect each joint
                 used safety clamp all the time, Lay down Artificial Lift Assembly.
           4.2.16  General Housekeeping work place and clean storage all tools has been used
   5.0   RECORDS
        None
   6.0   REFERENCES
        None
