  NIPPLE DOWN X-TREE


   1.0   PURPOSE
          This work instruction describes “Nipple Down X-Tree” operation for ADVANCED ENERGY
       SYSTEMS to assure that the operation is completed in a safe and effective manner.
   2.0   SCOPE
          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
   4.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1 The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
      implementing this procedure.
       3.2 Driller is responsible for leading this operation
       3.3 Drilling crew is responsible for performing the below operations in accordance to the below stated
        instructions.

   4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Job Preparation and Tools:
      •   All access to production deck is prepared and secured, build up proper scaffolding if required
      •   All lifting tools to be proper secured and good condition
      •   Prepare all required tools Hammer wrench for X.Mass-tree
      •   Production wing valves Prepare Sledge hammer, Finger safer to installed in Hammer Wrench to
           prevent finger injury
      •   All flange ring gasket to be ready and good condition
      •   All bolts to be clean and lubricate
      •   All third party involved the task to be ready
      •   Collect good instruction from the Company man and keep good communication
       4.2 Safety Procedures Required
      •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential dropped
           object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height, hand injury,
             slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side loading on lifting gear).
      •  Review work instructions, JRA, rig specific procedures and assure compliance with procedures
         from ADES Well Control Manual.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 123 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 490

                                 Policy:  NIPPLE DOWN X-TREE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2of 3

      •  Ensure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
          assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained between all
           crews.
      •   In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.
   Notes

          •  These work instructions are prepared by the team and can be changed to add more specific
               steps to provide the best possible instructions.
          •  They should be reviewed before each task to prevent any unsafe act or condition and to train
               the crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
          •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
          •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the JRA and
               capture best practices and lessons learned.
       4.3 Procedures

         1.  Prepare all required tools (Hammer wrench 1 7/16” for X-Mass tree T.H.F and Production wing
           valves, Sledge hammer, Finger safer and ring gasket).
         2.  Erect Scaffolding around the X-Mass tree and make sure is good condition.
         3.  Bleed of well pressure TBG & ANNULUS and observe well for 15 min. to be sure no trapped Gas
          during the observation.
         4.  Mark HGR direction with clear visible paint.
         5.  Tie up flange into top of X-Mass tree, connect 2" weco fig 1502 and kill hose, Test the line before
              kill the well, Pressure test the tubing. (announce on clear call for pressure test).
         6.  R/up Wire line test lubricator against x-mass swab valve Open – SSD valve (Only AD to Close or
         open X-Mass tree).
         7.  Pump kill fluid into the tubing and annulus according to well program.
         8.  Observe the well be sure no flow or trapped pressure.
         9.   Install BPV into TBG hanger by short bar connected with running tools from wellhead level.
        10. Install lifting cap on top of X-Mass tree for lifting operation Disconnect all nuts secure X-Mass tree
            to prevent jump, pick up slowly air hoist remove the X-Mass tree in safe place and secured.
        11. Lift up the X-Mass tree to the main deck and good secured (test X-Mass tree before next rig up and
           report the test result to the company man)
        12. Very important to check the X-Mass tree HGR Bushing to be removed, and the HGR Thread is
           inspected and be sure good condition before landing the riser to T.H.S, make sure the well T.H.S
              is secured and Installed cover to prevent any drop tools into the well (make sure the electric plug
           of cable installed by third party) Cover the tubing HGR To prevent any drop tools inside the tubing.
        13. General Housekeeping work place and clean storage all tools has been used

  DRILL LINE SLIP AND CUT
   1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish slipping and cutting procedures for drilling line that will maintain the safest, most
          economical use of drilling line.

   2.0   SCOPE
      This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring that the drill line slipping and
           cutting procedures comply with this standard. Exceptions to this policy must submit in writing to
           the Vice President.

   4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Determine Rig Drilling Line Ton-Mile Target using the following:
               4.1.1  Ton Mile Target = Drilling Line Goal × Length to be cut
               4.1.2  How to get Drilling Line Goal:


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 129 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 495
                                  Policy: DRILL LINE SLIP AND CUT
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 3


               4.1.3  To determine length to be Cut following API RP 9B Regulation, use the following
                      Table:





               4.1.4   Determine your Accumulative Ton-Mile as per API RP 9B





       4.2 The lengths cut from drill line vary in proportion to ton-miles accumulated. Drill line should not be
           cut at the same range of ton-miles each time. This procedure will move the wear points and achieve
           the objective of spreading the wear along the entire length of the wire rope.

       4.3 The best cut-off program is one with the most consistent ton-mile per foot cut values. Long cuts
          can be avoided by maintaining the ton-mile goal.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 130 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 495

                                  Policy: DRILL LINE SLIP AND CUT
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 3


       4.4 Accurate calculations of ton-miles must be recorded for all phases of operations, including jarring
         and working the drill string, drag, and working pipe between connections.

       4.5 Careful visual inspection of the wire rope must take precedence over any predetermined
            calculations.

       4.6 There shall be Twelve (12) wraps remaining on the drum with the travelling assembly in its lowest
            position. The driller shall ensure that the line is wrapped tightly and is in proper position on the
           grooves.

                                        Policy: DRAWWORKS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  DRAWWORKS

   1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance on how the drawworks are maintained and
           operated.

   2.0   SCOPE
      This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) maintains the overall responsibility for the
                  planning and safety of the operation including the operation and maintenance of drawworks
                    in the drilling program.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the drill crew
                and drilling operation on the rig floor.

           3.3    The Driller is responsible for the condition and safe operation of the drawworks.

   4.0   GENERAL

           4.1    The drawworks (whether automatic or manual system is in place) shall be maintained in
                 accordance with manufacturer’s recommendations. Brake bands, linkage, equalizer bar,
                     calipers, and adjustment bolts shall not be welded.

           4.2    Only authorized personnel shall be permitted to operate the drawworks. Prospective
                     Drillers and Assistant Drillers in training shall be supervised by an experienced Driller at
                       all times. Non-ADES personnel are not authorized to operate the drawworks.

           4.3    The Driller shall not leave the drawworks unattended without having first shut off power
                    to the drawworks, activate the parking brake, and chain down the brake handle.

           4.4    The drawworks shall be inspected on a per-tour basis by the Driller coming on tour.
                   Inspection points for the drawworks include but are not limited to:

                    4.4.1   Crown-O-Matic setting

                    4.4.2  Crown and floor saver functions to be tested and operational

                    4.4.3   Brake adjustment; brake lever chain (rake blocks, linkage, pins, cotter keys, etc.,
                         should be checked for operating efficiency)


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 132 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 500
                                        Policy: DRAWWORKS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 3


                    4.4.4   Lubrication, pressure, and leaks or plugged jets

                    4.4.5   Spear connections packing at drum and auxiliary brake for leaks

                    4.4.6   Air leaks in pneumatic systems

           4.5     Attention shall be taken while cleaning the drill floor and equipment not to allow wash
                  water to get onto the drawworks brake and / or flanges.

           4.6    Rope, hose, chain, and slings shall not be left in the proximity of the drawworks; it is
                  discouraged that these items be used on the drill floor during tripping operations unless
               deemed necessary by the Driller.

           4.7     Personnel shall not be on the drill floor wearing full body harnesses with lanyards attached
                  while the drawworks is in operation.

           4.8    The Driller is responsible for the proper lubrication procedures of the drawworks and that
                   the equipment is serviced every tour. Improper lubrication of the drawworks can lead to
                 premature equipment failure.

           4.9     Defective air control valves, levers, regulators, rheostats, etc., should be reported promptly
                    for repairs.

          4.10   The Driller should not leave the Driller’s shack / cabin while drilling, even if the drawworks
                       is equipped with an automatic Driller, unless he is relieved by a competent replacement.

          4.11  No one should be left alone at the drawworks controls unless they have received previous
                    instructions in the operation of the equipment and are knowledgeable.

          4.12  Do not attempt to repair or adjust the drawworks without approval of the Driller.

          4.13   Report all defective grease and element fittings for replacement. Report any unusual sounds
                coming from the drawworks.

          4.14  Do not direct a water stream onto the brake bands when washing the drawworks.

   5.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Dynamic Brake)

           5.1    The dynamic brake shall not be engaged while “drilling down” by any other means than
                   the hand of the Driller. It is prohibited to attach weights to the dynamic brake actuating
                    lever to maintain it in an engaged position.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 133 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 500
                                        Policy: DRAWWORKS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 3


           5.2      If the dynamic brake is equipped with an overriding clutch mechanism, there shall be a
               means of positively locking the dynamic brake into engagement with the drawworks.
           5.3    The dynamic brake shall be engaged at all times a top drive is in use.

           5.4   Do not stand directly over the brake lever when engaging the drawworks.

   6.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Guarding)

           6.1     All guards, hatches, and covers shall remain secured in place on the drawworks while in
                   operation.

                    6.1.1  The drawworks should not be operated unless the guards are in place and secure,
                          except when slipping and cutting line where extreme caution must be used.

           6.2    Guards, hatches, and covers shall only be removed with power off, unless under planned
                 maintenance inspection or fault finding and controlled under the Permit to Work System.
           A Job Safety Analysis shall be conducted.

           6.3    Power shall not be turned on until guards, hatches, and covers are all in place.

           6.4       It is prohibited to stand on top of the drawworks or inside the main drum guard when the
                drums are moving.

   7.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Reeving)

           7.1    The fast line and the deadline shall not come into contact with equipment and derrick
               members other than the crown sheaves and line stabilizers.

           7.2    Lebus grooving shall be bolted rather than welded to the drum. Kickback rollers shall be
                changed out when significant wear is evident.

           7.3      If fitted, the fastline stabilizer (line guide) shall be in good condition, spare rollers shall be
                    available on the drill floor.

           7.4    Make adjustments on the drawworks: the power is shut off and the brake chained down.

   8.0   RECORDS
        None

   9.0   REFERENCES
           9.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)
           9.2   HSE 100.09, Energy Isolation (Lock-out / Tag-out)
           9.3   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 134 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 502

                  Policy: INSPECTION REQUIREMENTS FOR BRAKE BANDS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 2


  INSPECTION REQUIREMENTS FOR BRAKE BANDS

   1.0   PURPOSE
          This procedure describes inspection requirements for brake bands of ADES to guarantees that
          equipment is suitable and capable before use.

   2.0   SCOPE
      This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible for ensuring that all maintenance supervisory personnel are aware of
             this standard.

       3.2 The senior rig Mechanic at the rig is responsible for ensuring that the inspections specified in this
           standard are carried out.

       1.1 The Maintenance Manager is responsible for ensuring that the inspections specified in this
           standard are included in the maintenance work order instructions contained within the rig
           preventive maintenance system.

   4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

          Brake bands used on the following equipment shall be inspected by wet magnetic particle
           inspection (MPI) prior to each relining:

             •  Draw works
             •   All crane winch drums
             •  Anchor winches and windlasses
             •   All air and hydraulic hoists, overhead cranes and trolleys

          Note: Each brake band design may have particular weaknesses that would dictate how magnetic
            particle inspections are performed, and how to interpret the results. For example, brake bands on
         most all models of National draw works have riveted ends, and rarely fail in service. The most
        common area of failure is the first bolt hole from the end of the band. For most of the Oil well
         draw works we have, the brake band has welded ends that have failed in service. Therefore, the
        MPI needs be focused more on the ends of the band. Whenever there is any doubt, the entire
           surface of the bands listed above should be inspected to be certain there are no defects prior to re-
           qualifying them for continued use.

   5.0   RECORDS
         None.

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 9


  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE

   1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define operational and maintenance controls employed to operate
          with top drives, crowns, and traveling blocks.

   2.0   SCOPE
          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) maintains the overall responsibility for the
                  planning and execution of the drilling operation and maintenance of the drilling equipment.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for the execution of the well program as directed and safe
                   operation of the top drive and associated equipment.

   4.0   PROCEDURE (Safety Specifications)

           4.1    Bumper blocks / expanded metal cage to prevent falling objects shall be attached to the
                  underside of crown beams. They shall be encased in expanded metal along their full length
                and fastened to the derrick at both ends.

           4.2    The mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU) equipment shall be serviced as recommended
                by manufacturer. This servicing includes the block, crown, drawworks, etc.

           4.3     Picking up drill pipe or casing while drilling with top drive is prohibited on ADES
                     installations
                   unless MODU is set up for offline stand building.

   5.0   PROCEDURE (Top Drive)

           5.1    The  top  drive  system  shall  be  maintained  in  accordance  with  manufacturer’s
                 recommendations.

           5.2     All top drive controls on the control panel shall be labeled according to function. If the
                   elevator release control is located in the vicinity of any link tilt controls, the elevator release
                   control shall be covered or otherwise made fail safe while operating link tilt mechanism.

           5.3    The dynamic brake shall remain engaged at all times the top drive is in use.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 137 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 505

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 9


           5.4    During explosives handling and wire line activities involving explosives, power to the top
                   drive shall be isolated, locked out, and documented on a Permit to Work and associated
                 Job Safety Analysis.

           5.5    The rotary hose shall be snubbed off by means of a 7/8” safety cable or vendor-supplied
                    safety chain and clamp arrangement to the body of the top drive. The Driller is to ensure
                   the snub line is not connected to any high pressure hydraulic or drilling fluid lines on either
                   the top drive or the stand pipe ends.

           5.6   No Driller lacking top drive operation experience shall be allowed to operate the top drive
                  unsupervised. The OIM / Toolpusher or an experienced Driller shall remain at the controls
                  with the inexperienced Driller until such time that the OIM  / Toolpusher feels direct
                   supervision is no longer necessary.

           5.7    There shall be no modifications done to the guarding components on the top drive. Hot
               work to be conducted on the top drive must be approved through the Management of
               Change (MOC) process.

           5.8    The Driller must be aware of clearances between the top drive and equipment in the derrick
                  while raising and lowering.

                    5.8.1  The link tilt shall not be extended while moving the top drive past the monkey
                          board.

                    5.8.2  The Driller shall observe the movement of the top drive on the camera monitor
                         while the top drive is positioned at or traveling past the monkey board.

           5.9    During personnel hoisting activities in the derrick, power to the top drive shall be shut off.
               Power to the drawworks shall be shut off and the drawworks parking brake shall be
                    activated as per isolation procedure under the permit to work.

          5.10  No work shall be done within the confines of the derrick other than monkey board activities
                by the Derrickman while the top drive is in operation.

          5.11   Cement, acid, and liquid nitrogen shall not be pumped through the top drive. The potential
                    for damage to equipment is too great to allow this.

          5.12     If the top drive is connected to the drill sting while a cement head or side entry sub is in
                    use, power to the top drive shall be switched off whenever chicksans or any other lines are
                  being connected or removed to prevent accidental rotation. Ensure pressure on the IBOP
                  valve from the mud pumps while cementing to prevent cement coming into the Top Drive
                System (TDS).


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 138 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 505

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 3 of 9


          5.13   Personnel shall not position hanging equipment such as tongs or mud buckets in such as a
                   position that the hanger line interferes by making contact with top drive components
                   including service loop members as the top drive moves up and down in the derrick.

          5.14   The Driller and the drill crew are directed to ensure that the movement of pipe being hoisted
                    into the derrick from the V-door (or pipe transfer conveyor [PTC]), for placement in the
                mousehole is controlled at all times in such a manner as to eliminate the possibility of the
                  top drive coming into contact with said pipe during its descent.

          5.15   The Driller shall ensure that the link tilt mechanism has been released before picking up
                   the string from the slips.

          5.16   The drill crew and Driller are to remain aware of the danger zone between the rotary and
                   the mouse hole while extending the link tilt at floor level.

          5.17   The top drive should have a magnetic particle examination of the exposed surfaces on all
                  load bearing components annually to determine the presence of fatigue crack indications.

          5.18   The Driller shall make a tourly check of the top drive including, but not limited to the
                  following checkpoints:

                   5.18.1  Torque limit settings

                   5.18.2  Proper functioning of the inside blowout preventer (IBOP)

                   5.18.3  Hydraulic and / or pneumatic leaks

                   5.18.4  Loose connections

                   5.18.5  Clearance of lines and equipment in the derrick

          5.19   The Driller shall check the top drive for tools and parts that may have been left on the top
                   drive anytime maintenance work has been performed on the top drive.

          5.20   The Driller shall check the top drive for loose connections and potential dropped objects
                     after the completion of jarring activities

          5.21   The drill crew shall inspect the top drive for loose connections, potential dropped objects,
                  equipment, and line clearance during tripping operations.

          5.22   The service loop of a top drive shall be wrapped with a cover that shall protect all electrical
                  wiring and hydraulic lines. The service loop must not rub against any object that shall cause
                wear on the cover of the service loop itself.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 139 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

   6.0   PROCEDURE (Crown and Traveling Blocks)

           6.1    Ensure that the opening in the crown runaround is fitted with a self-closing door at the
                crown level.

           6.2     All crown areas shall be equipped with adequate lighting on the crown for night work.

           6.3   ADES standard hand rails and toe boards shall be affixed to the crown areas where a person
                       is expected to work.

           6.4   An operational red strobe light shall be mounted on the crown for aviation purposes and
                    tested during annual safety inspections at a minimum.

           6.5     All lights, sheaves, and equipment mounted on and around the crown shall possess a safety
                  chain or cable that is secondary fall protection.

                    6.5.1   Ensure that every traveling block hook, other hook, or open link suspended from a
                            traveling block to which an elevator, elevator link, swivel bail, or other equipment
                                 is either directly or indirectly attached, including the open hooks or links of such
                           attached equipment, shall be equipped with a safety latch or device that shall
                         provide a complete and secure closed hook or link.

                    6.5.2   Every traveling block, traveling block hook, elevator, elevator link, and other
                            similar item of traveling equipment shall be reasonably free of projecting bolts,
                             nuts, pins, or other parts on which the clothing of employees may be caught or that
                   may foul the Derrick hand, other equipment, or material in the derrick. It should
                       be checked when servicing the equipment.

                    6.5.3  Crew personnel shall ensure that all lines, ropes, etc., in the derrick are secured in
                        such a manner that they cannot become entangled in the traveling blocks or drill
                              line while the blocks are in motion.

           6.6    Each time a Derrickman is in the derrick servicing the crown, turnbuckles, anchor bolts,
                      etc. shall be checked for security against falling objects. The Derrickman should inspect
                  everything in the derrick while ascending and descending.

           6.7     Traveling block sheave guards should be securely fastened to the traveling block to prevent
                them from becoming accidentally dislodged under the most severe operating conditions.

                    6.7.1  The sheaves of a traveling block should have guards that shall prevent any person
                           or thing from making accidental contact with the sheaves or with the nip point
                       where the hoisting lines run onto and off the sheaves.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 140 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 505

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 5 of 9


                    6.7.2   Report any issues to appropriate supervisor.

           6.8    The block and crown shall be serviced as per manufacturer’s recommendation.

           6.9    Sheave protection must be installed in the event of sheaves failure or breakage.

          6.10   The drawworks brake should be chained or anchored down before a person climbs into the
                crown for any reason.

          6.11   The Derrickman should be diligent when hooking and unhooking his safety belt at the
                crown for any service work.

                   6.11.1 The Derrickman should have proper personnel protective equipment (PPE) and
                         equipment, especially if the weather is wet or icy. He should have an extra safety
                          lanyard to hook up for a 100% tie-off when he unhooks from the climbing assist.

                   6.11.2 When climbing up / down to the crown, it is not permitted to slide or skip runs.

          6.13    All items that are carried up should be noted in the Derrick Log and have a safety line
                hooked to tool, grease gun, etc.

          6.14   Make sure when traveling up / down the ladder that your tools don’t hang up on the ladder
                  rungs that could cause it to break and fall, or make you miss a rung going up or down.

          6.15   The Driller shall remain consistent while running the traveling block. The block should be
                  run as smoothly as possible to avoid striking the derrick or mast members in their ascent
                   or descent.

          6.16   The Driller shall not attempt running the traveling blocks in the derrick during high winds
                   unless traveling block guides are used.

   6.17    All equipment must be inspected before use and verified.

           6.17.1  Safety keepers in all shackles.

           6.17.2 Tong dies are in good shape and are secured properly and shall not fall out.

           6.17.3 Snub line is in good condition and there are no broken strands; snub lines must have
                  thimbles at each anchor point.
           6.17.4  All shackles must be four-part and must be made from Crosby or equivalent. Foreign-made
                  shackles and materials are not permitted.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 141 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 505

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 6 of 9


           6.17.5  All slip dies are in operational shape, and not packed with mud. The handles are in
                   operational shape, not bent or cracked, and the safety pins are secured.

           6.17.6 When picking up a drive hammer with a top drive you should unpin the top drive from the
                  torque guide / track to hoist the hammer up into the derrick, and then do the same when
                   laying out the drive hammer. The crane should assist in performing this task. In either case,
                  a risk assessment is to be conducted and documented.

           6.17.7 When making a connection with a top drive, the backup tongs are needed.

   7.0   PROCEDURE (Crown-O-Matic)

           7.1     This procedure covers: routine drilling operations, by-passing the safety device, testing the
                   device, and modifications to the device on any MODU operated by ADES.

           7.2    Running the traveling blocks into the crown section or floor is an extremely high potential
                   hazard. The results could be catastrophic to personnel and equipment.

           7.3    During operations which involve the movement of the traveling blocks, the Crown-O-
                 Matic must be active and functioning properly at all times.

           7.4   CAUTION: If the Crown-O-Matic has been disabled for any reason (including repairs,
                    cutting drilling line, etc.), a sign with a statement such as “Crown-O-Matic Disabled” must
                 be placed in clear view on the Driller’s console. The sign must also be in appropriate
                   language(s).

           7.5   When a tour change or change of control of the rig takes place with a top drive system, the
                Crown-O-Matic must be tested as soon as well conditions allow.

           7.6   NOTE: The crown-o-matic test must be documented on the daily drilling report.

           7.7    While cutting drilling line, the Crown-O-Matic must be disabled to avoid inadvertent
                    activation of the Crown-O-Matic. Otherwise, the Crown-O-Matic must be installed and
                working at all times.

           7.8   NOTE: The specific height of the “trip setting” shall vary from rig to  rig. Proper
                   consideration must be given by supervisory personnel to determine the specific setting
                   height needed.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 142 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 505

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 7 of 9


           7.9   When the Crown-O-Matic  is momentarily bypassed,  i.e., working long stands, the
                 immediate supervisor of the person performing the bypass must be notified and must
                 approve the bypass. A Permit to Work and Job Safety Analysis must be raised.

          7.10     If modifications to the configuration of the Crown-O-Matic take place, i.e., shortening the
                    toggle, disconnecting the air line, or changing out the air cylinder, the MOC procedure
                must be followed.

          7.11   The Crown-O-Matic shall be tripped and reset as follows:

                   7.11.1 The Driller shall engage the clutch and pick up on the blocks until the drill line
                           contacts the toggle and sets off the Crown-O-Matic.

                   7.11.2  Note: Keep out of the line of movement of the brake handle as the actuator will set
                           the brake.

                   7.11.3  Trip the release switch and raise the brake handle to reset the actuator cylinder on
                           the brake linkage and slack off the line until it clears the Crown-O-Matic.

                   7.11.4 The Driller shall lower the brake handle and the Crown-O-Matic is reset. Then
                             reactivate the Crown-O-Matic with the release switch.

          7.12   The Crown-O-Matic shall be tripped and reset after the following operations:

                   7.12.1  After slipping or cutting drill line.

                           7.12.1.1 The clearance between the brake band rollers and the brake band itself
                                should be checked.
                           7.12.1.2 Check the clearance on the weight indicator.
                   7.12.2  After handling long, bottom hole assembly (BHA) components.

                   7.12.3  After any other events when the settings have been changed.

                   7.12.4 A full function test should be done based on the regular maintenance schedule
                            (daily under the drawworks planned maintenance task).

   8.0   PROCEDURE (Hanging the Block)

            8.1.    Send a Derrickman up in a riding belt with safety lanyard to a static line to connect the
                hang lines to the traveling block. If basket is available, consider this as primary access.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 143 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                          Policy No. OPS 505

                     Policy:  CROWN, TRAVELLING BLOCK AND TOP DRIVE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 8 of 9


           8.2    Once connected, the person on the blocks shall move away from the area by swinging to a
                   side of the derrick or mast to view the block setting onto the hanging line.

           8.3    The Driller shall partially rest the weight of the traveling blocks on the hang lines.

           8.4    Once stopped, the person on the riding belt shall return to the top of the traveling blocks
                and inspect the connection of the hang lines.

           8.5    Once the inspection is carried out, the person shall be lowered to the rig floor.

           8.6     After the person is on the rig floor and clear of the area beneath the traveling blocks, the
                 remainder of the block’s weight shall be rested on the hang line.

           8.7     Slack off weight onto lines.

           8.8    Check shackles/pins on top of blocks. Check lines.
           8.9    Cut off desired length and reinstall line to drum, tightening fast line clamp correctly and
                   assuring that clamp is seated correctly in clamp taper, or if wedge types make sure the
               wedge is in properly before spooling up drill line.

          8.10   Pick-up and spool excess fast line. Pick blocks up to tighten line on drum.

          8.11   Slack off weight again onto hang line.

          8.12   Loosen dead line clamp bolts to allow easy slipping of new line.

          8.13    Slip line while pulling with drawworks. Do not lift blocks completely from hang lines.

          8.14   When correct amount of line is slipped, make sure that the block weight is all onto the hang
                     line and tighten dead line anchor clamp bolts. Ensure that studs have not backed out of
                 clamp.

          8.15   Pick-up blocks off the hang line. Check fast line and dead line anchor clamps and re-tighten
                         if necessary.

          8.16   Remove hang line and secure to side of derrick.

          8.17    Roll up excess new line on spool to take up slack drill line between drill line anchor and
                       drill line spool.

          8.18   Discard old drilling line remnants appropriately.

           9.2    Rig Equipment Servicing, Oracle or PMS system as applicable, indefinitely

                            Policy:  CROWN BLOCK SHEAVE GUARDS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  CROWN BLOCK SHEAVE GUARDS

   1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide a standard for the fitting of sheave guards on crown blocks.

   2.0   SCOPE
          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
           operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring:

               3.1.1 The crown block is fitted with sheave guards in accordance with this standard.

               3.1.2 Inspections to the crown block are carried out at appropriate intervals to ensure the
                     clearance between the sheave and the sheave guards are in accordance with this
                    standard

           3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible through the normal chain of command up to and including
               the V.P. Operations for ensuring that all crown blocks are fitted with sheave guards in
              accordance with this standard for all rigs under their supervision.

   4.0   PROCEDURES (General)
           4.1 All crown blocks shall be fitted with sheave guards meeting the requirements of this standard.
            The arrangement of sheave guards will vary with individual installations, but the following
               general specifications apply to all crown block sheave guards:

               4.1.1 The clearance between the rope guards and the sheaves, as well as the side plate and the
                   sheaves shall be no greater than 3/8".

               4.1.2 A minimum of 3 each rope guards made of 2-inch, schedule 80 pipe shall be located
                   approximately as shown below on a conventional crown block:


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 146 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 507

                            Policy:  CROWN BLOCK SHEAVE GUARDS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2





                4.1.3 For split crowns, rope guards and side plates shall be arranged for each sheave or
                   combination of sheaves such that the same intent shown above for conventional crown
                    blocks is achieved.

                4.1.4 A side-plate shall be incorporated with the rope guards. The side-plate should be made
                     of V2" plate, and fully welded to the top of the crown block structure, as well as the 2-
                    inch pipe rope guards as shown above. The edge of the side plate should extend at least
                   2" further out from the edge of the sheave.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 509

                     Policy:  OPERATION AND USE OF CROWN-O-MATIC
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 2


  OPERATION AND USE OF CROWN-O-MATIC

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy regarding the use and operation of the Crown -O-Matic device.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager to ensure that the testing and operation of the
          Crown-O-Matic device is in compliance with this standard.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

           4.1 The Crown-O-Matic shall be tested daily by each Driller and witnessed by the senior drilling
                supervisor. This should be done immediately upon the beginning of each tour. An entry shall
             be made on the IADC Report - "Crown-O-Matic OK". Exceptions to this are as follows:
                 •   Stuck pipe
                 •  BOP's closed
                 •   Wire-line work
                 •  Running casing
         Whenever any of the above are in progress the test will be postponed only until completion of the
           job in progress

           4.2 If for any reason the Crown-O-Matic device does not trip at the intended setting or is tripped
                inadvertently, the operation must be stopped, and an investigation conducted to determine the
               cause. Once the cause is determined, repairs, replacements or reactivation can be carried out.
            The Crown-O-Matic must then be tested in accordance with the procedures specified below
                to the satisfaction of the OIM/PIC. These actions shall be documented in a report to the Rig
             Manager.

           4.3 Before the Crown-O-Matic is disabled in order to conduct special or unusual operations, such
               as handling extra-long BHA's, etc., the OIM/PIC shall be contacted, and he shall supervise a
              Job Risk Assessment to be carried out by the drilling crew members involved. The senior
                 drilling department supervisor shall be stationed on the rig floor, and shall assume full
                responsibility for supervising all special or unusual operations until such time as the Crown-
             O-Matic is reactivated, tested and reset in accordance with the procedures specified, and
             normal operations are resumed.

           4.4 A pre-determined setting must be determined, taking into consideration the physical
             measurements of the equipment involved, and the average length of the drill string sections
              being handled.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 148 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 509

                     Policy:  OPERATION AND USE OF CROWN-O-MATIC
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2


           4.5 Send the Derrickman to the top of the derrick to observe the blocks and note and measure
            when the Crown-O-Matic trips, or when the blocks may be approaching an interference.

           4.6 Raise the blocks slowly until Crown-O-Matic trips or until the blocks are above the
              predetermined elevation.

           4.7 If the device trips, check the measurements to reassure the correct distance is being
               maintained.

           4.8 If the device does not trip, make the necessary repairs. If the repairs cannot be made
               immediately, warn everyone the Crown-O-Matic is not in operation, and post a sign on the
             draw works.

           4.9  When repairs are made, recheck the device completely and recheck all measurements.

           4.10  Reset and test the Crown-O-Matic each time the drill line is slipped or cut.
       4.11       Adherence to these guidelines is mandatory. Failure to do so could result in serious
            injury or significant damage to equipment. Consequently, persons not properly observing these
           guidelines are subject to disciplinary action, including termination of employment.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

[ صفحه 149 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                                            Policy: Tongs
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 6


  TONGS
    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the controls that are required to ensure the proper handling
          and maintenance of rig tongs.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) maintains the overall responsibility for proper operation
         by working with the crews to ensure equipment is maintained and utilized in the appropriate
          manner.

    4.0   PROCEDURE (Safety Considerations)

           4.1    Ensure the proper size safety / backup lines are attached to the power tongs and that all
                 clamps / shackles used are of correct strength.

           4.2    Crew members are prohibited from standing in the danger areas of power tong operations,
                    in front or behind.

           4.3    Crew members are prohibited from straddling hoses or lines attached to power tongs.

           4.4    Check oil in power tongs daily.

           4.5    Check torque gauge cylinder for proper fluid level.

           4.6    Hang power tongs either on mud bucket, air hoist, or tong hang line.

           4.7     Attach safety/back up line to power tongs and inspect line for condition.

           4.8    Hook up hydraulic hoses to quick connect pipe fittings on tongs.

           4.9    Make sure other end is connected to hydraulic unit.

           4.10   Check tongs for alignment and safety latch.

           4.11   Never operate unit without latch closed.

           4.12   Never leave power tongs unattended while running.

           4.13    Set up proper work scaffolding for operator and secure the scaffold.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 150 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 510

                                            Policy: Tongs
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 6


           4.14   Only trained designated personnel, verified through applicable competency assurance
                     training, can operate the power tongs.

           4.15   Check the position of all crew and other equipment before operating tongs.

           4.16   Adjust power tong height with the hydraulic adjustment cylinder as required.

           4.17   Ensure the proper torque is applied to each joint.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (General)

           5.1    Tongs shall not to be modified or repaired in the field. This includes extending the tong
                   hanger, welding additional handles to the tong arm, welding steel to tongs to add weight,
                   or anything else that changes the nature of the set of tongs.

           5.2     All persons expected to work with drill pipe tongs shall first be familiarized with their care
                 and handling. This includes all tong accessories.

           5.3    Breakout lines shall be at least ¾” in diameter, Snub lines or safety lines shall be at least
                 1” in diameter. Note: the size of the line is to be specified by the original equipment
                  manufacturer (OEM) and be the appropriate size for the tongs in use.

           5.4    The following should be used as a guideline to replace wire rope when broken wires are
                    noticed. Replace rope if there are:

                    5.4.1   6 or more broken wires in one lay

                    5.4.2   3 or more broken wires in one strand, in one lay

           5.5    Snub lines shall always be left in place during operation. Jerk lines and snub lines shall be
                  purpose-designed with factory-applied eyes.

           5.6    The Driller is responsible for ensuring that drill pipe tongs are inspected and maintained
                on a daily basis (or as used on automated rigs), tong operators must:

                    5.6.1  Keep tongs clean and lubricated.

                    5.6.2  Keep tong head at proper level through use of adjusting mechanisms.

                    5.6.3  Know when and how to properly change out tong dies.

                    5.6.4   Maintain proper weight in the counterbalance for ease of raising and lowering.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 151 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 510

                                            Policy: Tongs
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 6


                    5.6.5  Know how to change out tong jaws and jaw extensions for various diameters of
                            pipe.

                    5.6.6   Ensure that the tong counterbalance line is in good condition.

                    5.6.7   Ensure that the tong counterbalance line is not interfering with traveling equipment
                             in the derrick.

                    5.6.8   Ensure that the counterbalance line is not rubbing against derrick members or
                        equipment installed in the derrick.

                    5.6.9   Inspect snub lines, jerk lines, jerk chains, and automatic tong lines for signs of
                       wear and corrosion.

                   5.6.10  Ensure tong head bolts are equipped with keepers.

                   5.6.11 Check for cracks in the tong jaws and jaw extensions.

           5.7    Tong operators must not make horizontal adjustments to the tong head by inserting tong
                    dies or other pieces of metal below the horizontal adjusting screw on the tong hanger.
                  Note: this is not only a potential pinched or broken finger; it is also a potential piece of
                  metal in the hole.

           5.8    Tong operators must not  adjust the balance of the tongs by adding  steel  to the
                   counterbalance weight (unless it is purposely designed). Most counterbalance weights are
                   adjusted by adding or releasing water from them.

           5.9    Tong operators must not place tools such as collar clamp wrenches and sounding hammers
                on the tong arm. The collar clamp wrench and / or hammer may end up down the hole.
                Always place hand tools used on the drill floor away from the rotary table.

           5.10   Tong operators must not change tong dies wearing only safety glasses. When changing out
                  tong dies safety goggles shall be utilized.
                  Note: the carbide steel of a tong die is very brittle, when a piece is chipped loose of the
                body of the die it travels with tremendous velocity, the minimal eye protection offered by
                     industrial safety glasses is not sufficient to fully protect one’s eyes while using a die punch
                 and hammer to change out tong dies.

           5.11   Tong operators must not work with tongs without making a physical inspection of the drill
                  pipe tongs.

           5.12   Manual Drill Pipe Tongs are to be equipped with a tieback line to stow the tongs away
                from the work area of the drill floor when not in use. The tieback line should be constructed


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 152 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 510

                                            Policy: Tongs
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 6


           of 3/8” steel chain or wire line. Rope or synthetic slings are not permitted due to their sensitivity to
                  weather and hydrocarbons.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Tong Operation)

           6.1    Tong operators must ensure the tongs are in safe condition. The safety lines should stay on
                    the tongs while the tongs are in operation.

           6.2    Tong operators must place their hands only on the tong handles.

                    6.2.1  The handles are the only areas of the tongs where it is safe to place ones hands.

                    6.2.2  Tong Operators should latch the tongs using their hands by placing their hands in
                            the protective handle.

                    6.2.3  When tension is placed on the tong, the person operating the latch shall remove his
                        hands until the tension on the tongs has been released.

           6.3     Drill pipe tongs must not be placed on drill pipe that is in motion.

                    6.3.1   Tongs shall be applied only when the slips are set and the string weight is off the
                         hook, suspended by the slips.

                    6.3.2  Tong Operators should put the tongs on after the slips are set, not while being
                           pulled out of the hole.

           6.4   Two sets of tongs are required to make up or break out a connection, other methods such
                   as rotating the pipe with the break out tongs attached is shock loading the snub line and is
                    prohibited.
           6.5    Locking the rotary table and using one set of tongs to make up connections, depending on
                    the string weight to keep the slips from turning is not allowed.

           6.6   When tongs are applied, the bottom set of tongs should go into place first then the top set.
                  This reduces chances of pinched fingers while applying the tongs.

           6.7    The Tong Operators shall apply tension to the snub lines by pushing the tongs into position
                 and “making them bite”.

                    6.7.1   At this time the tong operators must step away from the tongs, standing outside the
                            arc of the tong arms.

                    6.7.2  The Driller shall not apply tension to the tongs until all are clear.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 153 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 510

                                            Policy: Tongs
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 5 of 6


           6.8    The Driller shall never work faster than the least experienced crew member is capable. The
                     Driller should not operate the cathead pulling the tongs faster than his crew can function
                     safely.

           6.9     In the event a connection must be broken or made up above the normal working level of
                    the rig floor, the tongs may be hoisted into position and applied; however, the operator
                     shall be lowered to the floor before tension is applied to the tongs.

                    6.9.1    It is never permissible for a tong operator to be supported by the tong line or by
                       any line lifting the tongs into a position.

                    6.9.2  When a connection is to be broken and it is above normal working height in the
                              rotary, the Tong Operator should not remain on the tong to work it once it is hoisted
                             into the air.

                    6.9.3  The Tong Operator should not ride a tong that is being hoisted into the air to break
                         a “tall connection”.

                    6.9.4  Once tongs are latched onto a connection for breaking out or making up, no one
                         should stand between the tongs. Tongs can backlash causing a scissor effect.

                    6.9.5  A bar should not be used to open the latch on the tong.

                    6.9.6  When breaking out a galled or tight break, the tong can be double-lined or use an
                       EZ-Torque if equipped. The Driller should remain at the cathead or control and
                             floor personnel should move away from the area.
           6.10    In the event it is necessary to “double line” a set of tongs to break out a connection, the
                     Driller shall clear personnel from the floor.

           6.11   Both Tong Operators should coordinate their moves with those of the Driller and practice
                    perfecting the routine.

           6.12   While the tongs are being operated on the pipe, each Floorman should avoid standing
                 between the Driller and the pipe connection at the rotary. The Driller should always be able
                    to see the tong’s movements.

           6.13   During back-torqueing of pipe, the person operating the tongs should remain outside of the
                 swing circumference. This can best be achieved by using a soft line to hold the tong in
                     position. Once the back torqueing is completed, the Tong Operator shall get clear of the
                    rotary area.

           6.14   Tong torque gauges shall be used when single- and double-lining tongs to prevent
                 exceeding tong rating.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 154 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 510

                                            Policy: Tongs
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 6 of 6


    7.0   PROCEDURE (Tong Maintenance)

           7.1    Ensure that counterbalances are balanced with the proper amount of water or acceptable
                   weights. Make sure that if putting weights on, they are designed and fit for purpose.

           7.2    Tong lines must be checked every tour to ensure that they remain in good condition and
                    are properly connected at both ends.

           7.3    Ensure tong dies remain in good condition for prompt biting action by cleaning them.

           7.4    Ensure tong die slots remain in good condition and die pins and cotter keys are present to
                  avoid dies falling out the bottom of the tong and falling in the hole.

           7.5    Each tong jaw hinge should be properly lubricated daily for movement.

           7.6    Ensure each tong jaw hinge is in good condition, in place, and keyed so the pin shall not
                work out.

           7.7    Ensure the jaws are free of cracks or breaks.

           7.8    The tong jaws should not contain excessive wear which shall cause them to fly open when
                   tension is applied.

           7.9    The jerk lines should be in good condition and properly sized.

           7.10   The safety lines should be in good condition and properly sized.

           7.11   Ensure all wire rope is of correct size for the work being done.

           7.12   Keeper pins in the rear of the tongs should be in good condition with safety key attached.

           7.13   Ensure each line attached to the tong is placed the proper way for going in or out of the
                    hole.

           7.14   Ensure tiebacks are available to secure the tongs aside when not in use or when moving
                     rigs such as a moveable offshore-type unit.

    8.0   RECORDS
         None

    9.0   REFERENCES
         None

[ صفحه 155 از 259 ]

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 515

                                            Policy:  SLIPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 4


   SLIPS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the measures and controls employed to operate and maintain
              slips.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore  Installation Manager OIM/PIC are responsible for implementing  this
                  procedure during their assigned tour.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the drill crew
                 and drilling operation on the rig floor.

           3.3    The Driller is responsible for the condition and maintenance of the slips including their
                    selection and use.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1     Slips size must match the diameter of the drill pipe, this is not only dependent on slip body
                    size but on die insert size as well. Ample slips and dies shall be maintained on board to
                 conform to the various diameters of drill pipe and tubing required by well programs

           4.2     Slips shall be maintained in good working condition by the drill crew; the drill crew should
                     periodically, and always before tripping, check the drill pipe slips to ensure:

                    4.2.1   Handles must not be welded, broken, or deformed. Each handle must be attached
                             to slip body by means of a pinned keeper.

                    4.2.2   Slip bodies and webs shall be examined for signs of cracks or deformity.

                    4.2.3   Die inserts must be in good condition and wearing evenly. Uneven wear patterns
                             in the slip dies may indicate deformed slip bodies or problems with the removable
                         bushings or the master bushing.

                    4.2.4   Die keepers and bolts must be of proper type and maintained tight. Inspect slips
                            die keepers and dies at a frequent interval.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 156 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 515

                                            Policy:  SLIPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 4


                    4.2.5   Slip body hinge pins shall be in good condition. Check to see that wear of the
                          hinges is not excessive or to the point that the slips shall not stand evenly when set
                      on a level surface.

                    4.2.6   Note: always replace full die insert sets; replacing individual dies or mismatching
                            dies creates high points in the gripping area which shall cause damage in the slip
                           area of the drill pipe!!

                    4.2.7   Note: should a die keeper come loose while tripping, dies might be easily lost down
                            the hole.

           4.3    The gripping surface of the slips must be kept clean. A wire brush shall be kept at the drill
                     floor for cleaning dies on slips and tongs.

                    4.3.1   Cleaning the dies shall occur as frequently as necessary to keep them gripping
                             firmly.

                    4.3.2   Note: always make sure that slips and tongs are away from the rotary table when
                           cleaning dies. You don’t want to lose the brush down the hole!

           4.4    The slip body surface that contacts the split bushings must be kept lubricated as per OEM
                 recommendations to prevent metal-to-metal contact of the split bushings and the slip
                    bodies.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Drill Pipe Rotary Slips)

           5.1     All persons expected to work with drill pipe (rotary) slips shall first be familiarized their
                   care and handling.

           5.2    Using proper lifting techniques: at least two crew members should pull slips and use legs
                    to lift and lower slips.

           5.3   When setting the slips coming out of the hole, the crew must not act until the string has
                  stopped its upward ascent. At that time the crew should place the slips around the string in
                    the split bushing; the slips shall grip the string as the Driller then slacks off on the hook.

                    5.3.1  Do not allow the slips to “ride” the pipe while pulling pipe. This action causes
                       undue and uneven wear on the die inserts and the drill pipe.

                    5.3.2  Do not use feet to kick the slips in the split bushing. Keep feet on the floor. Always
                             pull slips to the pipe; it is not safe to push slips toward pipe.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 157 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 515

                                            Policy:  SLIPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 4


           5.4   When setting the slips going in the hole, the drill crew must not act before the Driller has
                    signaled.

                    5.4.1  The drill string must be moving slowly, coming to a stop when the crew pulls the
                               slips to the string.

                    5.4.2  The crew then lowers the slips as the Driller is stopping the string. The crew times
                               this step so the string descent stops as the slips are set.

                    5.4.3  When lifting and lowering slips, be sure to use proper lifting techniques. Use the
                              legs, not the back, to lift and lower.

                    5.4.4   Position hands on handles with thumbs up handles. The pinch point that can be
                       formed between the bottom of the elevators and the slip handles is the greatest
                         hazard while setting slips going in the hole

                    5.4.5  Once again, pull the slips to the string. One person pushing the slips by the rear
                         handle can easily cause the slips to tip forward placing the person’s hand directly
                             in the path of the descending elevators, ensuring a pinch point.

           5.5   When pulling slips coming out of the hole, at least two crew members shall use proper
                       lifting techniques, grasp the handle of the slips, and pull only when the pipe begins to
                   ascend. Raise the slips no higher than necessary to place them on the floor and pull away
                from the split bushing.

           5.6   When pulling slips going in the hole, the same routine is followed as for coming out.
                However, the Driller shall only lift the string enough to clear the slips for pulling. The crew
                must be ready and pull uniformly on the slips.

           5.7   When using automatic slips, follow manufacturers’ guidelines.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Collar Slips and Safety Clamps)

            6.1    Collar slips are multi-bodied slips used for much larger diameter tubulars than the drill
           pipe slips.
                 Diameter of the slips is adjusted by adding or removing slip segments.

           6.2     Safety clamps are normally used in conjunction with collar slips as a safety measure to
                   prevent loss of collar down the hole. The diameter of the safety clamp is also adjusted by
                 removing or adding segments.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 158 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 515

                                            Policy:  SLIPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 4


           6.3    There shall be no welding on handles or segments of slips and clamps.

           6.4    The  drill crew must maintain the collar slips and safety clamp in good condition.
                    Periodically and before any trip the drill crew shall:

                    6.4.1  Check the handles of the slips and clamp for deformity, pins, and keepers

                    6.4.2   Ensure all internal mechanisms of the safety clamp are operating

                    6.4.3  Check hinge pins for wear

           6.5     Collar slips shall be set only after all movement of the collar string has ceased. The slips
                     shall be applied at the fish neck area of the collar (if applicable) leaving space above the
                      slips and below the upset to apply the safety clamp.

           6.6    The safety clamp wrench shall be maintained well away from the hole, off the rotary table.

           6.7    The clamp shall be applied, ensuring that all dies are flush against the surface of the collar.
                  This may require tapping the hinged segments of the clamp.

    7.0   PROCEDURES (Color Coding)

           7.1     All Slips onboard should be color coded with the following colors as per below:

              •  Equipment designed for 5” pipe shall be color coded Orange.
              •  Equipment designed for 3.5” pipe shall be color coded Black.
              •  Equipment designed for 2 ⅞” pipe shall be color coded Brown.

           7.2     In case of contradiction between the above color codes and the lifting gear inspection color
           codes, the above colors to be changed by a written request submitted from the OIM/PIC and it must
          be approved and signed from Rig Manager and Operations Manager.

           7.3    For drill pipe sizes other than mentioned above, a rig specific color-coding chart to be
           created and approved by Rig Manger and Operations Manager.

    8.0   RECORDS
         None

    9.0   REFERENCES
         None

                                        Policy: ELEVATORS
