  ELEVATORS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the measures and controls employed to operate and maintain
            elevators.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
           3.1    The Offshore  Installation Manager OIM/PIC are responsible for implementing  this
                  procedure during their assigned tour.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the drill crew
                 and drilling operation on the rig floor.

           3.3    The  Driller  is responsible  for the condition and maintenance of  all pipe-handling
                  equipment.

           3.4    The Driller is responsible to ensure his crew is maintaining elevators as per this policy.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1     Elevators shall not be modified in any way by ADES personnel.

                    4.1.1  No hot work of any kind is allowed on elevators.

                    4.1.2  Keep elevators lubed for easy open and close operation.

                    4.1.3  Keep an extra set of elevators cleaned, lubed, and covered.

           4.2    The practice of riding elevators into the derrick is strictly prohibited.

           4.3    The drill crew shall not allow the elevators to strike tubulars in the rotary as the elevators
                 descend to a latching position.

           4.4    For manual elevators, personnel shall place their hands only on handles provided on the
                     elevators.

                    4.4.1   Manipulating the position of the elevators using links or placing hands in link eyes
                                  is not allowed.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 160 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 520

                                        Policy: ELEVATORS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 4


                    4.4.2  The employee’s hands should be placed on the elevator horns, palms down, with
                            the fingers pointing sideways instead of grasping the horns when they have to
                        manually guide to open or close the elevators.

                    4.4.3   Personnel should grasp the bails above in the area of the eyes when helping guide
                            the elevators from the back, not inside of the eyes.

           4.5     Personnel shall not operate elevators without proper instructions from the Driller.

           4.6     All operations in which tubing / pipe elevators are to be utilized shall be preceded by a pre-
                   job safety meeting and accompanying Job Safety Analysis.

           4.7    Lowering the blocks and elevators steadily when applying the brakes so they shall not
                 bounce or jerk in different directions.

           4.8   When changing out the elevators, make sure that the bolt that secures the ears in place are
                      tight and the nuts are facing the person that is latching or unlatching the elevators. This
                    practice allows the person to see that the nut is not backing out on its own.
           4.9   When using automatic elevators, follow manufacturers’ guidelines.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Inspections)

           5.1     Elevators shall be visually inspected periodically and always prior to tripping for:

                    5.1.1   Cracks or worn areas

                    5.1.2  Weak springs

                    5.1.3   Condition of hinge pins

                    5.1.4   Condition of latch and latch lock pin (if so equipped)

                    5.1.5   Slip type elevators shall be inspected for proper dies and operation.

                    5.1.6  When using automatic elevators, follow manufacturers’ guidelines.

           5.2    To hold the bails in place, all tubing elevators with detachable bails must have:

                    5.2.1   Bolts or pins through the hole in the ears, or

                    5.2.2  Some other positive locking method.

                    5.2.3    If bolts or pins are used, cotter keys must be used to hold them in place.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 161 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 520

                                        Policy: ELEVATORS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 4



    6.0   PROCEDURE (Casing Elevators)

           6.1     Slip- or collar-type elevators may be used for casing and tubing strings.

                    6.1.1  The inserts of slip-type elevators must be clean and sharp.

                    6.1.2   Extra-long slip-type elevators shall be used on long and / or heavy strings.

                    6.1.3  The bearing surface of collar-type elevators shall have smooth, flat bearing contact,
                           perpendicular to the pulling direction.

           6.2     All third-party elevators shall require a certificate of compliance / inspection before coming
                  aboard on ADES mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU).

           6.3     Side-door elevators shall be fitted with a secondary latch locking pin as recommended by
                    the manufacturer. Any side-door elevators not so equipped shall be sent to an authorized
                    service facility for modification.

           6.4     Side-door elevators shall not be used to elevate casing from the V-door to the rotary table
                  without a risk assessment and approval process.

    7.0   PROCEDURES (Color Coding)

           7.1     All Elevators onboard should be color coded with the following colors as per below:

              •  Equipment designed for 5” pipe shall be color coded Orange.
              •  Equipment designed for 3.5” pipe shall be color coded Black.
              •  Equipment designed for 2 ⅞” pipe shall be color coded Brown.

           7.2    For YC and BX elevators inserts shall be color coded as above

           7.3     In case of contradiction between the above color codes and the lifting gear inspection color
                   codes, the above colors to be changed by a written request submitted from the OIM/PIC
                 and it must be approved and signed from Rig Manager and Operations Manager.

           7.4    For drill pipe sizes other than mentioned above, a rig specific color coding chart to be
                   created and approved by Rig Manger and Operations Manager.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 162 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 520

                                        Policy: ELEVATORS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 4


    8.0 PROCEDURE (Control of tubular elevators change)

           8.1   An elevator change out log shall be maintained on location for the entire well and
                 documentation completed for every job requiring elevators for that well. The log to be
                  signed by Driller, Toolpusher and OIM/PIC.
                  This includes, but is not limited to: drillpipe, casing, liner, tubing, specialty tools, etc...
           8.2    The Elevator Change Out Log shall be completed before the elevator is used to lift tubulars
                   or equipment.
           8.3    At the end of each well, one copy of the Elevator Change Out Log will be kept by on record
                by the OIM.
           8.4       It will be the responsibility of the rig manager to audit when he goes on the rig that Elevator
                Change Out Logs are used and recorded.
           8.5    The elevator shall be gauged with the proper elevator test pin before the elevator is used to
                           lift tubulars or equipment.

    6.0   RECORDS

           7.1     Third-party equipment checklists, on-site, one year after date of equipment removal
           7.2     Elevator certificates, on-site, current

    8.0   REFERENCES
         None

                                 Policy SLIP TYPE ELEVATOR

   SLIP TYPE ELEVATOR

   1.0   PURPOSE

        To establish company policy for the operation of slip type elevators used while running in
           the hole.

   2.0   SCOPE

          This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
           operations.

   3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

        The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager to ensure compliance with this standard

   4.0   PROCEDURES

       When running in the hole while using slip type elevators, the blocks shall be stopped at the
        monkey board to allow the Derrickman to latch them. Once latched the Driller shall raise
           the blocks in a manner to avoid setting the slips before the elevators reach the pipe upset.

   5.0   RECORDS
        None

   6.0   REFERENCES
        None

                                   Policy IRON ROUGHNECK
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 2


  IRON ROUGHNECK

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the controls that are required to ensure the proper use of the
            iron roughneck.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) maintains the overall responsibility for safety of
                    the operation by working with the crews to ensure equipment is maintained and utilized in
                  a safe manner.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for the supervision of the rig floor and drill crew including
                   operation of the iron roughneck.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1     Hydraulic cylinders, articulating jaws and arms, rotating equipment, tracks and hydraulic
                  hoses all present various potential hazards to personnel expected to operate and work with
                    the iron roughneck.

           4.2     Personnel shall use the Job Safety Analysis process for operation of the iron roughneck
                  during any operation.

           4.3    Only those persons who have demonstrated their ability to operate the iron roughneck in
                  use during their competency assurance assessments on the mobile offshore drilling unit
             (MODU) shall be allowed to operate it.

           4.4    Only ADES personnel shall operate the iron roughneck; only ADES supervisors shall
                   supervise any operations done with the iron roughneck.

           4.5    Maintenance and repair work done on the iron roughneck shall be done under the Permit
                    to Work system with a Job Safety Analysis.

           4.6    Mechanical and electrical isolations shall be applied as necessary to secure the iron
                 roughneck and to protect those engaged in servicing it (HSE 100.09).

           4.7    Climbing up the iron roughneck to reach elevated objects is not allowed. A proper ladder
                 and fall protection as applicable should be available on the rig floor for this purpose.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 165 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 525

                                   Policy IRON ROUGHNECK
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2


    5.0   PROCEDURE (Operation)

           5.1    Each Rig to have an approved list of competent operators for Iron Roughneck signed by
         OIM.

           5.2    The operator of the Iron Roughneck shall not engage tubulars before the slips are set and
                        all personnel have cleared the rotary and that all drill crew members hands are clear of the
                        drill pipe / collar / casing.

           5.3    The operator of the Iron Roughneck shall ensure the iron roughneck is retracted to its
                     furthest position from the rotary on each stand / joint.

           5.4     Personnel shall not position themselves within the path of travel of the iron roughneck.

           5.5    The Driller shall not pick up on the blocks until the iron roughneck has begun its retreat
                from the rotary table.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Maintenance)

           6.1    The drill crew shall ensure dies are in good condition.

           6.2    The drill crew shall ensure that the iron roughneck is kept clean and shall inspect the iron
                 roughneck on a daily basis for hydraulic leaks, loose fasteners, and missing guards.

           6.3     In the event of leaks, loose or disconnected fasters, missing guards, etc., the drill crew shall
                    notify the Rig Mechanic who shall assure any and all repairs to the iron roughneck are
                 made.

    7.0   RECORDS

           7.1   PTW Log / Register, Permit Control, two years
           7.2    Job Safety Analyses shall be uploaded to QHSE Dashboard and retained two years
           7.3    Copy of Job Safety Analyses (which may be filed with relevant permits), on-site, six
                months

    8.0   REFERENCES

           8.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)
           8.2   HSE 100.09, Energy Isolation (Lock Out / Tag Out)
           8.3   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)
           8.4    Competency Assurance Program

                                       Policy MUD PUMPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 5


  MUD PUMPS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the controls that are required to ensure the maintenance and
           proper operation of mud pumps.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
           3.1    The Offshore  Installation Manager OIM/PIC are responsible for implementing  this
                  procedure during their assigned tour.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for immediate supervision of the drilling operation including
                   operation of the mud pumps.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1     All work completed to the rig pumps shall be preceded with a Permit to Work, electrical
                 and mechanical lockouts are to be in place, and completed Job Safety Analysis for the task
                    to be performed. All necessary isolations and precautions shall be observed including using
                   personal protective equipment (PPE) requirements.

           4.2    Pumps shall be identified with numbers visible on the front and back side of each pump.

                    4.2.1  As per American Petroleum Institute (API) Recommended Practice (RP) 7L,
                           guideline is when standing at the power end and looking towards the fluid end, part
                      on the right of the centerline is designated as right where as parts on the left end
                            are designated as left and parts at centerline is designated as Center (i.e., 1-L, 1-C,
                           1-R).

           4.3     Rotating shafts and couplings on cooling water and lube pumps shall be covered with
                  guards bolted into position.

           4.4    Rod wash boxes shall be equipped with removable; Plexiglas or steel mesh covers which
                     shall remain in place guarding the rod wash boxes while the pump is in operation. No cover
                     shall be removed to make allowances for clip-on pump stroke counters.

           4.5     All positive displacement rig mud pumps shall be equipped with a trolley beam and trolley
                    for maintenance personnel use in changing liners, pistons, modules, and other heavy fluid-
                 end components.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 167 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 530

                                      Policy MUD PUMPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 5


                    4.5.1  The trolley beam and trolley shall have a certified minimum Safe Working Load
                    (SWL) and be so labeled. The trolley beam shall be fitted with “stops” at the end
                           of the beam to prevent the trolley from rolling off the beam.

                    4.5.2   Certified manual or pneumatic chain hoists of not more than eight-ton capacity
                              shall be available for use with above stated equipment.

           4.6     Purpose-designed tools from the original equipment manufacturer (OEM) for rig pump
                 maintenance and repair shall be made available in the pump room. The use of pump rods
                 and sledge hammers on liner jam nuts and valve caps is potentially hazardous and
                     potentially damaging to equipment; it should not be attempted.

           4.7    Working access to rod boxes for personnel changing liners, pistons, etc., shall be provided
                   as an integral part of the pump / skid assembly. Standing on cooling water pumps or using
                    portable ladders to work on pumps is prohibited.

           4.8    During maintenance and repair activities, a pump shall not be rolled over other than by
                  cranking by hand with the proper tool.

                    4.8.1  The rollover tool shall be removed and accounted for during clean up and before
                            returning the pump to service.

                    4.8.2  No person shall have any part of his body in a rod box while rolling over a pump.

           4.9     Pistons shall not be removed from liners by directing charging pump pressure to the liner.
                   Pistons shall be pulled by mechanical means only.

           4.10   The following operating specifications are to be followed to ensure mud pump efficiency:

                   4.10.1  All pressures limited to 80% of maximum pump output.

                   4.10.2  All volumes are adjusted to 95% volumetric efficiency.
                   4.10.3 Make sure that the pop-off valves are set at the proper settings: 95% of working
                           pressure on the size liners in the pumps.

           4.11   Note: All pump strokes shall not exceed 95% of manufacture’s specification.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Pressure Relief and Isolation)

           5.1     Positive displacement mud pumps shall have adjustable, pressure-relief, safety devices
                 such as spring-loaded safety valves (pop offs) installed and maintained.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 168 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 530

                                      Policy MUD PUMPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 5



           5.2     Pressure-relief valves shall be set below the manufacturer’s rated maximum pressure for
                    the liners installed in the pump.

           5.3    There shall be no valves, T’s, outlets, or diversions of any kind installed in the piping
                 between relief valves and discharge manifolds.

           5.4   No field repairs of pressure-relief discharge lines shall be implemented without approval
                from the Engineering Department. Any change in the discharge piping system shall be
                 reviewed and approved by submitting a Management of Change (MOC) form in HSE
                 Dashboard.

           5.5    The discharge manifold line shall be fitted with an isolation valve between the manifold
                 and the stand pipe for purposes of isolating the pump for maintenance and repair activities.

           5.6    The discharge manifold line shall be fitted with means of relieving pressure from the
                     isolated pump and this line shall remain open for the duration of repairs in progress on the
                pump.

           5.7   Pump drive motors shall be isolated at the power main supply source as local kill switch
                   provides isolation for control circuit.

                    5.7.1   There shall be no control closed-circuit television (CCT) isolations on CCTV
                        system located in pump room while working on mud pumps.

                    5.7.2   After completing the isolations, the positive isolation shall be physically confirmed
                             prior to undertaking any repairs.

           5.8    Power to pumps undergoing maintenance or repair shall be locked out according to the
                  energy isolation policy (HSE 100.09). In addition, a tag shall be placed on the pump control
                    rheostat at the Driller’s panel informing of work being conducted on the pump

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Pulsation Dampener Pre-charge)

           6.1     Pulsation dampeners shall be charged and labeled with nonflammable gas labels.

           6.2     Nitrogen gas only shall be used to pre-charge the pulsation dampener bladder.

           6.3     Proper charging lines and valves shall be made available. Pre-charging lines shall be
                 equipped with a bleed-off facility.

           6.4     Pre-charge pressure gauges shall be in good working condition and properly guarded with
                  cover / guard bolted in place.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 169 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 530

                                      Policy MUD PUMPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 5


           6.5    API RP 7L recommended guidelines on pre-charge is in range of 10% of the maximum
                    anticipated pressure or liner pressure capacity whichever  is with lower value or to
                   manufacturers’ recommendations.

           6.6     Nitrogen bottles shall be checked for content purity prior to being placed in service in the
              pump room.

           6.7     Proper transportation and storage facilities shall be provided for nitrogen bottles and pre-
                  charge equipment in the pump room.

    7.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Pump Fluid End Parts Records)

           7.1    Every effort shall be made to prevent running liners, pistons, valves, and valve seats to the
                   point of failure.

                    7.1.1  On the other hand, efforts shall be made to get as much service life from fluid end
                             parts as possible.

                    7.1.2  The best way to establish expected service life and change out frequencies is
                         through the use of parts service records.

           7.2    Records shall be kept of the service life in hours of:

                    7.2.1   Liners and liner packing

                    7.2.2   Pistons and piston rubbers

                    7.2.3  Rod packing

                    7.2.4   Valves and valve seats

           7.3     Preventive maintenance routines shall be established based on service hours of fluid-end
              pump parts to:

                    7.3.1   Prevent failure of parts while in operation, thereby avoiding downtime and
                        unplanned repair.

                    7.3.2   Optimize service life of parts without jeopardizing operational integrity of pumps.

           7.4     Parts that have been removed from pumps due to hour accumulation rather than failure are
                    to be considered as not useable and shall be removed from service. Such parts shall not be
                   put back into storage for later use.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 170 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 530

                                      Policy MUD PUMPS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 5 of 5


    8.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Inspection / Troubleshooting)

           8.1     Frequent inspections of the mud pumps while in operation must be performed by a
                 knowledgeable person. Checkpoints include but are not limited to:

                    8.1.1   Valve / valve seat erosion (suction and discharge)

                    8.1.2  Pump module erosion (suction and discharge)

                    8.1.3   Liner / gasket / piston / wash plate erosion

                    8.1.4   Cooling water

                    8.1.5   Lubrication

                    8.1.6   Bearing noise / failure

           8.2    The designated person/s shall check fluid levels, lube and cooling water pump operation,
                   cooling water jets to liners, quality of cooling water, water contamination in oil, telltale
                   holes in modules, etc.

                    8.2.1  The inspector shall listen for sounds indicating erosion of valves and valve seats,
                              failure of bearings, and tension on drive chains and belts if so equipped.

                    8.2.2  The frequency of these inspections shall be established and followed on each
                        working unit based on ongoing frequency.

                    8.2.3  The frequency of the inspection shall be reviewed based on the information
                            available on well program and advised to designated person (s) prior to drilling of
                         each section of the well.

    9.0   RECORDS
           9.1     Fluid-end service life records, Oracle or PMS system as applicable, indefinitely

   10.0  REFERENCES

           10.1  HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)
           10.2  HSE 100.09, Energy Isolation (Lock-out / Tag-out)
           10.3  HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)
           10.4  HSE 100.18, Personal Protective Equipment (PPE)
           10.5  ADM 015, Management of Change (MOC)
           10.6   American Petroleum  Institute (API) Recommended Practice (RP) 7L, API RP 7L,
                  Procedures for Inspection, Maintenance, Repair, and Remanufacture of Drilling Equipment

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 8



  BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the measures and controls employed to operate and test the
          Blowout Preventers (BOP).

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
           3.1    The Offshore  Installation Manager OIM/PIC  is  responsible  for implementing  this
                  procedure during their assigned tour.

           3.2    The Driller is responsible for the execution and immediate supervision of the drill crew
                 and is the first line of defense against a well control situation.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1    The blow out prevention component system shall be maintained as per manufacturer’s
                 recommendations. Personnel shall comply with all regulations and legal requirements for
                      installation, testing, operation, and maintenance.

           4.2    The BOP assembly must be designed to withstand the maximum anticipated surface
                   pressure for each well drilled. The working pressure of the BOP stack shall be equal to or
                    greater than the required working pressure of the wellhead upon which it is installed.

           4.3   BOPs equipped with manually operated ram locks shall be provided with one set of
                   operating handles (hand wheels) at the closest point available to operate the ram locks on
                 one ram preventer.

           4.4   BOP side outlet valves shall be removed and the side outlets blind-flanged when a drilling
                  spool is used unless needed to test the blind rams. Wellhead outlets below the BOP stack
                must not be used for well control operations except in extreme emergencies.

           4.5     Drills shall be conducted in which drill crews are expected to react decisively and without
                     hesitation to a variety of scenarios in which various components of the blow out prevention
                 system are functioned. It is expected that all drill crew members shall be made familiar
                  with the BOP component system.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 172 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 535

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 8


           4.6    The OIM / Tool pusher shall ensure proper HSE and industry best practices are followed
                    in and during the testing of BOP equipment. The OIM shall also follow original equipment
                  manufacturer (OEM) recommendations when pressure testing the BOP.

           4.7    Any BOP equipment problem shall be reported immediately to the OIM / Tool pusher and
                    the Rig Manager.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Ram Preventers)

           5.1     In order to test blind rams located below the drilling spool, the side outlet can be double-
                    valve. The valves shall have the same working pressure as the BOP and shall remain on
                    the BOP stack for the entire well.

           5.2      If more than one size of drill pipe (tapered string) is in the hole, a three-ram preventer stack
                must be installed to have both sizes of rams available.

           5.3    During function tests, pipe rams shall only be closed on the designated size of pipe to
                   prevent damaging the seals.

           5.4   Ram preventers are not designed to be pressure tested from the topside, since this can
               damage the preventer. Rams shall only be pressure tested from the bottom side.

           5.5   BOP rams shall always be tested at 250 – 300 pounds per square inch (psi) prior to the
                   high-pressure test.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Annular Preventers)

           6.1    Annular preventers shall not be relied upon to close in on tubulars with irregular surfaces
                 such as stabilizers and grooved drill collars.

           6.2    Annular preventers shall not be used to close in an open hole (no tubulars across the
                   annular) except in extreme emergencies.

           6.3     Diverter lines shall extend in a straight line at 90 degrees from the casing / conductor pipe
                   well center. Diverter lines shall not be fitted with sweeps or elbows to direct flow
                downward.

           6.4   A diverter shall be installed on the conductor previous to drilling surface hole.

           6.5    The diverter hydraulic control is to be sequenced in such a manner as the diverter line
                   valves are at full open prior to closing the diverter element.

    7.0   PROCEDURE (Controls: Closing Unit)


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 173 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 535

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 8


           7.1    The Driller shall ensure that a tour check of the closing unit is performed and recorded in
                    the International Association of Drilling Contractors (IADC) report.

           7.2    The control handle on the closing unit for operating blind or blind/shear rams shall always
                 be protected against accidental closure with pipe in the hole.

           7.3   A hinged, flip-up cover without locking device is to be used. Lock, clip, or tie-down
                   devices on the closing handle of blind rams are not allowed.

           7.4    The hydraulic reservoir of the closing unit shall have the capacity to contain two times the
                amount of fluid required to close all rams and the annular. It shall be maintained at one-
                    half full of fluid at all times.

           7.5    The accumulator pre-charge and auto switch points shall be maintained at manufacturer’s
                     specifications.

           7.6    Pneumatic and electric power for pump systems shall be on line at all times while in
                    operation.

           7.7    The pre-charge pressure on each accumulator bottle or bank shall be checked prior to
                 spudding each well and as per preventive maintenance system / OEM recommendations.

           7.8    Only Nitrogen shall be used as a pre-charge medium.

                    7.8.1   All nitrogen bottles shall be checked for content before using to pre-charge
                         accumulator bottle banks.

                    7.8.2   Individual accumulator bottles shall be labeled with a nonflammable gas decal.

           7.9    There shall be at least one remote control panel for diverter and BOP systems. It shall be
                mounted at the drill floor, near the Driller’s console. It shall graphically represent the BOP
                   stack and the diverter.

    8.0   PROCEDURE (Equipment to be Tested)

         The following blowout prevention equipment shall be tested:

           8.1     All components of the BOPs, wellhead components, and their connections

           8.2    The choke and kill manifold valves, kill and choke lines, and valves on the side outlets

           8.3    The Top Drive System


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 174 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 535

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 8


           8.4     Standpipe manifolds

           8.5     All safety valves

           8.6     Chicksans, before their use

    9.0   PROCEDURE (Test Frequency)

         The BOP equipment shall be tested:

           9.1     Per governmental regulations

           9.2    Every Fourteen (14) days

           9.3    At installation

           9.4    Before drilling out of a string of casing or liner

           9.5     Prior to production testing

           9.6     Prior to drilling into known high pressure

           9.7    Any time the Operator requests tests

           9.8    Any time repairs or changes to BOP are made

   10.0  PROCEDURE (Test Pressures)

       ADES BOP equipment shall be tested to the following pressures:

           10.1  BOP at installation: to Operator’s requirements or the well head pressure rating.

           10.2   Annular to 70% of working pressure.

                   10.2.1  Never test the annular to its rated working pressure because it increases the wear
                              rate many times.

                   10.2.2  If required to test to maximum pressure, Rig Manager approval must be obtained.

           10.3   Subsequent BOP tests are to be anticipated wellhead pressure on.

           10.4    All BOP components shall also be tested at a low pressure of 250-300 pounds per square
                  inch (psi), minimum of five (5) minutes or held to Operator’s requirements.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 175 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 535

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 5 of 8


           10.5    All high-pressure tests shall be held to Operator’s requirements or minimum of ten (10)
                   minutes.

           10.6  A chart shall be made of the BOP equipment test with notation as to tested equipment
                 components.

           10.7    Safety valves, and top drives shall be tested to the same criteria as the BOPs.

           10.8   Blind / shear rams may be tested every 30 days or as required by regulatory.

           10.9   Wellhead side outlet valves below the test tool must be open during a test to avoid the risk
                   of a pressure increased below the test tool.

          10.10  Never close the annular on open hole; for testing, use a joint of drill pipe to close around.

   11.0  PROCEDURE (Standards)

           11.1  A Job Safety Analysis shall be conducted, reviewed, and acknowledged by  all job
                     participants.

           11.2   Areas in the danger zone during high pressure testing should be clearly marked so that no
                 one could easily place themselves in harm’s way.

                   11.2.1  Create a barrier around the area off with caution tape or rope and sign that reads
                        something similar to “Caution, High Testing Pressure Area”.

                   11.2.2 Remove all unnecessary tools from the area.

                   11.2.3  Position the tie-in point in a safe direction.

                   11.2.4 Do not look down the hole to see if an element is leaking while pressure is applied.

           11.3    Visually inspect all hoses and their connections to ensure they are in good working order
                 and suitably rated.

           11.4   When testing, announce over the public address (PA) system or by any other means of
                 communication when applying pressure to the testing area.

           11.5   BOPs, choke manifold, top drive upper and lower BOP valves, inside BOPs, and drill string
                    safety valves shall be tested on initial nipple-up. Then BOPs and all previous equipment
                      listed shall be tested every 14 days thereafter until well is complete. With dispensation
                from the Operations manager the BOP test can be extended to 21 days.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 176 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 535

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 6 of 8


           11.6   BOPs shall be tested before drilling out each string of casing or a liner.

                   11.6.1  This test may be omitted if the BOP’s integrity has not been breached or the next
                            section of hole to be drilled maximum anticipated surface pressure or less than the
                          previous BOP test.

                   11.6.2  This test can only be conducted with regulatory and Rig Manager’s approval.

           11.7   Water must be used for all BOP tests.

           11.8   Control station shall be alternated each test and logged on the IADC report.

           11.9   As operations allow, all operational components of the BOP equipment systems shall be
                   function tested at least once a week to verify the component’s intended operations,
                     alternating from the driller’s panel, remote panels or control stations. Function tests may
                   or may not include pressure tests.

          11.10  Pressure test variable bore pipe rams against the largest and smallest sizes of pipe in use,
                  excluding drill collars and bottom hole tools.

          11.11  BOPs and valves are to be tested to a low pressure of between 250 to 300 psi for five (5)
                  minutes and recorded on a chart. If pressure is raised above 300 psi, the system must be
                  bled back to zero and restarted.

          11.12 A check-off sheet should be used defining the equipment and the sequence of each piece
                   of equipment tested and kept for review by the regulatory agencies.

          11.13  High pressure test for the annular preventer is not to exceed 70% of the rated working
                    pressure.

          11.14  Upon completion of the low-pressure test, pressure shall be bled to 0 psi.

                   11.14.1 The high-pressure  test must be equal to the rated working pressure of the
                        equipment or 500 psi greater than calculated maximum anticipated surface
                           pressure for the applicable hole section.

                   11.14.2 All tests should be recorded on a chart.

          11.15  The interval between any blind or blind-shear rams test may not exceed 30 days or as
                    specified by regulatory body.

          11.16 A high pressure of 70% of the rated working pressure of the equipment is acceptable if
                 approved by local regulations.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 177 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                             Policy BLOWOUT PREVENTER (BOP)

          11.17 A pressure test of the affected BOP component is required whenever the integrity of the
                 equipment has been breached (e.g., if doors have been opened to change rams, break
                    flanges, etc.)

          11.18  Record keeping requirements for BOP test:

                   11.18.1 Record the time, date, and results of all pressure tests, actuations, and inspections
                           of the BOP system and system components on the IADC report.

                   11.18.2 Record BOP test pressure on pressure charts.

                   11.18.3 OIM / Tool pusher and Operator’s Representative shall sign and date BOP test
                            charts and reports as correct.

                   11.18.4 Document the sequential order of BOP and auxiliary equipment testing and the
                           pressure and duration of each test.

                   11.18.5 Identify the control station used during the test.

                   11.18.6 Identify any problems or irregularities observed during BOP system testing and
                          record actions taken to remedy the problems or irregularities.

                   11.18.7 Retain all records, including charts, IADC reports, and referenced documents
                            pertaining to BOP test, actuations, and inspections at the facility for the duration
                           of drilling.
          11.19  The BOP equipment tests shall be logged in the daily IADC drilling report.

          11.20  The BOP equipment tests shall be recorded on ADES test sheets and filed on the rig.

          11.21  Verify that the wellhead side outlet valves below the test tool are open during a test to avoid
                    the risk of a pressure increase below the test tool.

          11.22  Do not close the annular on open hole. For testing, use a joint of drill pipe to close around.

   12.0  PROCEDURE (Documentation)

           12.1   The following list of documents is used in the development of procedures for operation of
                    the BOP:

                   12.1.1  Manufacturer’s Operations Manual

                   12.1.2  Preventive Maintenance System and Standards

                   12.1.3 ADES Well Control Manual

           12.2    Prior to cutting a window in the casing for kick off purposes or for extensive milling
                    operations, ditch magnets shall be installed.

                   12.2.1 The customer should be advised that time needs to be provided to flush and clean
                         metal shavings from BOPs and adjacent equipment as soon as possible after the
                           milling operation is completed.

                   12.2.2  For extensive milling, opening the rams and annular may be required.

           12.3   When cleaning out the BOP, special attention shall be given to the annular pistons  /
                    cylinders and the rear of the ram blocks adjacent to the piston rods when the scoring of
                   these areas shall result in an operating failure.

           12.4    In order to avoid damage to the BOP stack, milling operations shall not take place inside
                 and adjacent to the bore of the BOP stack without the approval of the Rig Manager.

                   12.4.1 Any proposed mill for the operation should avoid having cutters along its outside
                          diameter (OD) that may bite into the BOP.

                   12.4.2 The bottom of the mill shall be determined relative to its position in the BOP stack.

                             Policy MUD AND CHOKE MANIFOLDS

  MUD AND CHOKE MANIFOLDS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy  is to define the controls that are required to ensure the proper
           maintenance, handling and use of mud and choke manifolds.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1   A review shall be made to ensure that well control equipment, including choke and kill
                   manifolds, exceed the drilling section anticipated pressure prior to drilling. If such situation
                      arises, then it shall be communicated to the OIM / Tool pusher and Rig Manager.

           4.2    Choke manifold shall be capable of providing at least two return flow paths: manual and
                  remotely controlled.

                    4.2.1  The choke manifold shall be capable of providing returns through one of at least
                      two remote controlled adjustable chokes.

                    4.2.2  The choke manifold shall be arranged in such a manner that, should an adjustable
                        choke fail for any reason, it can be repaired / replaced without interrupting well
                            control operations.

           4.3    Ensure only calibrated pressure gauges are installed in place on the choke and standpipe
                  manifold and the current calibration certification is available on board. The display on
                     installed gauges shall be legible and graduation of scales shall be within the pressure rating
                   of the system.

           4.4    The standpipe manifold and choke manifold shall be located in such a manner that an
                   unobstructed route of egress shall allow for emergency escape from the manifold area at
                        all times.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 180 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 540

                             Policy MUD AND CHOKE MANIFOLDS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 3


           4.5     All valves and controls on the standpipe and choke manifolds shall be numbered or
                   otherwise identified by labels. Open and closed positions shall be known to the Driller at
                        all times.

           4.6    Any external load forces application to the standpipe or other high-pressure mud manifolds
                        is prohibited at all times. This includes chaining snatch blocks to the manifold to use with
                       air hoist operations, looping wire line around or through the standpipe manifold to assist in
                     hoisting, or pulling an object or using the standpipe manifold to hang objects from either
                  permanently or temporarily.

           4.7     All valves from standpipe to cementing manifold or choke manifold and vice versa should
                 be closed during section drilling in progress.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Operation)

           5.1     Standpipe and choke manifold valves position and flow path routing and control of value
                    positions shall be included in the Driller’s handover at the end of each Driller’s tour of
                    duty.

           5.2    Oncoming Drillers shall check the routing and position of  all standpipes and choke
                  manifold valves and controls on a daily basis as part of his pre-tour / daily check. Any
                 changes to normal routings / control positions while on duty shall be recorded and included
                    in his handover.

           5.3     All drill crew personnel are expected to be familiar with various routing of the standpipe
                  manifold valves. This shall be part of the new drill crew induction on board.

           5.4     Circulation through the choke manifold shall be included in well control drills prior to
                      drilling out intermediate casing. At this time, with the well in a safe mode, hands-on
                  experience of choke adjustment and recording of circulation pressure readings shall be
                    carried out by all designated ADES well control crew including new Drillers and Assistant
                     Drillers on board for familiarity.

           5.5     In a well control situation, the choke manifold shall be operated only by authorized,
                 competent ADES  personnel. Competent  designated  persons  shall  possess  current,
                   appropriate Well Control certification.

           5.6     Periodical pressure testing shall be inclusive of all valves tested on the choke and kill
                  manifold and standpipe manifold valves. Testing of the manual and remote-control choke
                        is not recommended during periodical testing.
           5.7    During BOP pressure testing, high pressure mud manifolds including the standpipe
                  manifold and the pump discharge manifolds in the pump room shall be lined up to the
              pump pressure relief (pop off) valve in order to protect the pump from BOP test pressures.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 181 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 540

                             Policy MUD AND CHOKE MANIFOLDS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 3



           5.8    The standpipe manifold shall be lined up to the fill-up line in order to protect the top drive
                 and rotary hose from blowout preventer (BOP) test pressures.

           5.9    Before pressure is released through the standpipe manifold or the choke manifold, all
                   necessary downstream valves shall be in the open position.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Maintenance and Repair)

           6.1     All repairs and replacement of high-pressure mud and choke manifolds shall be recorded
                   as part of the rig maintenance system. It is mandatory to perform and record the pressure
                     testing at the full working pressure capacity of the manifold / equipment after any repairs
                  undertaken and/or replacement of standpipe and choke manifold components.

           6.2    Only original equipment manufacturer (OEM) or OEM-licensed parts shall be used in the
                   high-pressure mud and choke manifolds. Field modification of the components is not
                   allowed.

           6.3     Standpipe manifolds are normally of unit construction with valves welded into place. While
                     internal valve components may be replaced, the valve bodies themselves cannot be
                   replaced without proper approvals and procedures.

           6.4    Valve bodies cannot be modified or welded on the rig, and if body repairs are required,
                  then they shall be sent to a manufacturer franchised facility for repair.

           6.5    Choke manifolds are usually made of flanged connections. Changing chokes and valves
                  with flanged connections is permitted. The manifold is to be tested immediately following
                 any valve replacement.

           6.6     All RX and BX gaskets shall be used only once. Upon removal from a flanged assembly,
                        all used RX and BX gaskets shall be disposed (HSE 200.04).

           6.7    Choke and standpipe manifold valves shall be lubricated routinely by the drill crew.
                  Greasing of the valves/components under pressure is not permitted.

    7.0   RECORDS
           7.1     Calibration records, on-site, current
           7.2    Well Control Certificates, on-site, current
           7.3    Maintenance and testing records, Oracle or PMS system as applicable, indefinitely

    8.0   REFERENCES
        HSE 200.04, Waste Management

                Policy THREADED CONNECTIONS IN HIGH PRESSURE MUD,
                 CEMENT, AND KILL & CHOKE PIPING SYSTEMS

  THREADED CONNECTIONS IN HIGH PRESSURE MUD, CEMENT, AND KILL & CHOKE PIPING
  SYSTEMS

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy, guidelines, and procedures for the use of threaded connections in
           high pressure mud, cement and kill & choke piping systems.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring that:

                3.1.1 All threaded pressure-sealing connections that are larger than 1/2" NPT in the piping
                   systems covered by this standard are identified and recorded

                3.1.2 A Rig Modification Request Form to be submitted to head office Technical Support Dept.,
                    along with a list of threaded pressure-sealing connections required in 3.1.1 above

                3.1.3 The connections identified in 3.1.1 are replaced with approved connections specified by
                    Technical Operations Support within the time frame specified by this standard after a
                   budget is approved to replace them.

                3.1.4 Requisitions for Go/No-Go gauges specified shall be submitted and such gauges shall be
                   used to inspect all non-approved pressure-sealing threaded connections upon receipt.
                  Depending on the condition of the non-approved connections, frequencies for ongoing
                      inspections shall be established in the rig's PMS (Preventive Maintenance System) and
                      carried out until the non-approved connections are replaced with approved connections.

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible through the chain of command up to and including the V.P.
           Operations to carry-out the following:

                3.2.1 Ensure the information assembled by the OIM/PIC as required is submitted to Technical
                    Operations for review with a Rig Modification Request.

                3.2.2 Ensure requisitions submitted for Go/No-Go gauges specified are approved, and that such
                   gauges are expedited to the rig.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 183 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 542
                Policy THREADED CONNECTIONS IN HIGH PRESSURE MUD,
                 CEMENT, AND KILL & CHOKE PIPING SYSTEMS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 4


                3.2.3 Upon receipt of Technical Operations review, ensure that a budget is prepared and
                    submitted for affecting the modifications required to comply with this standard for all
                       rigs under his direct supervision.

                3.2.4 Upon approval of such budgets for any given fiscal year, ensure that AFE's
                     are submitted, and third parties contracted as necessary to perform the modifications at
                 any time during that fiscal year when the opportunity arises. If the modifications cannot
                  be carried out for whatever reason during that fiscal year, the Rig Manager shall ensure
                      that funds are budgeted for the following year to accomplish the modifications required
                     to comply with this standard.

       3.3 The Technical Operations is responsible to the V.P. Operations for ensuring the modification
            requests submitted from the field are reviewed, and where required, unique specifications are
          developed and delivered to the Rig Manager for certain piping arrangements on rigs that require
          unique solutions to comply with this standard, and when requested, provide budgetary estimates
            for the cost of the approved modifications.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 All threaded connections that are not approved in existing systems covered by this standard shall
          be replaced with approved connections.

       4.2 Go/No-Go thread and plug gauges specified shall be purchased and used to evaluate the condition
           of the non-approved threaded connections at sufficient intervals to ensure the threads are in
            serviceable condition. Those threads that are found to be worn or corroded to the point where the
           condition is nearing the point of rejection should be inspected at greater frequencies than those
          found to be in good condition. All threaded connections shall be replaced with approved
           connections as soon as practicable after discovery of any non-approved threaded connections that
         do not pass the Go/No-Go inspection.

       4.3 For all new rigs, and for all rigs that are removed from service for the purpose of conducting
          upgrades and modifications,  all new piping systems or those piping systems that are to be
            substantially modified that are covered by this standard shall have no pressure-sealing threaded
           connections. If such rig upgrades and modifications do not include any modifications to such piping
           systems, the Rig Manager shall take advantage of the period of time that the rig is inactive to affect
            the modifications required.

       4.4 High pressure hoses carrying a valid and current test certificate are preferred over hard piped
           chicksan piping, whether the use of it is temporary or permanent. However, if high pressure hoses
            are not available, hard-piped chicksans shall be constructed of full Penetration, butt-welded fittings
          and WECO-type unions with NO pressure-sealing threaded fittings allowed.

       4.5 GAUGES FOR NPT THREADED CONNECTIONS

                4.5.1 Ring and plug gauges for NPT threaded connections can be ordered through various
                      sources. The gauges to order are called L1 NPT Thread Gauges. The procedures for use
                      are provided below:

                   ▪  L1 plug Gauge





                       Insert L1 Plug Gauge into female NPT fitting threads, and screw in until hand-tight Look
                        at gauge step to see if it is flush with the end of the fitting as shown above. Threads of
                      the fitting are acceptable if the gauge step is within plus or minus one turn of being flush
                    with the end of the fitting.

                   ▪  L1 Ring Gauge





                       Insert L1 Ring gauge onto the male NPT fitting threads, and screw on hand tight look
                        at end of ring gauge to see if it is flush with the end of the fitting as shown below.
                   Threads of the fitting are acceptable if the end of the ring gauge is within plus or minus
                  one turn of being flush with the end of the fitting.

                   ▪  The above gauges are available in nominal NPT diameters ranging from 1/8" NPT
                           to 12" NPT. The rig needs only to order those gauges that fit the nonapproved NPT
                             fittings identified in 3.1 above.

                   ▪  Gauges should be delivered with a short form certificate.

[ صفحه 185 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 542
                Policy THREADED CONNECTIONS IN HIGH PRESSURE MUD,
                 CEMENT, AND KILL & CHOKE PIPING SYSTEMS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 4


    5.0   PROCESS AND DEFINITIONS
       5.1 Pressure Sealing Threaded Connection: A thread designed to provide two (2) functions in the
           connection:

                5.1.1 A mechanism to retain the separation load across the connection

                5.1.2 A sealing mechanism that retains the internal media.

            Examples of a pressure sealing thread are N.P.T. (National Pipe Thread), B.S.P. (British
             Standard Pipe) threads, etc.

       5.2 Non-Pressure Sealing Threaded Connection: A connection that utilizes a separate sealing
         mechanism from the load retaining mechanism. Non-pressure sealing threaded connections are
          allowed in all API high pressure rated manifolds and systems. Examples of non-pressure sealing
           threaded connections are WECO or equivalent union connections, Autoclave connections, etc.

       5.3 Approved connections are defined below:

                5.3.1 Non-pressure sealing threaded connections.

                5.3.2 API 6BX Weld-neck, ring joint flange connections

                5.3.3 Pressure sealing threaded connections that are 1/2" NPT or smaller for systems with
                      rated working pressures equal to or less than 10,000 psi.

    6.0   RECORDS
         None

    7.0   REFERENCES
         None

                                    Policy  ROTARY HOSES
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  ROTARY HOSES

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to define the controls that are required to ensure the maintenance and
           operation of the rotary hose.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1    Rotary hoses shall not be lifted by attaching or wrapping slings or chains around the
                    external sheathing of the hose. Lift rotary hoses:

                    4.1.1  When rolled up are to be secured to a pallet or boxed.

                    4.1.2   Should only be connected by the steel couplers on either end of the hose.

           4.2    Each Rig must carry a spare rotary hose ready for installation.

           4.3    Upon installation of a rotary hose, it must be examined to ensure that it hangs straight with
                no twists in the length of the hose. Such twists shall result in premature failure of the hose.

           4.4    Rotary hoses shall be of sufficient length to allow the traveling assembly to travel the full
                  range of its operational requirements without introducing undue strain or stretch to the
                    rotary hose.

           4.5    Rotary hoses should not come in contact with tong counterbalance lines, pneumatic hoist
                      lines, mud box lines, etc., in the course of their travel. Drill crew personnel are to be
                  mindful of the rotary hose when putting away tongs, hoist lines, etc.

           4.6    Adequate safety lines shall be installed on each end of rotary hoses is use.

           4.7    Rotary hoses  shall be  periodically  inspected  internally  as  well  as  externally  for
                     deterioration:

                    4.7.1   Inner sheathing (particularly sloughing of inner sheathing)


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 187 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 545

                                    Policy  ROTARY HOSES
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2


                    4.7.2   Internal connector area

                    4.7.3   External sheath fatigue

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Safety Lines)

           5.1     Safety lines on rotary hoses shall consist of 7/8” wire rope or larger. Note that fit for
                  purpose safety chains with certification may be used.

                    5.1.1  The safety line shall not be attached to the standpipe or top drive gooseneck or any
                       component of the high-pressure mud system.

                    5.1.2   Safety lines may be attached to the frame of the top drive unit and to structural
                      members of the derrick.

           5.2     Safety clamps shall be placed a distance of between 6 and 18 inches from the steel sleeve
                   of the coupling.

                    5.2.1  The upset of the rotary hose must be fitted tightly between the clamp and the
                          coupler or manufacturer’s markings.

                    5.2.2   In the event the rotary hose has a manufacturer indication of where to install the
                         clamp, use the mark as indicated.

           5.3     Safety lines shall be of sufficient length as not to restrict the movement of the rotary hose
                   as it travels with the traveling assembly.

           5.4     All personnel are to be mindful of the fact that some manufacturers place lifting eyes on
                    the steel coupler as a proper lifting point. This is not to be confused as a snubbing point on
                    the hose; it is for lifting purposes only.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Pressure Testing)

           6.1    Pressure tests of rotary hoses should not exceed the rated working pressure of the hose.

           1.1    After pressure testing, crews must be alert to any small leaks in the coupler area. At the first
                   indication of a leak or a bulge, the rotary hose shall be marked as out of service and properly
                  discarded / removed from service.
    7.0   RECORDS
         None

    8.0   REFERENCES
   None

                               Policy   SHAKERS AND MUD PITS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  SHAKERS AND MUD PITS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy  is to define the controls that are required to ensure the proper
          maintenance and operation of the shakers and mud pits.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1       It is the responsibility of the OIM / Tool pusher to ensure that the hazardous zone integrity
                   of the shakers and mud pit areas are maintained. These issues include but are not limited
                       to:

                    4.1.1   Intrinsic  safety   /  explosion-proof  quality  of  electrical  installations  within
                         hazardous zones.

                    4.1.2   Equipment installation within hazardous zones.

                    4.1.3   Piping and Motor Control Center (MCC) installations within hazardous zones.

                    4.1.4   Ventilation requirements of hazardous zones.

                    4.1.5  Alarm system requirements within hazardous zones.

           4.2    The requirements to maintain hazardous zone integrity shall be communicated to personnel
                    regularly in weekly safety meeting topics.

           4.3    Emergency drills conducted on board shall include considerations for the mud pit area in
                   regard to equipment and ventilation shut down procedures.

           4.4     Fire detection, alarm, and suppression systems as well as gas detection and alarm systems
                     shall be maintained in good operating condition and periodically tested as per the planned
                 maintenance system. Arrangement of these systems shall be as per the rig fire and safety
                    plan.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 189 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 550

                               Policy   SHAKERS AND MUD PITS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 5


           4.5    The shaker area is a high noise area. When shakers are in operation, hearing protection
                     shall be worn in the shaker area. There shall be signs posted advising of high noise level
                 and need for hearing protection.

           4.6     Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) shall be stored in purpose-designed boxes mounted
                    in permanent positions at

                    4.6.1  The jet hoppers (sack room)

                    4.6.2  The shale shakers

                    4.6.3  The pit area

           4.7    Permanently mounted emergency showers and eye wash stations shall be installed at the
                   shale shaker area and the jet hopper.

                    4.8.1   These showers and stations shall be clearly marked.

                    4.8.2  The access routes to the emergency showers and eyewash stations shall have
                          convenient access and not be blocked.

           4.8     Shale shakers and mud agitators shall have local shut off switches capable of being “locked
                    out”.

           4.9     All rotating equipment including belt drives shall be fully enclosed.

           4.10    All piping shall be labeled as to content and direction of flow.

                   4.10.1  All valves shall be identified and labeled.

                   4.10.2 The drill crew shall ensure that all valves and lines are properly “lined up” or
                          routed for the operation at hand.

           4.11    All dump valves from the pits and sand traps should have two valves.

                   4.11.1 The outer valve should be a gate valve type and equipped with a mechanical lock.

                   4.11.2 Dumping of mud pit content shall take place under a cold Permit to Work only and
                         a Job Safety Analysis.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 190 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 550

                               Policy   SHAKERS AND MUD PITS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 5


    5.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Housekeeping)

           5.1    Housekeeping in the shale shaker and mud pit areas shall be maintained in optimal
                   condition considering the operations in progress. Each person entering these areas is
                   responsible to maintain an orderly, efficient, and uncluttered environment.

           5.2     Appropriate trash containers in sufficient quantity shall be maintained in the shaker and
             mud pit areas to prevent rags, bags, rope, paper, gloves, used parts, light bulbs, etc., from
                   finding their way into inappropriate disposal sights and becoming unsightly and hazardous.

           5.3    Chemical bags and containers must not be allowed to accumulate to an unsafe level. They
                must not interfere with routes of egress nor block access to emergency equipment such as
                emergency showers, eye wash stations, and portable fire extinguishers. If necessary, the
                 Derrickman shall seek assistance in clearing the area to maintain its safety aspects.

           5.4    The Derrickman shall maintain all PPE boxes in good condition.

                    5.4.1   Only PPE shall be stored in PPE boxes.

                    5.4.2   Tools, rags, screen tensioner bolts, springs, etc., shall not be placed in PPE boxes.

           5.5    Marsh funnels and mud balances shall be cleaned after every use and returned to their
                   designated storage area. The area around the Derrick man’s basin shall remain clean and
                    uncluttered.

           5.6     Jet hopper areas shall be thoroughly cleaned after mixing activities have ended for the
                    period. No person going off duty shall leave waste or refuse of any kind at the hopper area.

           5.7     Shale shaker areas shall be frequently washed down; no materials shall be stored on the
                     floor level of the shaker area.

           5.8    Used shaker screens shall be cleaned and stored in a dedicated storage area, purpose
                  designed for storage of used shaker screens. They shall not be left draped over flow lines,
                     rolled up, standing in corners, etc.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Hatches and Doors)

           6.1    Hatches and doors shall be maintained in good condition with hinges and dogs lubricated
                   as required.

           6.2     All cargo hatches and doors in the mud pit and shale shaker areas shall be closed when not
                    in use to maintain hazardous zone integrity. Door alarms shall be maintained in operating
                    condition, they shall not be disabled except as required for maintenance activities.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 191 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
