                               Policy   SHAKERS AND MUD PITS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 5


           6.3    Cargo hatches shall be equipped with either handrails around the opening, or alternative
                     barriers of sufficient height to prevent personnel from inadvertently falling through the
                    hatch.

           6.4   When in use, hatches shall be positively secured in the open position. This shall be done
                  through the use of an approved device. Rope is not approved for opening or securing hatch
                   doors.

           6.5    Rubber sealing strips or gaskets on all watertight and airtight doors and hatches shall be
                  maintained in good condition. Inspections shall also include the gasket groove / lip for
               damage due to strikes and corrosion.

           6.6     Pit room doors shall be equipped with automatic closing devices. These doors shall not be
                  secured open with rope, wire, or any other improvised device.


    7.0   PROCEDURE (Specific Controls: Working in Mud Pits)

           7.1    The Driller shall be notified prior to transferring or dumping of any drilling fluid.

           7.2   No person shall enter a mud pit without an approved Permit to Work, and a Job Safety
                   Analysis. No person shall enter a mud pit without a standby / watch person present at the
                  time of entry and for its duration.

           7.3     All open hatches and gratings that has been removed for the purpose of tank entry shall be
                 guarded with an appropriate barrier.

           7.4     Agitators must be locked out as per PTW and lockout policy at the Silicon-controlled
                     Rectifier (SCR) Room (or other similar location) as well as locally before entering mud
                        pits.

           7.5     In the event a chemical barrel / caustic soda barrel is present in the pit area, it shall be
                removed from overhead of the tank that is to be entered and shall be stored in an appropriate
                  chemical storage area.

           7.6   When cleaning tanks having contained oil-based mud (OBM), personnel shall wear
                   appropriate PPE. Refer to the applicable Safety Data Sheet (SDS).

  CLEANING OF MUD LINES, SHAKER BOXES AND GUMBO SYSTEMS

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide minimum procedural requirements to be taken when cleaning out mud flow lines,
        dump chutes, header boxes, gumbo boxes/tanks, and any other drilling mud related line or
          box/system where entry is required.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring the requirements of this standard are
            carried out.

    4.0   PROCEDURES
           4.1 This work must be authorized by a work permit issued by the OIM/PIC under the
               requirements set forth for confined space entry in accordance with HSE Policy.

           4.2 An alternative method of well monitoring shall be implemented during the course of the work
                     if the trip tank is not used and the flow line is open for cleaning operations.

           4.3 The on-site supervisor shall be identified during the Job Risk Assessment (JRA) to manage
             and co-ordinate the work, ensure that personnel gaining entry into confined tanks and lines
                are monitored at all times, ensure contingency plan and adequate means of rescue are
                available in work site and ready to use, crew members are trained to use them, and ensure the
               requirements of the Work Permit are complied with throughout the course of all work until
            work is completed the Work Permit is closed.

           4.4 While the flow line/boxes are being cleaned out, all valves in piping systems connected to the
              flow line/boxes shall be closed, locked, and tagged, as well as locking and tagging-out mud
             mixing / transfer pumps and mud pumps and mud agitators.

           4.5 The OIM/PIC shall ensure that:

                    4.5.1 Appropriate Job Risk Assessments (JRA's) have been carried out by the on-site
                         supervisor and the crew prior to the commencement of any work

                    4.5.2 Work control measures are in place


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 194 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                 SHAKER BOXES AND GUMBO SYSTEMS

Issued: 28. Jan.2020

                    4.5.3 Work permits are renewed every 12 hours and when a different crew is used to
                         carry out the work, if area manager / supervisor is working deferent shift than the
                        crew, permit should be renewed upon his crew change

           4.6 4 Adequate provisions for entry and egress for personnel to clean out the specific mud tank or
                 line shall be identified during the JRA.

           4.8 Assess the mud tanks and lines to determine if any other system requires formal lock-out and
                tag-out procedures to be implemented. During this assessment, instances or situations shall be
                 identified and recorded in writing when personnel should not enter the system for cleaning.

           4.9 Warning signs and barricades shall be installed at the work site as well as in conspicuous
                locations in all access and egress routes to and from the work area.

           4.10 This standard is to be reviewed and discussed with all rig crew members.
    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

  ROTARY TABLE

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide consistent and standard instructions for rotary table
            operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore  Installation Manager OIM/PIC  is  responsible  for implementing  this
                  procedure during their assigned tour.

           3.2    The Driller shall carry out the instructions and guidance contained in this policy.

    4.0   PROCEDURE

           4.1    The Driller shall ensure the hole cover is placed in the rotary table whenever the table is
                   not in use.

           4.2    Rig floor personnel shall check the oil in the rotary table on a per-tour basis and keep it
                       filled as per maintenance instructions.

                    4.2.1  The rotary inner bushings shall be locked at all times, only unlocked when the
                            rotary inner bushings are to be removed.

                    4.2.2  The locks must be marked to show when locked or unlocked, so anyone could
                            visually see if they are locked or unlocked.

           4.3    Rig floor personnel shall not place bars in holes in the rotary table to turn the rotary.

                    4.3.1   Personnel should not attempt to engage the rotary table locks while the rotary is
                           turning or while the pipe is in motion.

                    4.3.2   While drilling, no one should step on the rotary while it is turning.

                    4.3.3   Rig floor personnel should secure the pipe tongs back away from the rotary area
                     when the rotary is spinning.

                    4.3.4  When the rotary bushing is set on the rig floor, personnel should ensure their feet
                            are out of the way.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 196 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 555
                                      Policy: ROTARY TABLE
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 2


                    4.3.5   Rig personnel are not permitted to jump across any portion of an open rotary slot
                             in the rig floor while performing their duties.

                    4.3.6  No one should physically get inside the open rotary slot while the rotary table is
                         being guided and lowered back into the slot.

                    4.3.7  No one should get between the rotary table and the rotary opening when a rotary
                          supported by pickup lines and snubbed by a tugger is swinging toward the opening.

                    4.3.8  No one should get on top of the rotary table while it is supported by pickup lines
                           or being moved.

                    4.3.9   Rig personnel should ensure that drilling tools such as stabilizers, subs, etc., being
                       removed from the string are not dropped on the rotary cover guards. Such impact
                        can bend the guards, damaging the rotary and making uneven footing for working
                           personnel.

           4.4     Driller shall not attempt to break out drill pipe or collars with the rotary table. Pipe and
                     collar connections must first be broken with two sets of tongs.

           4.5    Ensure that the rig floor is clear of obstructions.

           4.6    Use adequate matting to provide safe footing. Have proper mats covering the table and area
                 around the table to ensure personnel have proper footing while performing their duties.

           4.7    Keep the hole covered at all times.

           4.8    The Driller shall ensure that the proper maintenance is carried out as per preventive
                 maintenance schedule.

           4.9    Any time a rotary bushing is lifted or lowered by the pickup lines, the Hoist Operator
                  should ensure that everyone is in safe position and should move the bushing slowly.

           4.10  A protective barrier should be in place around the rig floor rotary opening once the table is
                removed so personnel shall not slip and fall through (HSE 100.15). The barrier must have
                  a kick rail.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
           6.1   HSE 100.15, General Safety Rules
           6.2   HSE 100.29, Man riding Operations

                                         Policy:  PIPE RACK

   PIPE RACK

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines for pipe rack operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   PROCEDURE
           4.1    Rig personnel should ensure all box and pin thread protectors remain on the tubulars (drill
                    pipe, casing, and collars), until they are added into the drill string. If the pipe shall be stored
                    for a while, the threads should have dope around all the threads.

           4.2     Stripping boards should be used as pipe strips to prevent pipe from shifting on the racks.

           4.3    While rolling pipe or collars from the ends, care should be taken not to stand between two
                       joints. If you are working with someone, you should both communicate with each other
                   before moving any pipe.

           4.4      If drill pipe is on the deck, you should walk around the pipe instead of walking on top of
                    the pipe. This action shall reduce the chance of slips and falls.

           4.5   When rolling pipe, collars, or casing off the pipe racks onto the catwalk, you should not
                   attempt to hold a bar beneath the pipe. Instead, a board should be laid on the catwalk before
                    the pipe is rolled onto the catwalk so a pickup sling can be placed beneath the pipe for
                       lifting purposes.

           4.6    Rig personnel should not stand on the catwalk other than on each end when rolling drill
                  pipe or collars onto the catwalk.

           4.7   When removing pipe from a pipe bin, personnel should not remain inside the bin once the
                           lift slings have been placed on the pipe. Personnel should be out of the area before a lift is
                   attempted.

           4.8    Rig personnel should ensure unneeded objects such as stabilizers, bits, tools, etc., are kept
                    off the catwalk and off the rig floor while work is being performed.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 198 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 560

                                         Policy:  PIPE RACK
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 2


           4.9   When a joint of pipe or collar is being pulled on the catwalk, personnel should remain off
                    the catwalk until the pipe comes to rest.

           4.10   Rig personnel should ensure that sledge hammers, bars, and other hand tools used on the
                  catwalk are returned to an assigned place when the work is completed.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                                   Policy:  USE OF MUD BUCKET

  USE OF MUD BUCKET

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines for mud bucket operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   PROCEDURE

           4.1    Unless pulling a wet string before breaking off a stand of pipe, always tap the pipe with a
                  small brass hammer to see if the pipe is wet or dry. Use a brass hammer for (two reasons):

                    4.1.1    It shall not make any sparks, in case of gases trapped in the rotary pan.

                    4.1.2    If it was to fall into the hole, it could be drilled up. It’s a good practice to secure
                       any tool used around the rotary by a type of lanyard.

           4.2     Training should be given so that all rig floor personnel know the correct operations and
                    safety precautions concerning mud buckets.

           4.3    Ensure a good set of seals is on the mud bucket so that mud does not spray on personnel
                 and the right size inserts for the pipe that is being used.

           4.4    Check the hang-offline and clamps frequently to ensure safe operations.

           4.5    You should not put your hands on the pipe when putting on the mud bucket.

           4.6     After a connection is broken and the pipe is spun out, position the mud bucket around the
                    pipe, secure the door latch, and secure the dump hose onto the mud bucket and into the
              dump drain hole.

                    4.6.1  Make sure to secure the end of the hose.

                    4.6.2  The pressure of the fluid coming out of the hose may cause it to blow out of the
                    dump hole.

           4.7    Crew members should step back as the Driller carefully picks up on the stand / joint.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 200 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 565

                                   Policy:  USE OF MUD BUCKET
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 2



                    4.7.1  The Driller should take care when lifting stand in case threads are not fully backed
                          out

                    4.7.2   Sometimes it could be backed out and depending on the type of threads; it could
                       hang up.

           4.8    Unhook the mud bucket, pull it clear of the drill string and secure it with a pullback hook
                             / strap.

           4.9   When finished, the mud bucket should be washed off and the hinges should be oiled or
                   greased.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

  JERK / DRIVE CHAINS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines for jerk and drive chain operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   PROCEDURE (Jerk Chains, make up or Break out Wires)

           4.1    Examine the jerk chain, or make up and break out wire, each tour to ensure the chain is in
                good condition and not stretched or worn to the point that it could break under tension.

           4.2    The Rig Mechanic should check the pressure regulators in the hydraulic cathead every six
                    (6) months to ensure the foot-pound pull does not exceed the breaking strength of the jerk
                  chain / wire.

           4.3    The Driller should check each guard and/or headache / Cat head post around the Driller’s
                  console on each tour to ensure each is structurally sound.

           4.4    The Driller should observe the position of the jerk chain or wire on the rig floor before
                 each pull to make certain it is not in a floor crack, around slip handles, or around a piece
                   of equipment.

           4.5    The Tong Operators should check the tongs pull pins and tong dies each tour to ensure
                  proper assembly thereof.

           4.6    The Driller should remain alert to the possibility of a Floorman straddling or getting too
                   close to the jerk chain while the chain is in operation. The cathead should be stopped and
                    the endangered man informed of the potential hazard.

           4.7   Do not use spinning chains to make up or to back out tubulars. Spinner wrench or chain
                  tongs are to be used.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 202 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 570

                                  Policy:  JERK / DRIVE CHAINS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 2



    5.0   PROCEDURE (Drive Chains)

           5.1    Rig personnel servicing or working on chain-driven equipment should not place their hands
                on or physically go inside a compartment where chains are moving.

                    5.1.1  The equipment shall first be stopped and the power locked out. A Job Safety
                          Analysis and Permit to Work are required.

           5.2    Rig personnel should ensure that each guard or inspection cover to the drive chains remains
                   closed to contain flying chain fragments should a breakage occur.

           5.3     Personnel working on a broken drive chain should use the proper equipment such as chain
                    tensioners for fitting chains back together.

           5.4     Personnel removing drive chains from sprockets should devise a method of holding back
                   excessive chain as it is being removed to prevent it from building up momentum and going
                   out of control. No one should stand in the direct line of movement of such a chain.

           5.5    Due to the weight of the chain, more than one person should be available to place heavy
                   drive chains on sprockets to prevent mashing of fingers or hands in the process. Most drive
                  chain links are held together with cotter keys. Personnel are to use impact gloves and other
                   applicable personal protective equipment (PPE).

           5.6    Most drive chains are packaged by the manufacturer in containers or rolls too heavy for
                 one person to lift. Rig personnel should get help and/or use lifting equipment if possible
               when handling such chains.

           5.7     Drive-chain assembly most often requires a drive and backup hammer to remove or place
                  a master link in a chain. Due to the close work involved, the person wielding the hammer
                   as well as all parties in the immediate vicinity should use eye protection.

    6.0   RECORDS
         None

    7.0   REFERENCES
           7.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)
           7.2   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)
           7.3   HSE 100.18, Personal Protective Equipment (PPE)

                                    Policy:  ACCUMULATORS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

  ACCUMULATORS

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidelines for accumulator operations and testing.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore  Installation Manager OIM/PIC  is  responsible  for implementing  this
                  procedure during their assigned tour.

           3.2    The Driller and Rig Maintenance Supervisor (RMS) are responsible to conduct monthly
                  accumulator and initial accumulator draw down test.

    4.0   PROCEDURE (Accumulators General)

           4.1   Do not work on accumulator or BOP control panels with the drill string in the BOP. The
                        drill string should be out of the hole or hung off below the BOP rams.

           4.2      If a serious leak of control fluid develops, put the control on the accumulator into (neutral)
                     position. Carry out repairs immediately or as risk assessment / management of change
              (MOC) dictates (see ADM 015).

           4.3    Once the well is secure and well operations permit, repairs can be carried out.

                    4.3.1   Ensure pressure is bled off system before lines are broken.

                    4.3.2   Close valves to save pressure where possible.

           4.4   A Job Safety Analysis and Permit to Work for energy isolation (lock-out / tag-out) are
                    required.

           4.5    Once repairs are complete and before drill string is in the BOP, the control system and/or
                  accumulator shall be function / pressure tested to ensure correct operation. High pressure
                  bypass can be used to pressure test the lines.

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Accumulator Testing)

          Before pressuring up, check accumulator fluid system volumes.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 204 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 575
                                    Policy:  ACCUMULATORS
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 2


           5.1    Check accumulator fluid pressure.

           5.2    Check for any leaks that may occur.

           5.3    Check accumulator reservoir level.

           5.4    Switch off accumulator pumps, air, and electric.

           5.5    Close and open all preventers and check accumulator fluid pressure after each function and
                   record closing time.

                    5.5.1   Adequate pressure and volume should still be present to close one ram and one
                          annular preventer.

                    5.5.2   Pre-charge pressure should still be the same in all accumulator bottles.

           5.6    Switch on accumulator pumps.

           5.7    Record accumulator recharging time.

           5.8    Check BOP closing times and accumulator recharge time with manufacturer’s data for the
                  system.

           5.9    Cycle the annular preventer and check that the pumps shall automatically start when the
                   closing unit pressure has decreased to less than 90% of the accumulator operating pressure.

           5.10   The Driller and Senior Mechanic shall document monthly accumulator inspections and
                        initial BOP nipple up tests.

    6.0   RECORDS

           6.1    Monthly Accumulator Inspection Form and Initial BOP Nipple Up form
           6.2     Function and operational tests

    7.0   REFERENCES

           7.1   HSE 100.08, Permit to Work (PTW)
           7.2   HSE 100.11, Job Safety Analysis (JSA)
           7.3   ADM 015, Management of Change (MOC)

                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 580

                          Policy:   OPERATION OF ADES EQUIPMENT
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1


  OPERATION OF ADES EQUIPMENT

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy for personnel authorized to operate ADES rig equipment.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
           3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for:

                3.1.1 Notifying him of any requests submitted by the Operator or third party to operate rig
                   equipment or systems.

                3.1.2 Ensuring compliance with this standard on or at the rig.

                3.1.3 Ensuring any exceptions to this standard are carried out in accordance with the
                      directions provided by the Rig Manager.

           3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible for obtaining a duly executed letter of agreement attached
               hereto from the Operator prior to issuing authorization to the Operator or any third party to
               operate rig equipment.

    4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

           4.1 Only ADES personnel are permitted to operate rig equipment and systems. While other third-
               party personnel such as fishing tool company representatives, directional drilling consultants,
              wellhead service engineers and Operator Representatives may provide guidance, the actual
               operation of the equipment will be carried out by ADES employees only.

           4.2 Exceptions to this policy require the prior approval of the Vice President. The Rig Manager
             must ensure all of the liability issues regarding personnel injury or death, equipment damage,
             and rig downtime are accepted in writing by the Operator prior to his request for approval for
           non-ADES personnel to operate ADES equipment. A sample letter of agreement is attached
                hereto, and made a part of this standard.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                 Policy:   OPERATING RIG EQUIPMENT AT 100% CAPACITY

Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1




  OPERATING RIG EQUIPMENT AT 100% CAPACITY

    1.0   PURPOSE
           This procedure  describes  the  policies  for  operating  rig equipment  at 100%  capacity  for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEM to provide company policy governing the operation of rig
          equipment continuously at 100% of rated capacity.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring the Operator's Representative and
                 rig crews are aware of this policy, and that it is complied with.

           3.2 All personnel who are responsible for operating rig equipment shall be aware of the rated
               capacity of the equipment they are operating, and shall not operate the equipment beyond its
                rated capacity.


    4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

           4.1 ADES equipment shall be operated up to a maximum of 85% of its rated capacity on a
               continuous basis unless exceptional circumstances require it to be operated at 100% capacity
               as dictated by emergency situations that threaten the integrity of the rig or its equipment or the
                safety of personnel.

           4.2 At no time will ADES equipment be operated above its maximum rated capacity.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                             Policy: PROCEDURE FOR HANGING-OFF
                        TRAVELING EQUIPMENT
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1


  PROCEDURE FOR HANGING-OFF TRAVELING EQUIPMENT

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish standard procedures for the suspension of traveling equipment in the derrick/mast

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC and the senior drilling department supervisor are responsible to the Rig Manager
            for ensuring compliance with this standard.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 The entire weight of the traveling equipment shall be suspended from the crown in accordance with
            the slings and hardware specified in Engineering Standard.

       4.2 Travelling Block shall be secured to main beam, certified pad-eye at crown block.

       4.3 Check hang-off line length, strength, and certification.

       4.4 Hanging Line often stay in the derrick; it must be secured when finished.

       4.5 It is expressly prohibited to support the total weight of the Top Drive (TDS), swivel, hook, and
           block by pinning off on the guide rails, setting down on the bottom guide rail stops or by setting
          any weight on the rotary via drill pipe, etc.

       4.6 This standard should not be construed to infer the bottom guide rail stops are not adequate to
           support the weight of the TDS. They are acceptable to bear the weight of the TDS only.


    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                   Policy  HIGH PRESSURE VESSELS, PIPES, AND HOSES
Revision: 03-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 2



  HIGH PRESSURE VESSELS, PIPES, AND HOSES

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance on high pressure.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is responsible for implementing this procedure during
             their assigned tour.

    4.0   PROCEDURE (Hull Structure)

           5.1     All high-pressure systems are to be reviewed and approved by the relevant Class Society
                     prior to installation as applicable.

           5.2     High-pressure vessels, piping, and hoses shall be tested, inspected, and certified.

           5.3     High-pressure air or fluid lines, hoses, and piping shall be pressure tested to certified
                 working pressures at least on an annual basis.

           5.4     Steel lines and piping configurations, such as choke and kill and standpipe manifolds and
                 any interconnected fluid piping shall be pressure tested and thickness gauge tested on an
                  annual basis.

           5.5    Chicksans are to be inspected, pressure tested, and certified by a ADES-approved third-
                   party once each year.

           5.6     Testing, inspections, and any remedial measures shall be thoroughly documented. This
                 documentation is to be maintained onboard the installation until the subsequent inspection
                        is completed and filed.

           5.7     Spots where measurements indicate deterioration has occurred shall be clearly marked and
                  recorded to ensure that future measurements are taken from the same spot.

           5.8     Pressure testing shall be recorded and witnessed. The original records and certification
                 documentation shall be maintained on the installation.

           5.9     Pre-set pressure relief valves shall be inspected and pressure and function tested annually
                by a competent person, normally from a third-party company.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 209 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2



    5.0   PROCESS AND DEFINITIONS
         High pressure is considered to be 200 pounds per square inch (psi) or greater.

    6.0   RECORDS
           Test records and certifications with corresponding files, on board, current

    7.0   REFERENCES
        HSE 100.22, Pressurized Systems, Vessels, and Piping

                             Policy   PAD-EYE DESIGN AND USE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 3


  PAD-EYE DESIGN AND USE

    1.0   PURPOSE
         The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance to ensure the integrity of pad-eyes and their
             installation.

    2.0   SCOPE
       This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The Offshore Installation Manager OIM/PIC is individually responsible for implementing this
           procedure during their tour.

    4.0   GENERAL

           4.1    Pad-eyes shall meet recognized standards for design and shall be installed in accordance
                  with approved placement and weld procedures.

           4.2    Recognized standards include the applicable Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit (MODU)
                 Code, American Petroleum Institute (API) recommended practices and standards.

           4.3    The OIM  / Tool pusher shall submit a Management of Change (MOC) form in HSE
                 Dashboard prior to pad-eye installation (see ADM 015).

    5.0   PROCEDURE (Proof Load Tests)

           5.1     After the installation of a pad-eye, a proof load test with load at 150% of the rated load
                     shall be performed. Prior to load testing the pad-eye, welding is to be 100% non-destructive
                     tested (NDT).

           5.2    The load test shall cover the worst side-load conditions that the pad-eye is designed to
                  handle and operate.

           5.3    For example, if a pad-eye is designed to accommodate side loads up to 45 degrees to the
                  plane of the pad-eye, the pad-eye shall be loaded at 150% of the rated load with directions
                up to 45 degrees away from the plane of the pad-eye. Testing criteria is to be confirmed by
            ADES Engineering Department as it is pad-eye specific.

    6.0   PROCEDURE (Adequacy of Supporting Structure)

           6.1    The OIM shall submit a Management of Change (MOC) into HSE Dashboard to assess and
                  confirm the adequacy of the structure that the pad-eye is welded onto and loaded to the
                    rated load.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 211 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 610

                             Policy   PAD-EYE DESIGN AND USE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 2of 3


           6.2   A pad-eye shall NOT be installed on the following structures (unless an engineering study
                  has been completed):

                    6.2.1   Derrick

                    6.2.2  Crown block

                    6.2.3   Piping

                    6.2.4   Side shell

                    6.2.5   Jack-up legs

                    6.2.6  Any other structure where high strength steel alloys are believed to have been used

           6.3     All pad-eye installations are to be approved via the MOC process.

    7.0   PROCEDURE (Guidelines for Use of Pad-eyes)

           7.1    Pad-eyes are considered critical lifting gear and therefore shall be used strictly for their
                   intended purposes.

           7.2    Pad-eyes are designed with limits on the load directions, particularly on side loading.
                   Therefore, when using pad-eyes, the directions of the loads shall not violate the design
                     limits of the load directions.

           7.3   When using a pad-eye for lifting and/or carrying loads, the increase in load due to dynamics
                     shall be kept to a minimum. More specifically, the load should be lifted or released as
                  slowly as possible, particularly when the pad-eye would be loaded sideways due to
                  swinging.

           7.4     Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM) pad-eyes should only be used for the purpose
                    specified by the OEM.

    8.0   PROCEDURE (Pad-eye Designs)

          Pad-eyes with rated loads exceeding six (6) short tons, or pad-eyes that shall be loaded sideways
         more than 45 degrees from the plane of the pad-eye, shall be designed and engineered by the ADES
           Engineering Department.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 212 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                             Policy   PAD-EYE DESIGN AND USE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 3



    9.0   PROCEDURE (Material Selection and Welding Procedures)

          For pad-eyes to be designed and installed, the material selection and welding procedures shall
          conform to the requirements specified in the Engineering Drawings.

   10.0  RECORDS

           10.1   Pad-eye certifications, on-site, current

           10.2    Non-destructive Test (NDT) reports, on-site, five years

   11.0  REFERENCES

           11.1  HSE 100.20, Lifting Gear

           11.2  ADM 015, Management of Change (MOC)

   SKID IN DERRICK AND CANTILEVER

    1.0   PURPOSE
       This work instruction describes Skid in Derrick and Cantilever operation for ADVANCED ENERGY
     SYSTEM to assure operation is completed in a safe and effective manner.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

           3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
          implementing this procedure.
           3.2    Barge Engineer and Tool pusher are responsible to lead this operation.
           3.3    Marine, Maintenance and Drilling crew are responsible to perform below operations in
           accordance to below stated instructions.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Job Preparation and Tools:
                 •       All Handrails under the Cantilever and Derrick in STBD and PORT sides to be
                       removed and secured before skid in Cantilever and make sure area is secured and
                             identify to prevent failing.
                 •     The pathway of skidding (Gear Teeth or guide rails) to be checked
                 •    BOP should be secured and tied up and the BOP Bridge crane hook to latched up.
                 •      Secure work platform.
                 •      Pick up and lay down mouse hole.
                 •     Remove flow line ditch cover All hoses to hung up and clear from any
                           Obstruction.
                 •      Radio communication as required
                 •       All Electric cable to be free during skid the cantilever.
                 •      Pick up V.Door using drill floor airhost.
                 •      Loose Derrick escape Lubricate skid Plate and service grease.
                 •      Visual check PORT-STBD-Under Cantilever before start skid into Center
                 •      Confirm Work plat form is secured
                 •      Confirm no drop objective before skid in the Cantilever or Derrick.
                 •       All hoses should be disconnected and hanged up to prevent obstruction while
                           skid out.
                 •      Prepare the OEM recommended type of grease to be use pre-skidding.

                 •        Install All Handrails and secure all area to prevent failing.

                         use 2 tag line

  Policy    PROCEDURES FOR ADJUSTING RIG FLOORS THAT ARE OUT OF LEVEL ON
                 JACKUPS DUE TO CANTILEVER DEFLECTION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1


  PROCEDURES FOR ADJUSTING RIG FLOORS THAT ARE OUT OF LEVEL ON JACKUPS DUE
  TO CANTILEVER DEFLECTION

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish procedures for adjusting rig floors that are found to be out of level due to cantilever
         beam sag, or when the cantilever hold-down structures have worn to an extent such that the rig
            floor is not level.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring that the procedures contained herein
            are complied with whenever it is discovered that the rig floor is out of level.

    4.0   PROCEDURES
       4.1 Whenever jack-up cantilever beams are extended 40 feet or more beyond the transom after pre-
            loading, the rig floor shall be checked for level and re-leveled as necessary to correct for any
          misalignment caused by cantilever deflection.

       4.2 True level of the rig floor should be initially checked on top of the rotary table with a 5 foot
            carpenter's level after rig is fully preloaded and jacked to operating air gap. To level the rig floor,
           jack the high side of the rig down until the rig floor is level as indicated on console level indicator.
          Re-check rig floor level. Continue jacking .01 degree in the downward direction, then jack the rig
         up to obtain desired level to ensure that the brakes are set with the rig being jacked in the upward
            direction upon obtaining final leveling. Rig level shall not to exceed 0.3 degrees in any direction
          measured on the rig floor control console level indicator.

       4.3 Additional leveling may be performed of to 0.5 degrees correction by using the jacking bolts that
            are normally provided at the derrick leg foundations. When the desired level is achieved, add shims
           as required to fill the gap, and adjust the jacking bolts back down so that the weight of the derrick
              is firmly resting on the shims.

       4.4 If these methods are not successful, please contact Rig Manager for assistance as needed.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

   RIG DOWN AND SECURE WORK PLATFORM


    1.0   PURPOSE
           This work instruction describes “Rigging Down and Securing Work-Platform” operation for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS to assure that the operation is completed in a safe and effective
          manner.
    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
          implementing this procedure.
       3.2 Driller is responsible for leading this operation
       3.3 Drilling crew is responsible for performing the below operations in accordance to the below stated
            instructions

    4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

        4.1. Job Preparation and Tools
          •   All required shackles for the job have the safety pins attached
          •  A total of 4 prepared, inspected, and function tested Air Hoists
          •   Proper Housekeeping on Work Platform
          •  Removing all tools on the work platform if any
          •   Securing all handrails and grating plates
          •   In case of using safety, chain attached under the rotary table; make sure it is well secured and
                   all the shackles have safety pins in place.

       4.2 Safety Procedures Required
         •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential dropped
                object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height, hand injury,
                  slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side loading on lifting gear).
         •  Review work instructions, JRA, rig specific procedures and assure compliance with procedures
             from ADES Well Control Manual.
         •  Ensure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
              assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained between
                   all crews.
         •   In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.

                      train the crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
              •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
              •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the JRA
                 and capture best practices and lessons learned.

           4.3 Work Instructions
                    1-  Confirm that all handrails are secured and visual inspection is maintained at all times
                    2-  Position the drilling crew at each air hoist
                    3-  2 persons to climb on the work platform and attach the air hoists to each pad eye.
                        •  Confirm good securing and that the safety pins of the shackles are installed
                        •   Full PPE is a MUST
                    4-  Pick up the work platform Slowly to the required position
                    5-  Install a total of 4 chains and make sure they are properly secured, then remove the
                            air hoists


    5.0   RECORDS
         None


    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

           Policy     RIG UP WORK PLATFORM OVER CLUSTER OR PLATFORM
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

   RIG UP WORK PLATFORM OVER CLUSTER OR PLATFORM

    1.0   PURPOSE
           This work instruction describes “Rig Up Work Platform over Cluster or platform” operation for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS to assure that the operation is completed in a safe and effective
          manner.
    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1    The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
              implementing this procedure.
       3.2     Driller is responsible for leading this operation
       3.3     Drilling crew is responsible for performing the below operations in accordance to the below
                stated instructions
    4.0   PROCEDURES

             4.1. Job Preparation and Tools
                     •   All required shackles for the job have the safety pins attached
                     •  A total of 4 prepared, inspected, and function tested Air Hoists
                     •   Proper Housekeeping on Work Platform
                     •  Removing all tools on the work platform if any
                     •   Securing all handrails and grating plates
                     •   In case of using safety, chain attached under the rotary table; make sure it is well
                          secured and all the shackles have safety pins in place.
             4.2. Safety Procedures Required
             •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential
                 dropped object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height,
                hand injury, slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side
                   loading on lifting gear).
             •  Review work instructions, JRA, rig specific procedures and assure compliance with
                  procedures from ADES Well Control Manual.
             •  Ensure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
                  assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained
                 between all crews.
             •   In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.

                 Notes
                 •  These work instructions are prepared by the team and can be changed to add more
                         specific steps to provide the best possible instructions.
                 •  They should be reviewed before each task to prevent any unsafe act or condition and
                         to train the crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
                 •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
                 •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the
                JRA and capture best practices and lessons learned.

             4.3. Work Instructions

                   1- Confirm that all handrails are secured and visual inspection is maintained at all times
                   2-  Position the drilling crew at each air hoist
                   3- 2 persons to climb on the work platform and attach the air hoists to each pad eye.
                        •  Confirm good securing and that the safety pins of the shackles are installed
                        •   Full PPE is a MUST
                   4-  Pick up the work platform Slowly and remove all lifting chains
                   5- Crews to be depart the work platform to start lowering
                   6- Lower the work platform using the air hoists under observation from the leader, once
                         the work platform reaches the required position stop all air hoists
                   7- Crew planned to embark on the platform must wear all required PPE and the required
                              fall protection tools.
                   8-  Secure the work platform using the air hoist under the observation of the leader of the
                          job. Once it reaches the required position, stop all air hoists
                   9-  Secure the work platform with 4 chains and make sure they are installed properly to
                       prevent any slipping.
                 10- Once the work platform is secured, slack off the air hoists and remove them
                 11- All handrails should be secured and the gratings must be in a good condition and be
                        properly secured.
                 12- In case there is a need to remove grating, a minimum number of 2 personnel are
                        required for this job, this is to ensure a proper handling of the grating and ensure the
                         control of drop objects.
                 13- Always make sure proper housekeeping is maintained especially after the completion
                        of jobs such as Nippling up and Nippling down of BOP.


    5.0   RECORDS
         None


    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

   P/U & RIH RABBITED 2 7/8” VACUUM STRING ON 3 ½” DP

    1.0   PURPOSE
           This work instruction describes P/U & RIH Rabbited 2 7/8” Vacuum string on 3 1/2” D.P.
           operation for ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEM to assure operation is completed in a safe and
            effective manner.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore operations.
    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1 The Offshore Installation Manager (OIM) and the Tool pusher on duty are responsible for
          implementing this procedure.
       3.2 Driller is responsible to lead this operation.
       3.3 Marine crew and Drilling crew are responsible to perform below operations in accordance to
          below stated instructions.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

           4.1 Job Preparation and Tools:
                   Elevator (slip type elevator for TBG), slips, single joint elevator with two leg sling, safety
                  clamp, Caliper Rabbit to correct size of tubing, air hoist, power tong and ropes.
              Be sure that all handling tools in good condition and certified, cleaned dies and all bolts
                 equipped with safety pins.

            4.2 Safety Procedures Required:
             •  Conduct a Tool Box Meeting and discuss control of work tools with focus on potential
                 dropped object (both static and dynamic), body position, line of fire, working at height,
                hand injury, slips/trips, energy sources and lifting plan where applicable (avoid side
                   loading on lifting gear).
             •  Review work instructions, JRA, rig specific procedures and assure compliance with
                  procedures from ADES Well Control Manual.
             •  Ensure that the work instructions are clear and well understood by crew members, specific
                  assignments to be given to crew members and adequate communication is maintained
                 between all crews.
             •   In case there is any unsafe act or conditions, stop the job and re-plan.
          Notes
                 •  These work instructions are prepared by the team and can be changed to add more
                         specific steps to provide the best possible instructions.

                 •  They should be reviewed before each task to prevent any unsafe act or condition and
                         to train the crew to manage a good and safe working environment.
                 •  Work Area housekeeping is a MUST before task start and after task completion.
                 •  A de-brief to be conducted follow task completion to assess the effectiveness of the
                JRA and capture best practices and lessons learned.
           4.3 Procedures
                 •  Conduct safety meeting with the people about -this operation (pre-job safety meeting)
                    be sure all crewmembers know his part and duties.
                 •  R/U 2 7/8” slip type elevator on top drive link, air hoist to be ready with 2 7/8 SJE.
                 •  Marine crew arrange 2 7/8” TBG pipe on catwalk with crane, keep hands off pipe
                       bundles, use tag line on bundle, stay out from under load. Inspect slings for damage.
                Two rigger and banks man to give proper signal to the crane operator.
                 •   Experienced Air hoist operator to lower the air host attached w/ 2 7/8” SJE down to
                       catwalk. Be sure that the air hoist wire proper spooling on the air hoist drum and good
                    communication with R/A on the cat walk.
                 •  R/A on the catwalk latch the SJE on the first 2 7/8” blind end tubing then give the
                        signal to the air hoist operator to start lifting the first joint to the rig floor.
                 •   (good communication between the man who operate the air hoist and be sure that the
                        single joint elevator safety pin in place).
                 •   Lifting the TBG JT to the rig floor, Floor men to watching the air hoist wire to avoid
                       entangle with A-frame, control the joint swing movement using a rope and lower it in
                        the mouse hole and unlatch SJE.
                 •   Driller tilt the top drive link towards mouse hole and F/M latch the slip type elevator
                     around the joint , driller start to pick up the joint by TOP drive and lower it in the rotary
                          table, three F/M to put the slips then driller lower the weight on the slips , floor men to
                       attached the safety clamp around the joint & unlatch the elevator.
                 •   (good communication between all the crew and give proper signal to the driller, watch
                     your hand when latching slip type elevator be sure the latch was proper closing around
                        the pipe)
                 •   Repeat steps 4, 5 & 6 with the same precautions to pick up 2 7/8” perforated joint, latch
                           slip type elevator around the pipe and start pick up by top drive , F/M unscrew the pin
                        protector and apply TBG dope on the pin then stab in the joint gently on the joint in
                        the rotary table, two floor men to help the power tong operator to engage the power
                      tong around the pipe then power tong operator start to spin in & torqued the two joint
                        together , secured the power tong away from top drive movement.
                 •   (only guide the power tong from the green marked hand).
                 •   Driller take the string weight gently on the elevator, F/M remove the safety clamp &
                         pull out the slips then driller start RIH carefully thru BOP & well head. Floor men put
                        the slips, driller to lower the string weight on the slips, floor men attached safety clamp
                    and unlatch the elevator.
                 •  P/U and drift the rest of 2 7/8” vacuum string as follow:


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 226 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 628

            Policy    P/U & RIH RABBITED 2 7/8” VACUUM STRING ON 3 ½” DP
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

                     •   Floor man lower the rabbit to the R/A at catwalk using the rope.
                     •  R/A place the rabbit in the JT on catwalk and latch the single joint elevator.
                     •   Air hoist operator picked up the joint to rig floor, control the pipe swinging using
                            the rope.
                     •  F/M to unscrew the pin protector and remove the rabbit (keep your feet clear from
                       below the tubing until the rabbit is removed).
                     •  Screw the pin protector again and lower the joint in mouse hole.
                     •   Unlatch single joint elevator, latch the slip type elevator and driller start to pick up
                            the joint from the mouse hole, F/M unscrew pin protector, apply dope on the pin
                          then stab in the joint on the string in the rotary and torqued same using power tong
                         with the same precautions mentioned above.
                     •   Repeated the steps above until we finished all 2 7/8” tubing assembly, RIH
                            carefully while pass the RTTS packer thru BOP and well head. (Using safety clamp
                                  all the way while picking up the tubing until you have enough weight).
                     •  RIH and drifted 3 ½ “D.P. stands from the derrick as follow:
                        •  Change 2 7/8” elevator to 3 ½” drill pipe elevator. (Turn elevators and bails
                                 in desired direction, Shackle air hoist into the elevators and take the slack on
                                    air hoist. Open the pins of elevator ears and pick up on hoist, Hold the
                                elevators tight and lower it on the floor, ensure your foot is not under the
                                elevators while it is being lowered on to the floor and install the other
                                elevator with the same way).
                        •   Derrick man check monkey board (safety harness & fall arrester)
                        •  Cover the string in the hole, Inspect the rabbit & rope, install hanger on top
                               drive bail, hang drift of hanger and lift top drive up to monkey board level.
                         (Do not stand under the drift while lifting traveling block in derrick or when
                                derrick man removes it)
                        •   Driller tilt the link to enable the Derrick man on monkey board to take the
                                 rabbit and put it inside the 3 ½ “stand, keep hands, feet & other body parts
                                 clear of tools joint until drift is removed from the bottom of the drill pipe.
                        •   Control the stand movement using the rope, Stab the stand on the string in
                                the hole, Never place hands on box or pin of pipe while stabbing, Be aware
                               of the position of your hands that it not caught between stand being lifted and
                                the next stand adjacent to it, lock the rotary table to avoid turning the
                                 string, Spinning up using pipe spinner and torque the pipe using rig tongs,
                          Never put hands on pipe while placing spinner or rig tongs on the pipe and
                               take safe position while the driller apply torque on the pipe.
                        •   Cont. RIH with drifted 3 ½” drill pipe with the same precautions and control
                             running speed.
    5.0   RECORDS
         None
    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                  Policy  PROVISION OF RIG SYSTEM CONNECTIONS AND
                   SERVICES TO THIRD PARTY EQUIPMENT
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1


  PROVISION OF RIG SYSTEM CONNECTIONS AND SERVICES TO THIRD PARTY EQUIPMENT

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish a standard for the provision of auxiliary services such as compressed air, and electric
         power to third party contractors.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring compliance with this standard.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 ADES will not provide:

                4.1.1 Compressed air to well testing equipment, burner booms, or other equipment or systems
                        that connect to or contain wellbore fluids, unless otherwise specified in the contract
                   between ADES and the client.

                4.1.2 Primary compressed air, water, or electric power for man-diving operations.

       4.2 Systems or equipment provided by the Operator or other third party to supply compressed air to
           well testing equipment, burner booms, and other equipment and systems that connect to or
           contain wellbore fluids are subject to review and approval by ADES Maintenance/ Rig Manager.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

               Policy  SUSPENDING THIRD PARTY EQUIPMENT IN THE DERRICK
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1


  SUSPENDING THIRD PARTY EQUIPMENT IN THE DERRICK

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy regarding safe operating practices when suspending third party
          equipment in the derrick.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible for ensuring that third party equipment is suspended in the derrick in
           accordance with this standard.

    4.0   PROCEDURES
           Third party equipment that needs to be temporarily suspended inside the derrick shall be suspended
         on an existing rig floor tugger line (K5 or bigger) unless there is a designated hang off line installed
            in the derrick. Where there is adequate equipment provided, i.e. lifting subs which fit into the
            elevators, the traveling blocks may be used. In all cases, the OIM/PIC shall ensure that all the
           requirements of HSE Policy are complied with, and all sheaves, hobbles, and hardware used to
           secure it must have a safe working load (SWL) that is twice that of the rating of the equipment
           being used.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                           Policy  COMMENCING WELL TESTING
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 1


  COMMENCING WELL TESTING

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish ADES policy for conducting well testing.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
         The OIM/PIC is responsible for ensuring the senior drilling supervisor is aware of this policy, and
             that exceptions to this policy require prior approval by the Rig Manager.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 The initial opening of well test tools shall only be conducted during daylight hours. Subsequent
          opening and closing may be carried out at night, provided that pressure-containing piping and
          equipment has not been opened or repaired.

       4.2 This policy ensures that any defects or leaks can be observed during daylight hours, which makes
            detection, troubleshooting and possible evacuation of non-essential personnel safer and faster to
           accomplish.

       4.3 Exceptions to this policy can be made as long as prior approval is obtained from the Rig Manager,
          and maximum safety precautions are taken, including but not limited to providing a means of:

                4.3.1 Pressure-testing piping and equipment that has been opened for repair or inspection is
                      carried out to confirm the integrity of the system prior to opening the well.

                4.3.2 Adequate lighting to allow the system to be visually monitored during well testing
                      operations.

       4.4 If there is any conflict regarding the requirements of this standard between the parties involved, the
          most stringent of the existing policies in place shall apply.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                        Policy TEMPORARY WELL TEST INSTALLATIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                      Page 1of 3


  TEMPORARY WELL TEST INSTALLATIONS

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide a policy for the installation of temporary well test lines and equipment installed on
       ADES drilling rigs.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring that:

                3.1.1 The Rig Manager shall be notified whenever information is received that the Operator
                     intends to install temporary test lines on or at the rig.

                3.1.2 A copy of this standard shall be provided to the Operator Representative at the rig and
                        that he understands the requirements set forth herein.

                3.1.3 No temporary well test lines shall be installed or used at or on the rig without the approval
                     of the Rig Manager, and meeting the requirements of this standard.

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible through the operations chain of command up to and including the
           V.P. Operation to ensure that:

                3.2.1 Shore based Operator Representatives shall be provided a copy of this standard and are
                  aware of the requirements set forth herein.

                3.2.2 The documentation package specified below shall be requested of the Operator upon
                       receipt of notification by the Operator to install well test piping and equipment on the
                          rig.

       3.3 The Maintenance Manager is responsible to the Cairo Office Management to assist with the review
          and evaluation of the documentation package specified below, and to enlist the assistance of
           Technical Operations when necessary to resolve any potential hazards to personnel, equipment or
            the environment and any concerns with the installation of well test equipment and systems.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Installation of temporary well test equipment and systems should be addressed at the well planning
            stage. Upon notification by a client representative of the intent to install temporary well test
          equipment and systems on or at any ADES rig, the Rig Manager shall:


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 231 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 638

                        Policy TEMPORARY WELL TEST INSTALLATIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 3


                4.1.1 Notify the OIM/PIC.
                4.1.2 Notify the Maintenance Manager.
                4.1.3 Provide a copy of this standard to the client representative.

       4.2 Upon notification, the Rig Manager shall ensure the client is requested to provide a documentation
          package of the well testing equipment and systems intended to be installed on the rig consisting of
           but not necessarily limited to the following:

                4.2.1 A description of the equipment and systems and where they are intended to be installed
                  on the rig. Such descriptions shall include a description of specific features that are
                     required to be addressed including, but not necessarily limited to the following:
                        •   Potential hazards of uncontrolled pressure release.
                        •  Well bore effluent leakage or spillage, and containment methods.
                        •  The temporary creation of new hazardous areas/zones, and the appropriate
                                   electrical fittings, cable, and gas detection system and alarms.
                        •  Any safety devices, such as pressure relief valves or similar devices and their
                               pressure settings.
                        •   Piping, pressure vessels, heaters, and separators have sufficient working
                               pressure rating suitable for the service intended.

                4.2.2 One-line piping drawings showing all piping materials intended to be used, along with
                    evidence that qualified welding procedures and appropriately qualified welders will be
                   used to install such piping systems.

                4.2.3 Any requirements for power, water, or fuel that is needed to be supplied by the rig that is
                   needed to support such equipment specifically prohibits any connection of rig air supply
                      to well testing Equipment.

                4.2.4 Electrical power distribution and control circuit diagrams required to operate the
                   equipment

       4.4 The Rig Manager shall enlist the assistance of the Maintenance Manager to review same and advice
           of any issues or concerns with regard to the safety of personnel or the equipment, and protection of
            the environment. Should any questions or issues arise, the Rig Manager shall contact the Operator
           Representative to communicate such concerns, and request that such issues be addressed. If the
             client is unwilling or unable to address such concerns, the Rig Manager shall notify the V.P.
           Operations with a request for assistance in resolving any issues at a higher level.

       4.5 If resolution of any outstanding issues are not forthcoming by the time the installation of such well
              test equipment is commenced, and the OIM/PIC believes that potential hazards to personnel or the
          environment are unacceptable, he shall notify the Rig Manager.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 232 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 638

                        Policy TEMPORARY WELL TEST INSTALLATIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 3


       4.6 If resolution of any outstanding issues are not forthcoming by the time the installation of such well
              test equipment  is being commissioned, and the OIM/PIC believes that potential hazards to
           personnel or the environment are unacceptable, he shall notify the Rig Manager.

       4.7 If resolution of any outstanding issues are not forthcoming by the time the client intends to actually
           use such equipment or systems to conduct well testing, and the OIM/PIC believes that potential
           hazards to personnel, environment and equipment are unacceptable, he shall notify the Rig Manager
           of his intentions to advise the onboard Operator Representative that such well testing shall not be
         commenced until such hazards are addressed.

       4.8 Once it is agreed to schedule a time for commencing a well test, the OIM/PIC shall enlist the
           cooperation and assistance of the Operator Representative to plan and conduct a meeting with all
       ADES crews and third parties to review the well test plan, personnel responsibilities, chain of
         command, primary and secondary means of communication, required materials, equipment and
            safety gear, and to conduct JRAs for all potential hazards.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None
