  WELL TESTING WITH DRILL PIPE

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish policies and procedures for the use of company-owned drill pipe for well testing
            operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for:

                3.1.1 Communicating this policy to the Operator Representative at the rig whenever a request
                          is submitted to use company-owned drill pipe for the purpose of carrying out well testing
                      operations.

                3.1.2 Advising the Rig Manager whenever such a request is submitted by the Operator
                     Representative at the rig.

                3.1.3 Ensuring that company-owned drill pipe is not used for well testing until written approval
                          is received from the Rig Manager

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible to his respective operations chain of command up to and including
            the Vice President for:

                3.2.1 Submitting notification of any requests received from the Operator for utilizing company-
                 owned drill pipe for well testing operations.

                3.2.2 Preparing and submitting the sample  letter attached to the appropriate Operator
                     Representative and conducting all other communications with the Operator until an
                   agreement is reached. A sample form letter is included on page 3 of this policy for
                     guidance.

                3.2.3 Submitting written directions to the OIM/PIC regarding the decision to or not to conduct
                    well testing operations with company-owned drill pipe.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 234 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                           Policy WELL TESTING WITH DRILL PIPE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Drill pipe will NOT be used in conducting well testing operations.

       4.2 Exceptions to this policy may be granted by the Vice President for operation, the Operator shall
           accept (in writing) the responsibility for all costs related to inspection, repair, and replacement of
              all drill pipe components damaged during well test operations.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

                           Policy ACIDIZING THROUGH TUBULARS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020

   ACIDIZING THROUGH TUBULARS

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish company policy regarding the use of tubular goods owned by ADES for well acidizing
                                                                                                  operations.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring that:


                3.1.1 Requests received by the Operator's Representative to acidize through tubular goods
                 owned by ADES are communicated immediately to the Rig Manager, and


                3.1.2 No acidizing operations are conducted with tubular goods owned by ADES until
                    permission is granted by the Rig Manager.

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible through the Operations chain of command to the Regional Vice
           President to communicate any requests received by Operator Representatives to use tubular goods
         owned by ADES for the purpose of carrying out well acidizing operations, and to prepare and
          submit a letter (see sample attached) to the Operator stating the conditions under which such
            tubulars can be used.

       3.3 The Vice President is responsible for making the decision to either deny or grant requests submitted
         by the Operator for using tubular goods owned by ADES for well acidizing operations, and if so
            granted, ensure the conditions set forth in the sample letter attached hereto are complied with.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 Drill strings owned by ADES shall NOT be used to conduct well acidizing operations.

       4.2 Operator furnished work strings and related surface equipment will be utilized to conduct acidizing
            operations.

       4.3 Exceptions to this policy may be granted by the Vice President for operations to the maximum of
        15% HCL concentration.
       4.4 Each rig should have a dedicated logbook for recording acid jobs in details.

                                    Policy HAMMER UNIONS

  HAMMER UNIONS

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide policies, procedures, and guidelines for the selection, installation, and use of Hammer
           unions.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM is responsible to the Rig Manager for ensuring:

                3.1.1 Rig personnel are aware of this standard, and that  it is referenced in all Job Risk
                   Assessments  (JRAs)  that  pertain  to  the  identification,  installation,  assembly,
                     disassembly, and pressure testing of hammer unions.

                3.1.2 JRAs shall be created to identify the hazard of connecting hammer unions that may be
                      similar in appearance but are of a slightly different configuration and shall reference this
                     standard to provide guidance for the crew members involved in this work.

                3.1.3 Connecting and disconnecting hammer unions in piping systems shall not be carried out
                    without a Work Permit that references this standard along with a JRA.

                3.1.4 Third parties and third-party equipment shall comply with the requirements this standard.
                     In the event of discovering any discrepancies regarding such compliance, or if unions
                     designated for connection with ADES rig piping systems cannot be identified as to their
                    corresponding Fig. No., the OIM shall advise the Client Representative onboard of the
                    discrepancy and request that these unions be replaced, and shall ensure that any
                    connections to rig systems with these unions shall not be carried out until they can be
                        identified. All such communications with the Client Representative shall be forwarded
                      to the Rig Manager.

                3.1.5 All hammer unions installed in ADES piping systems that could potentially be used to
                    connect with third party systems or equipment shall be identified and labeled in
                    accordance with the requirements of this standard.

                3.1.6 A copy of the third-party one-line, piping schematic diagrams required shall be provided
                      to the senior maintenance supervisor on the rig, and  all third-parties shall submit
                      revisions to these diagrams whenever modifications to their systems or equipment result
                      in a change or an addition to the hammer unions that could be used to connect with ADES


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 239 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 648

                                    Policy HAMMER UNIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 5


                   piping systems. Copies of the schematic diagrams shall be maintained in a readily
                      accessible file onboard the rig. Schematics of third-party systems and equipment that are
                  removed from the rig shall be purged from this file.

                3.1.7 All field requisitions generated for the purchase of hammer unions shall reference this
                     standard and shall contain all of the information required below.

       3.2 All personnel responsible for creating, reviewing, and approving field requisitions and purchase
           orders shall ensure:

                3.2.1 All hammer unions are ordered with a butt-weld prep with the exception of the Swivel
                   gooseneck hammer unions described that must be ordered from the Swivel gooseneck
                     manufacturer.

                3.2.2 The pipe size and schedule (or wall thickness) of the piping system that the hammer union
                          is to be installed shall be specified for each hammer union specified in the requisition,
                  and the vendor shall be required to machine a butt-weld prep onto the ends of the hammer
                   union sub that will match with the piping it is to be welded to.

                3.2.3 All hammer unions have a pressure rating that is suitable for the piping system into which
                               it is to be installed, as well as whether it must be rated for sour gas service.

       3.3 The senior Mechanic supervisor on the rig shall visually inspect third-party systems and equipment
         on a frequent basis using the one-line schematic drawing supplied by third-parties as required. All
         hammer unions that are identified on these schematic diagrams that could be connected to an ADES
           piping system shall be labeled in accordance with Discrepancies shall be reported to the OIM upon
           completion of all inspections.

    4.0   PROCEDURES
       4.1 All hammer unions installed in piping systems and on the ends of hose assemblies from the date of
            issue of this standard shall be butt-welded. When ordering hammer unions, the pipe size and
           schedule (or wall thickness) onto which the union is to be installed shall be specified so that the
           proper butt-weld preparation is machined on both the female and male sub of each union by the
           vendor. Hammer unions that are threaded in existing fixed rig piping systems that have a working
           pressure below 2,000psi, and where no connection with third party equipment is likely to be made
             shall be replaced with butt-welded unions only when it becomes necessary to replace the unions or
            the piping system adjacent to such unions due to damage or deterioration.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 240 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 648

                                    Policy HAMMER UNIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 3 of 5


       4.2 All hammer union connections installed in ADES rig piping systems where temporary connections
           with hammer unions are possible shall be conspicuously labeled as to the size and Fig. No. of the
          union that is installed. Such labeling shall be made of a durable material that is suitable for the
           surrounding ambient conditions and be legibly marked with the Fig. No. of the union that is
             installed. Such labeling is not required for hammer unions installed in fixed piping systems where
       they have been installed to allow the convenient replacement of a valve, filter, or other piping system
          component for the purpose of maintenance as depicted below:





       4.4 Hammer Unions on Rotary Hoses, Standpipe Goosenecks, Swivels, and Top Drive "S" Pipes:
        Hammer unions fitted to rotary hoses, standpipe goosenecks, and Top Drive "S" Pipes shall be
            rated for a minimum of 10,000 psi, and shall be butt-welded unless otherwise specified below.

                4.4.1 Top Drive "S" Pipes: As of the issuance of this standard, most "S" Pipes supplied and
                      currently installed on Top Drives have hammer unions that are affixed to the ends with
                    threaded NPT (National Pipe Thread) connections. Whenever it becomes necessary to
                     replace the "S" Pipe for any reason, it shall be ordered with unions that are butt-welded
                      to the "S" Pipe at each end.

                4.4.2 Swivel Goosenecks: As of the issuance of this standard, most Swivel goosenecks are
                         fitted with hammer unions that are affixed to the end of the gooseneck with an NPT-
                    threaded connection. The reason for this is that most swivel goosenecks are manufactured
                  from cast steel that does not lend itself to welding. In addition, most hammer unions
                     supplied with swivel goosenecks are a special type of union that has a tolerance for
                    misalignment. Until  the OEM (Original Equipment Manufacturer) of the swivel
                   gooseneck revises this type of connection to accommodate a butt-welded connection, all
                hammer unions ordered for the purpose of replacing this specific union shall be ordered
                       directly from the Swivel gooseneck OEM (No exceptions!).


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 241 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 648

                                    Policy HAMMER UNIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 4 of 5


       4.5 Each time a hammer union is disassembled and reassembled, a new rubber seal shall be installed,
          and a pressure test shall be performed at full working pressure. When a pressure test at full working
           pressure cannot be performed without creating unnecessary risk, the pressure test shall be carried
           out at the maximum pressure that can be generated without creating such risk. The JRA that is
           generated for this procedure shall identify the risks of mismatched hammer unions and reference
             this standard, all other risks and preventive measures involved with pressure testing.

       4.6 For third-party equipment installed either permanently or temporarily on ADES rigs, the third-party
             shall identify all hammer unions utilized in their systems on a schematic, one-line piping diagram.
         Each third-party valve, hose, chicksan, or other connection point that is fitted with a hammer union
             that is designated or could be utilized as a connection point with an ADES rig piping system shall
          be labeled with a durable tag or marker that identifies the size and Fig. No. of the hammer union.
          Three copies of each diagram shall be provided to the OIM upon completion of installation of the
          equipment or system.

       4.7 All hammer unions installed in kill and choke piping and manifolds as well as in well test piping
          systems shall be certified for sour gas (H2S) (Hydrogen Sulfide) service in accordance with NACE
           (National Association of Corrosion Engineers) specification MR0175 requirements. The selection
            chart specifies the unions that are made to meet this requirement. The union parts made for sour
           gas service are marked with either "MR0175", "Sour Gas", or "SG". Note: All standard, non-ELS
        Wing Nuts for all Fig. 1502 unions and below WILL properly mate with the corresponding Fig.
          No. union components that ARE certified for H2S service. HOWEVER, mixing up hammer union
            parts that are not all rated for sour gas (H2S) service will render the union unfit for sour gas service.
         The potential for mismatching in this regard shall be included in all JRAs developed for this
           procedure.

       4.8 In certain operations such as when batch drilling is performed on platforms, and cement jobs are
          performed "off line" where the same hammer unions are assembled and disassembled on a more or
             less daily basis, a Work Permit may be issued that will cover a maximum period of 12 hours, and
          renewed each tour for the period of time that these operations are undertaken to ensure that all
         hammer unions are identified and labeled so that they will not be mismatched.

    5.0   PROCESS AND DEFINITIONS

       5.1 Hammer Unions: These are commonly referred to as Weco unions because they were originally
          manufactured by the Weco Company. Several companies now manufacture the same unions. Each
           type of union has a "Figure Number" (Fig. No.) That is used by all companies who manufacture
           these unions. A hammer union selection chart is provided in this standard. The hammer unions are
           color-coded for the purpose of visual recognition of the type of union it is. The wing nut, and each
          union half is also die stamped with the Fig. No. The most common unions installed in our rig
          systems are shown below:


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 242 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 648

                                    Policy HAMMER UNIONS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 5 of 5





         The pressure seal of a hammer union is established at the contact interface between the two union
            halves. These are compressed together by screwing the wing nut onto the female half of the union
          and tightening it with a sledge hammer that is used to strike the lugs extending from the O.D. of
            the wing nut. Most hammer unions have an elastomeric seal between the two union halves that acts
           as the primary seal. A secondary seal is provided by the metal-to-metal contact between the two
           unions halves.

    6.0   RECORDS
         None

    7.0   REFERENCES
         None

                     Policy GROUNDING REQUIREMENTS FOR WELDING
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 1


  GROUNDING REQUIREMENTS FOR WELDING

    1.0   PURPOSE
           This procedure describes grounding requirements for welding of ADVANCED ENERGY
       SYSTEM to provide a standard for ensuring that hull or rig structures are not used as a current
            return path when welding is performed on a rig.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
          3.1The OIM/PIC is responsible for ensuring that this standard is referenced in all hot work
           permits involving electric arc welding or gouging.

          3.2The rig welder and his supervisor are responsible for the properly grounding each welding job.

    4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

           All items being welded on the rig shall have a ground cable attached as close as possible to the
           area being welded, with the other end of the cable attached securely to the ungrounded common
           lug directly on the welding machine. Grounding directly back to the welding machine avoids the
            potential for electric currents to flow through steel structures and equipment and causing
           corrosion problems or bearing and electrical equipment failures

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

  PROVISION OF SERVICES TO PLATFORMS

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To establish policies for the provision of auxiliary services such as compressed air, electric power
          and control instrumentation, firefighting and detection systems, etc., to offshore fixed platforms.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

   3.0RESPONSIBILITY

       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible for:

                3.1.1 Notifying the Rig Manager whenever a request is received by the Operator's
                     Representative on the rig to provide services to a platform, and to ensure that no
                      services are provided until approval is received from the Rig Manager.

                3.1.2 Ensuring that all systems associated with supporting the platform are maintained in
                      serviceable condition at all times, and that all required preventive maintenance
                        activities required to maintain such systems are added to the rig's preventive
                   maintenance system

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible to the appropriate Operations chain of command to Vice
           President for ensuring that:

                3.2.1 A Rig Modification Request is submitted to Operations - upon approval by the
                    Operations chain of command and Vice President. Such request must include a detailed
                      description of the scope of work intended to supply supporting services and systems to
                      the offshore platform, including all rig modifications necessary.

                3.2.2 A letter of agreement attached hereto is prepared and submitted to the Operator upon
                       receipt of approval by the appropriate Operations chain of command to Regional Vice
                      President.

                3.2.3 Copies of the duly executed letter of agreement are distributed to the appropriate
                       individuals.

       3.3 The Vice President is responsible for making the decision to accept or reject requests received by
            the Operator to supply requested services to the platform based on requirements specified by
           Operations and obtaining approval for the expenditures required to procure, install, and maintain
          such services.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 245 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                       Policy PROVISION OF SERVICES TO PLATFORMS

       3.4 The Vice President, Operations shall ensure all engineering is completed satisfactorily, and all
           drawings, specifications, and requested project supervision that may be required are provided to
            the Rig Manager for all rig modifications and other installations necessary to provide the
            specified services to the platform. Such services shall be provided without sacrifice to the
           capacity of the remaining rig systems to support all rig demands, including those required to meet
             rig class rules and flag state statutory requirements.

    4.0   PROCEDURES (General)

       4.1 Auxiliary support services will be supplied to platforms only to the extent that surplus capacity is
            available alter all rig demands, including rig class rules and flag state statutory requirements are
           met.

       4.2 Compressed air from rig sources shall not be connected to well testing equipment, burner booms,
           or any other equipment or systems that may interface with or contain wellbore fluids under any
           circumstances. If such connections are required, compressed air shall be provided by equipment
           supplied by the Operator subject to review and approval by Maintenance & Operations as part of
            the Rig Modifications Request prior to installation and commissioning.

       4.3 The Operator shall assume full liability for and indemnify and hold ADES (Contractor) harmless
            against any and all claims resulting from our furnishing such services.

       4.4 A Request for Rig Modifications must be approved, and a letter of agreement, substantially in the
          form attached to this standard, must be duly executed by the Operator prior to providing such
            services.

                     Policy USE OF SALT WATER FOR RIG WASH DOWN
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

  USE OF SALT WATER FOR RIG WASH DOWN

    1.0   PURPOSE
           This procedure describes the policy regarding the use of salt water for rig wash down for
       ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEM to establish a policy regarding the use of salt water for washing-
         down ADES owned/operated equipment.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for:

                3.1.1 Ensuring the Operator's Representative is aware of this policy, and that salt water is not
                   used to wash the rig down.
                3.1.2 Notifying the Rig Manager of any requests by the Operator's Representative to use salt
                    water to wash the rig.

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible for ensuring the Operator is aware of this policy.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 It is the policy of ADES never to use salt water for the purpose of washing down equipment,
           machinery, or hulls on ADES rigs/barges.

       4.2 An allowable exception to this policy is during the removal of heavy solids from the mud tanks,
           preload tanks, BOP's, mud flow lines and ditches, troughs etc. Salt water may be used during this
           procedure but must be followed with a fresh water rinse.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                       Policy AIR HOIST AIR SUPPLY REQUIREMENTS
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 1


   AIR HOIST AIR SUPPLY REQUIREMENTS

    1.0   PURPOSE
           This procedure describes air hoist air supply requirements of ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEM
            to provide a standard for limiting the air pressure supplied to all air hoists to prevent overloading
          and premature failures.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible for ensuring that all supervisory maintenance personnel are aware of
             this standard.

       3.2 The senior maintenance supervisor shall ensure that the air supply lines to all air hoists are fitted
           with a pressure regulator, adjusted, set, and locked in accordance with this standard.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       4.1 The air supply lines to all air hoists shall be fitted with a pressure regulator of a size to deliver
             sufficient volume for the hoist to operate within manufacturer's recommended parameters, and
           adjusted to either the manufacturer's recommended pressure limit, or 90 psi, whichever is less.

       4.2 Such air pressure regulators shall also have either a lock or a tamper-proof feature to discourage
           or prevent the adjustment of the regulator to discharge pressures above the requirements of this
            standard.

       4.3 Exceptions to this standard must be granted in writing by the Maintenance Director.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None

                       Policy DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 1 of 10


  DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION

    1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide a standard for the inspection of used drill pipe, heavy weight drill pipe (HWDP) and
            rotary shouldered connections used on drill collars and other bottom hole assembly (BHA)
          components.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 Exceptions to this standard must be obtained in writing from the Vice President, Operations.

       3.2 All personnel responsible for creating, reviewing, and approving field requisitions, and all
           personnel responsible for procuring materials and services for the company are responsible for
           ensuring this standard is referenced in all field requisitions and purchase orders issued for the
           inspection of both new and used drill pipe.

       3.3 The Vice President, Operations and Materials Manager is responsible for ensuring that: All used
           pipes from any rig via material transfer are inspected in accordance with this standard, and all
           pipe found not to be in API "Premium" condition shall be sold for scrap and or returned to
           custom.

       3.6 The Operation Manager is responsible to the VP, Operations for maintaining this standard to
             reflect the latest technology and industry "best practices".

    4.0   PROCEDURES

       General: Include all items in purchase order as appropriate.

       4.1 Drill pipe classification, dimensional criteria, and other specification guidelines shall be governed
         by the latest editions of DS-1 unless specified otherwise in this standard. This specification shall
           apply to drill pipe, and the connections utilized on HWDP. Inspection and rejection criteria for
              drill collars are covered by ADES Standard for D.C.

       4.2 Manufacturer's identification markings as required by DS-1 shall be recorded on the inspection
          company's Log Sheet along with the ADES Joint Serial Number.

       Previous 2 points should refer to T H HILL DS-1 inspection standard, API tubular inspection is no
           longer used nor accepted by OPCO’s


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 249 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 660

                       Policy DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 2 of 10


       4.3 When drill pipe pin tool joints are recommended for re-cutting, the joint serial number shall be
            paint stenciled along the side of the tube section nearest the pin end. The joint serial number is
           configured as follows:

          Mfg./PO Code:                 (3 Letter Sequence)             AAA
             Serial Number:                    (Five digits)                     12345
              Grade:                             (Letter)                        E, G, S, etc.

           In addition, a different serial number configuration scheme is also in effect that is configured as
            follows:
              Grade:                           (1-Letter)                       E, G, S, etc.
          Rig Cost Center:                   (3-digit number)                          1, 2, 3
             Serial Number:                    (4-digit number)                        1, 2, 3, 4
       Replacement Sequence:                    (Letter)                      A, B, etc.


       4.3 Recommendations for the repair of drill pipe shall be accompanied with a clear definition of the
           conditions found warranting such repairs, such as galling, wear, pitting, or mechanical damage to
           threads  or  shoulders and  excessive/improper  re-facing,  etc.  If  necessary, show  separate
          documentation as required. The inspection company representative shall obtain approval from the
       ADES representative for all repairs recommended prior to conducting any repairs. (inspection
          companies are not performing repairs to tubular; repairs are performed by a 3rd party machine shop)

       4.4 Inspection methods and sequence shall be devised to seek the shortest route to rejection and/or
             reclassification to Class 2 or lower, along with clear and concise documentation pertaining to the
            specific reasons for rejection/reclassification. ADES, or their representative shall retain the sole
           option of determining the  rejection/reclassification of any  drill pipe found with vague or
           inconclusive indications, along with approving any optional inspection methods that may be
           required to clarify the actual condition of the joint(s) in question.

       4.5 After all inspections have been completed, all drill pipe connections shall be doped with the thread
         compound specified by the ADES representative, and fitted with either Drill- Tec or MSI Magnum
             plastic thread protectors (no substitutions).

       4.6 For long term storage after the drill pipe is placed in their final storage position, all thread protectors
             shall be removed and stored, and all threads and shoulders shall be liberally coated with Esgard
          Thread Kote or equal, subject to the prior approval of ADES.


       4.7 A Master Service Agreement between ADES, and the tubular inspection company shall be drafted,
            signed, and fully valid at the time of purchase order placement.

       4.8 Upon request, the tubular inspection company shall show appropriate training certification and
          documentation recognized by ASNT TC IA for their employees and/or agents performing the
            inspection. The inspection company shall also demonstrate that all tools and equipment are
           examined/certified for acceptable accuracy, and suitable for the service intended before any work
              is carried out.
            In addition, the latest editions/revisions of the following documents shall be available at the
           inspection location:

                 •  API RP7G

                 •  API Specification 7

                 •  API Specification 5D

                 •  The latest revision of ADES Drilling Operations Standard, "Drill Pipe Inspection
                        Specification"

                 •   Inspection Company's Standard Operating Procedures

                 •   Standard DS-1, "Drill Stem Design and Inspection", by T.H. Hill Associates, Inc.,
                           latest edition.
                      Note: Discrepancies between local inspection standards, and those referenced above
                         shall be resolved prior to the inspection. If such discrepancies cannot be resolved at the
                         local level, contact the Operation Manager for Resolution.

       4.9 The inspection company and their agents/assignees shall conduct all handling and transportation of
            tubular goods in a manner that is consistent with good oilfield practice to avoid damage to the drill
            pipe. The inspection company shall be responsible for, and shall repair at their own expense, any
         damage incurred to the drill pipe during the course of work carried out under the purchase order
          from the time of receipt to delivery.

       4.10 The inspection company shall provide two (2) hard copies, and one (1) Excel diskette of the
           complete report of all inspection findings with the invoice. The Inspection Log utilized by the
            inspection company shall contain all of the information listed below:

                  •   Rig Name
                  •   Date
                  •   Inspection Company
                  •   P.O. No.
                  •  Work Order No.
                  •   Joint No. assigned by the inspection company
                  •   Manufacturer's API stamping
                  •  ADES Serial number

       4.11 The inspection company shall, as a minimum, maintain an electronic spreadsheet in Microsoft
           Excel containing, as a minimum, the same information specified above for each inspection
           performed for each rig. These files must be readily accessible and shall be provided to any ADES
             representative upon demand. Those inspection companies which have specially designed software,
            or relational data base software to store this information is subject to prior written approval of the
        ADES representative.

       4.12 Drill pipe inspection procedures

               4.12.1 The procedures offered below are offered as guidelines only and may be altered
                   depending on additional inspection requirements and other factors. These guidelines do
                     not relieve the inspector of his responsibility of carrying out appropriate inspection
                  methods and sequence that will result in the shortest route to rejection.

               4.12.2 Rack the pipe, remove protectors, clean connections and the I.D. and O.D. of the tube
                       sections, stencil joint inspection numbers, and record same, along with both the API
                     Manufacturer's stamp, and the ADES Joint Serial Numbers on the inspection log sheet.
                  Based on the age and visual condition of the drill string, including but not limited to tool
                        joint wear/length, internal plastic coating conditions, etc., devise inspection methods and
                   sequence to determine shortest route to rejection.

               4.12.3 Set aside the bent joints that are to be straightened and record this information on the
                     inspection log sheet and Excel  spreadsheet. These  will be inspected only  after
                       straightening.

               4.12.4 O.D. gauge tube section with Go/No-Go Gauge set for a diameter equal to the amount
                     of O.D. loss required to classify the joint as API Class2 condition (20% wall loss).
                  Example:

                            •   5", 19.50 lbs./ft. = .362" nom. wall thickness
                            •  API Class 2 Condition = 20% wall loss = .290" wall thickness
                            •   Resulting Tube O.D. @ .290" wall thickness due to O.D. wear =4.850"

               4.12.5 Determine the minimum wall thickness with a manual U.T. thickness gage in all areas
                  where the O.D. gauge passes over the O.D. of tube and record the minimum reading on
                      the inspection log sheet. If the wall thickness is found to be API Class 2, set these joints
                       aside, and apply two yellow bands to both ends of the joint per API. No further
                      inspections shall be done to these joints. Condition codes provided must be used to
                     describe the cause of tube O.D. loss; i.e., O.D. wear, necking, etc.

               4.12.6 Measure tool joint O.D. and tong space to nearest 1/32 inch for both the pin and box and
                     record this information on the Inspection Log Sheet. If tool joints have raised hard
                   banding applied, and it is still proud to the surface of the tool joint, then this measurement


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 253 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 660

                       Policy DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 6 of 10

                        is not required. However, the original tool joint O.D. must be recorded on the Inspection
                   Log. Note: If eccentric wear is evident, the minimum O.D. shall be the dimension
                    recorded for the tool joint O.D. The maximum O.D., and the difference between it and
                      the minimum O.D. shall be recorded in the comments section for each joint that applies,
                    along with the appropriate condition code (EW).

               4.12.7 Shoulder width measurements shall be limited to box connections that are eccentrically
                 worn to the extent that the width of the bevel anywhere along the circumference of the
                    shoulder measures less than 1/16". Such measurements must only be taken at the point
                  where maximum eccentric O.D. wear has taken place. Enter the minimum shoulder width
                      in the inspection log sheet, along with the appropriate condition code (EW).

               4.12.8 Measure bevel diameter to the nearest 1/64 inch for both the pin and the box and record
                        this information on the inspection log sheet. Enter the appropriate condition code for
                     those shoulders that have been found to be excessively refaced (ER), based on benchmark
                     observation, or in the absence of benchmarks, the maximum bevel diameter for the
                    connection as follows:

              Connection                           Orig. Bev. Dia        Max. Bev. Dia.
            NC38                                4-37/64"                    4-45/64"
            NC46                                  6-3/32"                      6-7/32"
            NC50                                  6-1/16"                      6-3/16"
            HT55                                 6.873"                      6.937"(1)
            XT57                                 6.721"                      6.846"(2)
                5-1/2 FH                               7-3/32"                     7-7/32"(3)
                6-5/8 FH                             7-45/64"                    7-53/64"

                    Notes:

                         1)  For 7" O.D. connections
                         2)  For 7" O.D. x 4-1/4" I.D. connections
                         3)  For Grade S135 only. See API Spec 7 for the bevel diameters assigned to other
                            grades and weights of 5-1/2" O.D. drill pipe
                         4)  If benchmarks are still visible on any of those joints culled for excessive bevel
                             diameter, notify the ADES representative
                         5)  For connections not  listed above, or for connections having  different, or
                           nonstandard O.D.s and/or I.D.s, contact the ADES representative.

               4.12.9 Evaluate and record the shoulder condition for both the pin and the box for each joint
                  on the inspection log sheet. The condition codes specified shall be used to describe the
                     conditions found in each case, along with the repair codes when repairs such as re-facing
                   (RF) are recommended by the inspector. Additional notations are required to describe
                    unusual conditions not covered by these codes.

                       Policy DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz

               4.12.10 Evaluate and record the thread condition for both the pin and the box for each joint on
                      the Inspection Log Sheet. The condition codes located at the bottom of each log sheet
                  must be used to describe the conditions found in each case, along with the repair codes
                when repairs such as re-cutting (RC) are recommended by the inspector.

               4.12.11 Evaluate the condition of the internal plastic coating, and record condition grade in the
                     Inspection Log Sheet for both overall condition, and the internal upset areas.

               4.12.12 Conduct full-length Electromagnetic Inspection (EMI) or Ultrasonic Inspection (U.T.)
                     inspection of tube section from upset to upset in accordance with the following inspection
                  methods in accordance with availability, and best application for the age of the pipe, wall
                      thickness, and the tube conditions evidenced by visual means:

                            •   Hall Effect EMI Scan (l) 5.12b Full-Length U.T. Scan (2)

                      Notes:
                           1)  Hall Effect EMI scans must cover 100% of the tube body and must incorporate
                                the latest technology for both crack/flaw detection, as well as minimum wall
                               thickness indication.


                           2)  Full-length U.T. scans must cover 100% of the tube surface and incorporate a
                       minimum  of 2  ea.  compression wave  transducers  for  wall  thickness
                            measurement, and sufficient shear wave transducers for both lag and lead
                                 directions for the detection of longitudinal, transverse, and oblique indications.
                                       If only one set (lag and lead) of oblique transducers are available, they must
                           be set at 22 degrees to the pipe axis. If another set of oblique transducers is
                                 available, they must be set at 45 degrees from the pipe axis.
                           3)  The inspection report must include the original tapes of  all scans, with
                               flaw/crack indications, and minimum wall thickness indications marked on the
                               tape by the equipment operator with his initials. The minimum wall reading
                            found during prove-up must written next to the indication on the tape.

                           4)  Prior to conducting the scans, and for every 50 joints thereafter, the inspection
                         company inspector shall calibrate the scanning device with a test sample
                            having known,  previously measured  defects  or  "notches"  in  order  to
                             demonstrate to the ADES representative, the proper threshold setting by which
                                      all indications shall be considered acceptable or not acceptable.

                           5)  The sensitivity of the unit shall be adjusted to the maximum extent possible to
                             allow all indications to be clearly detected, both in terms of what the inspector
                              witnesses in real time, as well as on the tapes afterwards. Any indication close

                                 to the "threshold" setting shall be proven-up manually via hand U.T. device or
                               equivalent NDE techniques acceptable to the ADES representative.

                           6)  For drill pipe having a nominal wall thickness in excess of .400", both full-
                               length U.T. inspections and Hall Effect EMI inspections are mandatory.

               4.12.13 Prove-up all indications on the tube section by the most appropriate inspection methods
                      to define the status of the indication. Flaws or cracks must be proven-up by one or more
                     of the following inspection techniques:

                            ▪  Wet magnetic particle
                            ▪   Florescent black light inspection
                            ▪  Manual Shear and/or Compression Wave U.T. gauge
                            ▪  Wall thickness indications must be proven-up by manual compression
                            wave U.T. thickness gauge readings. Sufficient time must be taken by the
                                    inspector to ensure that the lowest possible wall thickness is verified.
                              Record the lowest wall thickness measurement found on the Inspection
                           Log Sheet for each joint.

               4.12.14 DS-1 INSPECTION STANDARDS
                 Whenever DS-1 Inspection Standards are specified for drill pipe, the following criteria
                       shall be used to determine rejection or repairs in addition to that which is specified in
                API RP7G, and part 5.0 of this specification:
                   Tool Joint Visual Inspection

                 •   Shoulder/Bevel Width: As applied to drill pipe tool joints, seal width measurements
                         shall not be taken when there is a visible bevel around the entire circumference of the
                       shoulder. In addition, ADES specifications for both G and S grade, 5" O.D., 19.50
                              lbs./ft.  drill pipe fitted with NC50 connections establish the minimum O.D. for
                  Premium Class at 6-3/32" based on a pin I.D. of 3-1/4". For NC50 connections with a
                       2-3/4" pin I.D., the min. O.D. of the box for API Premium Class is 6-5/16".

                 •  Thread Surface Pitting: The criteria specified in DS-I, "Visual Connection", shall only
                      apply to the first five (5) thread roots from the shoulder of the pin, and the first five (5)
                       thread roots from the bottom of the box.

                 •  Box Swell: This shall be defined as an enlargement of the counterbore diameter, called
                   "Box Belling" due to excessive torsional loading. It does not cover counterbores that
                    have been machined incorrectly, nor does  it cover localized shoulder seal area
                       deformation/rolling due to over-torquing. Box swell shall be determined only by
                     measuring the Box counterbore diameter. This measurement shall not be taken unless
                   more than 2 pins inspected are found stretched per API RP7G (exceeds .006" over 2


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 256 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 660

                       Policy DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                    Page 9 of 10


                       inches) and shall only be done on boxes which have outside diameters of 6-3/8" or less
                          (for NC50 connections). The maximum counterbore diameter for NC50 connections is
                        5-3/8". Shoulder Rolling (SR) has been confused with box swelling by certain
                       inspection companies. This occurs when there is localized deformation of the shoulder
                         seal surface due to over-torquing, causing some of the metal of the shoulder to be rolled
                         into the edge of the counterbore. In most cases, this can be felt by hand, or observed
                  when counterbore
                 •  measurements are taken. Usually, there will be no evidence of any galling of the
                      shoulder sealing surface associated with this problem, but shoulder flatness may be
                    compromised. If this condition is found, the shoulder should be refaced with a lathe-
                      type re-facing tool, and the edge between the counterbore and shoulder redressed to
                   remove any rolled metal.

                 •   Rotary Shouldered Connection Stress Relief Features:
                 •   Stress relief features to be machined into all connections used on drill stem subs and
               HWDP. These must be cut to API Spec 7 requirements. The alternative configuration
                       offered in API Spec 7 is not acceptable. If ADES can prove that such features have
                    been employed in their specifications for purchase of new HWDP, as well as for re-
                        cutting connections on HWDP, then DS-1 requirements to measure these features may
                    be waived, based on spot-checking 3 connections to verify proper dimensions of these
                         features.
                 •   Pitting on Stress Relief Feature Surfaces: The rejection criteria specified in DS-1,

                 •   Shoulder flatness should only be checked on shoulders that have not already been
                        rejected for re-facing for other reasons, and on tool joints that have not been.
                     For box and pin stress relief features on HWDP, and drill collar pin connections, pitting
                          that is measured or visually estimated to be deeper than 1/16-inch shall be cause for
                          rejection.
                      "Visual Connection, Part 3.11.4.j, shall apply only to the box and pin connections on
                             drill stem subs, and drill collar box connections in the transition areas between the
                        cylinder and tapered bores in the box bore back.

                 •  The diameter of the center wear pad on HWDP need not be measured. This feature has
                   no bearing on the strength or reliability of the HWDP while in service. Instead, visual
                      confirmation should be made that the hard banding on the center wear pad is still raised
                    from the surface. If it is not, then it should be recommended that raised field hard
                     banding be re-applied. In addition, if any HWDP connections are found with "heat
                       checking", the center wear pad should be examined for this also.
                 •  Thread Protectors: The use of Drill-Tec or MSI Magnum plastic thread protectors for
                             all connections on drill pipe. No substitutions are allowed.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 257 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual
Advanced Energy Systems (ADES)                                           Policy No. OPS 660

                       Policy DRILL PIPE INSPECTION SPECIFICATION
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                   Page 10 of 10

                 •  Thread Compound: Thread compounds are specified in the selection of which
                  compound to apply is the decision of the ADES representative

       •  DS-1 Tool Joint Dimensional 1, 2, and 3 Inspections.
                 •   Counterbore Depth: This measurement shall not be taken unless benchmarks are not
                        present, and unless this measurement is the only way to accurately determine the
                   amount of material that has been removed from the shoulder of a box connection as a
                          result of re-facing.
                 •   Bevel Diameter: In all cases,  it is mandatory that this measurement be taken and
                      recorded to the nearest l/64th of an inch. The maximum bevel diameters are listed in
                           this standard.
                 •   Pin Stress Relief Groove Width: This does not apply to drill pipe, but it does apply to
             HWDP and drill stem subs. Maximum allowable groove width shall be 1-1/8", +/-
                        1/32". Minimum allowable groove width is 1" +/- 1/32".
              •  Box O.D. Measurement: All box connection O.D. measurements shall be taken 3/4" from
                    the shoulder. Those boxes found swelled and rejected based on excessive counterbore (Qc)
                 measurements, shall have the O.D. re-measured, and recorded on the Inspection Log Sheet
                      after the connection has been re-cut. For those boxes found to be worn eccentrically, the
              minimum O.D. measurement shall be the one recorded on the Inspection Log Sheet. In
                    addition, the appropriate condition code (EW) should be noted in the comment section for
                     that particular joint, along with the measurement of the difference between the maximum
                 and minimum O.D.s.
              •  Tool Joint Tong Space: The minimum tong space for rotary shouldered connections is
                  based upon the length of the tong dies used for make-up and break-out, plus 1/2" not
                   including raised hard banded areas. If there is no raised hard banding, the minimum tong
                  space for rotary shouldered connections is the length of the tong dies, plus 1/2". For HT
                 and XT box connections, the minimum tong space is the length of the tong dies, plus 2-
                     1/2", not including raised hard banded areas. If there is no raised hard banding, the
              minimum tong space is the length of the tong dies, plus 2-1/2".
              •   Pin I.D.: Pin I.D. must be measured only on Drill Collars to obtain accurate Bending
                   Strength Ratio (BSR) values. This dimension is not needed for heavy weight drill pipe, and
                 normal weight drill pipe, as long as ADES can show what the I.D.s are for each joint, based
                on past inspection records, and/or manufacturing records that are traceable by joint serial
                number
              •  Tool Joint Hard Banded Areas: Measurement of the height of the hard-banding above the
                    tool joint surface is not required. If the hard-banding is found to be flush with the tool joint
                    surface, the joint should be recommended for the re-application of hard banding in
                  accordance with Drilling Operations Standard.

                                   Policy LIVE OIL STORAGE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 1 of 2


   LIVE OIL STORAGE


    1.0   PURPOSE
        To provide policies and procedures when storage of live oil in any hull tanks is requested by the
           Operator.

    2.0   SCOPE
           This procedure shall apply to all ADVANCED ENERGY SYSTEMS (ADES) offshore
            operations.

    3.0   RESPONSIBILITY
       3.1 The OIM/PIC is responsible to the Rig Manager for:

                3.1.1 Communicating this policy to the Operator Representative at the rig whenever a request
                          is submitted to store live oil in the rig tanks or compartments.

                3.1.2 Advising the Rig Manager whenever such a request  is submitted by the Client
                     Representative at the rig.

                3.1.3 Ensuring that no live oil is stored in the rigs tanks or compartments until written approval
                          is received from the Rig Manager.

       3.2 The Rig Manager is responsible through the Operations chain of command through to the Vice
           President to:

                3.2.1 Submit notification of any requests received from the Operator for storing live oil in any
                        rig tanks or compartments.

                3.2.2 Upon approval by the Vice President, prepare and submit a Request for Modifications to
                   Maintenance/ Structure/ Engineering Dept. for project cost –and conduct  all other
                   communications with the Operator until an agreement is reached.

                3.2.4 Submit written directions to the OIM/PIC regarding the decision to or not to store live oil
                      in any rig tanks or compartments.

    4.0   PROCEDURES

           4.1     All approvals from Maintenance/ Structure Dept. shall be received, as well as approvals
                    for expenditure (AFE's), and a contract letter of agreement established between ADES and
                    the Operator before any live oil storage is permitted in any rig tank or compartment.


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 259 از 259 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                           Offshore Drilling Operations Manual

                                   Policy LIVE OIL STORAGE
Revision: 01-00                                                  Author: Ahmed Mahfouz
Issued: 28. Jan.2020
Approval: Mohamed Khalil                                                     Page 2 of 2



       4.2 This policy does not include special portable tanks supplied by the Operator or a well test service
         company tank that is specifically designed, fabricated, and certified for the storage of live oil.

    5.0   RECORDS
         None

    6.0   REFERENCES
         None
